- **LimitRange:** a namespace object, enforced by the LimitRanger admission plugin, that checks pods against minimums and maximums and applies default requests to pods that specify none ([Multi-Tenancy, Policy & Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-multi-tenancy-and-policy#rbac-quotas-and-limits)).
- **Linkerd:** the service mesh created by Buoyant, the first to graduate in the CNCF, for which Buoyant stopped publishing open source release artifacts from 2.15 ([Cluster Networking](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cluster-networking#service-meshes)).
- **List, then watch:** the Kubernetes client pattern of listing a kind and then watching from the returned `resourceVersion`, to get the current state and every later change ([Kubernetes Architecture](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-architecture#the-control-loop)).
- **Managed Kubernetes:** a provider-run control plane (EKS, GKE, AKS, DOKS, LKE, OKE) with customer or provider nodes, a conformant API with forced upgrades and extended-support fees ([Distributions & Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-distributions-and-alternatives#the-alternatives)).
- **Multi-team and multi-customer tenancy:** the two Kubernetes tenancy cases: multi-team, where tenants are teams using the API with RBAC, quotas and network policies, and multi-customer, where customers never see the cluster ([Multi-Tenancy, Policy & Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-multi-tenancy-and-policy#the-official-tenancy-models)).
- **MutatingAdmissionPolicy:** the in-process counterpart to mutating webhooks, CEL expressions in the API server that produce an apply configuration or a JSON patch ([Multi-Tenancy, Policy & Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-multi-tenancy-and-policy#admission-control)).
- **Namespace per tenant:** the soft Kubernetes tenancy model of namespaces with Roles, quotas and network policies, cheap but leaving cluster-scoped objects, nodes, the API server and DNS shared ([Multi-Tenancy, Policy & Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-multi-tenancy-and-policy#the-official-tenancy-models)).
- **Node controller:** the controller that assigns pod CIDRs, reconciles nodes with the cloud's machines and monitors node health, deciding what happens when a provider's machine fails ([Kubernetes Architecture](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-architecture#the-controller-managers)).
- **Node pool:** a group of identical nodes in a managed cluster that owns its upgrade parameters (surge, unavailability, drain timeout, soak), set per pool by every provider ([Managed Kubernetes](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-kubernetes#node-pools-and-node-upgrades)).
- **Node-less mode:** a managed Kubernetes mode where the provider runs the nodes as well as the control plane (Autopilot, Auto Mode, AKS Automatic, Fargate, virtual nodes), with less hardware control ([Managed Kubernetes](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-kubernetes#the-alternatives)).
- **OKE virtual nodes:** Oracle's serverless node pools on enhanced clusters, charged per virtual node-hour with pods paying for the compute they consume ([Managed Kubernetes](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-kubernetes#node-less-modes-hybrid-nodes-and-arc)).
- **Omni:** Sidero Labs' fleet manager for Talos clusters, source-available under the Business Source License, free for non-production use ([Distributions & Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-distributions-and-alternatives#lightweight-and-immutable)).
- **OPA Gatekeeper:** the Kubernetes policy engine based on Open Policy Agent, where a ConstraintTemplate holds Rego logic and a schema and a Constraint instantiates it; the engine behind GKE's and AKS's policy products ([Multi-Tenancy, Policy & Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-multi-tenancy-and-policy#policy-engines-kyverno-and-gatekeeper)).
- **OpenCost:** a CNCF vendor-neutral Kubernetes cost allocation specification and agent that bills workloads at the larger of request and usage and keeps idle and overhead explicit ([Autoscaling & Cost](/books/hosting-and-cloud/autoscaling-and-cost#the-alternatives)).
- **OpenShift:** Red Hat's Kubernetes distribution, whose operators upgrade every component including Kubernetes and the RHEL CoreOS host ([Distributions & Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-distributions-and-alternatives#enterprise-distributions)).
- **Operator pattern:** combining custom resources and custom controllers to encode domain knowledge for an application into an extension of the Kubernetes API ([Kubernetes Architecture](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-architecture#extending-the-api)).
- **Optimistic concurrency (Kubernetes):** updates carry the `resourceVersion` they were based on, and the API server returns `409 Conflict` if the object changed, so controllers reread and retry instead of locking ([Kubernetes Architecture](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-architecture#the-control-loop)).
- **Overlay CNI:** pod networking on a private range with SNAT on egress (Azure CNI Overlay, flannel, Calico IP-in-IP or VXLAN), conserving VPC space but hiding pods behind node IPs ([Cluster Networking](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cluster-networking#the-alternatives)).
- **Owner references:** links that tell the control plane which objects depend on others, so deleting an owner cascades to its dependents ([Kubernetes Architecture](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-architecture#the-control-loop)).
- **Packaging and delivery:** the two problems of shipping to Kubernetes: describing, parameterizing and versioning a set of objects (Helm, Kustomize), and getting them from a repository into a running cluster with drift noticed (Argo CD, Flux) ([Packaging & GitOps](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packaging-and-gitops)).
- **PersistentVolume (PV):** a cluster-scoped piece of storage, created statically or dynamically by a driver, bound one-to-one to a claim ([Storage & Stateful Workloads](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-storage#claims-volumes-and-classes)).
- **PersistentVolumeClaim (PVC):** a workload's request for storage in its namespace (size, access modes, volume mode and class), a claim check on a volume ([Storage & Stateful Workloads](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-storage#claims-volumes-and-classes)).
- **Pod Security Admission:** the built-in admission controller that enforces the Pod Security Standards levels (`privileged`, `baseline`, `restricted`) per namespace through labels ([Multi-Tenancy, Policy & Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-multi-tenancy-and-policy#from-podsecuritypolicy-to-pod-security-admission)).
- **Pod-billed modes:** cluster billing where the provider runs nodes and the customer pays for pod requests or pod-sized compute (Autopilot, Fargate, OKE virtual nodes), moving the packing gap to the provider ([Autoscaling & Cost](/books/hosting-and-cloud/autoscaling-and-cost#the-alternatives)).
- **PriorityClass:** an object mapping a name to an integer priority, so the scheduler can preempt lower-priority pods when the cluster is full, with higher values reserved for system-critical classes ([Kubernetes Architecture](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-architecture#the-scheduler)).
- **Provider VPC CNI:** pod networking where pods get VPC addresses (Amazon VPC CNI, GKE alias IPs, Azure Pod Subnet, OKE VCN-native), routable and attributable but subject to address exhaustion ([Cluster Networking](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cluster-networking#the-alternatives)).
- **Provisioned control plane:** EKS's tiers of pinned control plane capacity sold on top of the cluster fee for very large or bursty clusters ([Managed Kubernetes](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-kubernetes#the-alternatives)).
- **Rancher:** SUSE's management plane for many Kubernetes clusters rather than a single distribution ([Distributions & Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-distributions-and-alternatives#enterprise-distributions)).
- **Reclaim policy:** a StorageClass's setting (defaulting to `Delete`) for whether a volume's data survives when its claim is released, in effect a data-loss setting ([Storage & Stateful Workloads](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-storage#claims-volumes-and-classes)).
- **Release (Helm):** one installation of a chart in a namespace under a name, with a numbered history of revisions, upgraded by three-way merges and acting with the caller's permissions ([Packaging & GitOps](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packaging-and-gitops#helm-charts-values-releases-and-hooks)).
- **Request gap:** the waste between what pods request and what they use, plus space left over on nodes and node overhead, which node autoscalers buy capacity for ([Autoscaling & Cost](/books/hosting-and-cloud/autoscaling-and-cost#bin-packing-and-the-request-gap)).
- **Requests (Kubernetes):** the CPU and memory a pod reserves, which the scheduler and every autoscaler decide on instead of usage, so a cluster can be fully booked and mostly idle ([Autoscaling & Cost](/books/hosting-and-cloud/autoscaling-and-cost)).
- **ResourceQuota:** a namespace object that caps what the namespace can consume and create, rejecting requests that would exceed it and requiring pods to set requests or limits for capped resources ([Multi-Tenancy, Policy & Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-multi-tenancy-and-policy#rbac-quotas-and-limits)).
- **RHEL CoreOS:** OpenShift's fully immutable, container-optimized host operating system, upgraded by OpenShift's operators ([Distributions & Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-distributions-and-alternatives#lightweight-and-immutable)).
- **Rook:** the open-source cloud-native storage orchestrator for Kubernetes, in practice the Ceph operator that deploys and runs Ceph for block, file and object storage on the cluster's nodes ([Storage & Stateful Workloads](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-storage#operators-for-databases-and-storage)).
- **RuntimeClass:** the cluster-scoped Kubernetes object that selects a CRI runtime configuration such as a sandbox, adding its overhead to every pod and steering pods to nodes that support it ([Multi-Tenancy, Policy & Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-multi-tenancy-and-policy#sandboxed-runtimes-and-runtimeclass)).
- **Scheduling framework:** the plugin architecture of the Kubernetes scheduler, with ordered extension points from `PreEnqueue` through `Filter`, `Score` and `Reserve` to `Bind` and `PostBind` ([Kubernetes Architecture](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-architecture#the-scheduler)).
- **Service (Kubernetes):** a stable virtual IP for a selector of pods, typed `ClusterIP`, `NodePort` or `LoadBalancer`, where `type: LoadBalancer` buys a billed provider load balancer ([Cluster Networking](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cluster-networking#services-and-kube-proxy)).
- **ServiceExport and ServiceImport:** the Multi-Cluster Services API objects that declare a Service exported to other clusters in a clusterset and describe a service imported from it ([Cluster Networking](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cluster-networking#multi-cluster)).
- **sigstore policy-controller:** an admission controller that validates images in opted-in namespaces against `ClusterImagePolicy` signature and attestation rules ([Multi-Tenancy, Policy & Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-multi-tenancy-and-policy#admitting-only-signed-images)).
- **Skew policy:** the Kubernetes version skew policy, saying which components may differ in version and by how much, and so the order in which a cluster must upgrade ([Kubernetes Architecture](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-architecture#version-skew-and-upgrade-order)).
- **Stacked etcd:** the HA topology with an etcd member on each control plane node, fewest machines but with failed coupling, so a lost node is an API server and a member together ([Kubernetes Architecture](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-architecture#etcd)).
- **StatefulSet:** the workload kind giving pods a stable identity (ordinal names and DNS under a headless Service), stable storage through per-pod claims, and ordered rollout, without database logic ([Storage & Stateful Workloads](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-storage#statefulsets)).
- **StorageClass:** how Kubernetes makes volumes: a provisioner (the CSI driver), its parameters, a reclaim policy, expansion and binding mode ([Storage & Stateful Workloads](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-storage#claims-volumes-and-classes)).
- **Sync phases and waves (Argo CD):** the ordering of an Argo CD sync through hooks in `PreSync`, `Sync`, `PostSync`, `SyncFail` and `PostDelete` phases and numbered waves ([Packaging & GitOps](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packaging-and-gitops#argo-cd)).
- **Sync status (Argo CD):** whether an Application's live objects match its rendered source ([Packaging & GitOps](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packaging-and-gitops#argo-cd)).
- **Talos Linux:** Sidero Labs' immutable, API-driven OS for Kubernetes with no SSH, shell or package manager, managed over mutual-TLS gRPC and upgraded with Kubernetes as one atomic image ([Distributions & Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-distributions-and-alternatives#lightweight-and-immutable)).
- **Tanzu (after Broadcom):** VMware's Kubernetes line restructured by Broadcom, with standalone TKG ended and Kubernetes moved into vSphere Supervisor and VKS inside VMware Cloud Foundation ([Distributions & Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-distributions-and-alternatives#enterprise-distributions)).
- **ValidatingAdmissionPolicy:** a declarative alternative to validating webhooks, CEL expressions evaluated inside the API server with no webhook process or network hop ([Multi-Tenancy, Policy & Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-multi-tenancy-and-policy#admission-control)).
- **Values (Helm):** a chart's interface: defaults in `values.yaml`, overridden field by field by user files and `--set` flags ([Packaging & GitOps](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packaging-and-gitops#helm-charts-values-releases-and-hooks)).
- **vCluster:** a virtual control plane per tenant on a host cluster; with shared nodes it is not a security boundary for untrusted tenants, while private nodes give each tenant its own CNI, CSI and compute ([Multi-Tenancy, Policy & Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-multi-tenancy-and-policy#virtual-clusters-vcluster-and-capsule)).
- **Velero:** an Apache 2.0 cluster backup tool that saves API objects to object storage and volumes by snapshot or file copy, for backup, restore and migration ([Storage & Stateful Workloads](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-storage#the-alternatives)).
- **Vertical Pod Autoscaler (VPA):** a recommender, updater and admission controller outside core Kubernetes that computes and applies pod requests, closing the request gap ([Autoscaling & Cost](/books/hosting-and-cloud/autoscaling-and-cost#vertical-scaling-and-in-place-resize)).
- **Virtual control plane per tenant:** a tenancy model giving each tenant its own API server, controller manager and etcd synced to shared nodes, isolating the control plane but not the data plane ([Multi-Tenancy, Policy & Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-multi-tenancy-and-policy#the-official-tenancy-models)).
- **`WaitForFirstConsumer`:** the late volume binding mode every provider's default class uses, so a zone-bound disk is created only after the scheduler picks the pod's node ([Storage & Stateful Workloads](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-storage#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Watch cache:** the API server's in-memory mirror of etcd data that serves lists and watches, keeping thousands of watchers off etcd ([Kubernetes Architecture](/books/hosting-and-cloud/kubernetes-architecture#the-control-loop)).

## Managed Services

- **Ably:** a realtime pub/sub service whose building block is the channel, multiplexed over one WebSocket per client, for pushing messages to connected browsers and devices ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#realtime-pub-sub)).
- **AGPLv3:** the network copyleft license whose protection is that modifications must be shared with a service's users; Grafana moved to it, and Elastic and Redis later returned to it ([Open Source & the Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/open-source-and-the-clouds#choosing-a-license)).
- **Amazon MQ:** AWS's managed Apache ActiveMQ (and later RabbitMQ) broker speaking JMS, NMS, AMQP, STOMP, MQTT and WebSocket, single-instance or active/standby across zones, billed in broker-hours ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#managed-brokers-rabbitmq-and-activemq)).
- **Amazon MSK:** AWS's managed Kafka, sold as provisioned brokers billed per broker-hour plus storage, or as Serverless billed per cluster-hour and partition ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#kafka-as-a-service)).
- **Apache Software Foundation:** the foundation that holds Kafka and Pulsar as Apache 2.0 projects owned by the foundation rather than by a company that could relicense them ([Open Source & the Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/open-source-and-the-clouds#foundations-as-the-settlement)).
- **APM:** Datadog's application performance monitoring product, priced per host alongside Infrastructure ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#datadog-the-benchmark-business)).
- **Aurora Capacity Units (ACU):** the unit Aurora Serverless v2 scales in, in steps of 0.5, where one ACU is about 2 GiB of memory with matching CPU and network ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#aurora-the-log-is-the-database)).
- **Aurora DSQL:** AWS's distributed SQL answer to Spanner with PostgreSQL compatibility, an engine that only a provider controlling the hardware and network can sell ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#key-value-and-distributed-sql)).
- **AWS CloudWatch:** AWS's built-in monitoring, billed per metric-month, per GB of logs, per alarm and per dashboard on the AWS invoice ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Azure Event Hubs:** Azure's log service, selling capacity by tier as throughput units, processing units or dedicated capacity units, with retention that lengthens on higher tiers ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#kinesis-and-event-hubs)).
- **Azure Monitor:** Microsoft's built-in monitoring, priced per GB ingested into Log Analytics with commitment tiers and retention charges ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Azure Service Bus:** the enterprise broker as a service: queues, topics whose subscriptions carry filter rules, sessions for FIFO and request/response, scheduled delivery, deferral and duplicate detection ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#pub-sub-and-service-bus)).
- **Backups and point-in-time recovery:** a daily snapshot plus continuously shipped transaction logs, which let a managed database be restored to any second within the retention window ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#how-a-managed-relational-database-works)).
- **Bookies:** the storage nodes of a BookKeeper cluster, which hold each Pulsar topic as a series of append-only ledgers while the brokers stay stateless ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#pulsar)).
- **Bookmark:** the token each D1 query carries under the Sessions API, so a replica serves a version at least as new as the session has seen, giving sequential consistency per session ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#sqlite-at-the-edge)).
- **BSL (Business Source License):** a source-available license that allows non-production use, lets the licensor set an Additional Use Grant, and converts to a named open-source license on its Change Date or after four years ([Open Source & the Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/open-source-and-the-clouds#the-license-spectrum)).
- **BYOC (bring your own cloud):** a deployment in which the provider's cluster runs in the customer's own cloud account, so the provider bills its fee while the customer's cloud bills the infrastructure ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#how-to-build-it)).
- **Capacity units (Event Hubs):** single-tenant capacity sold on the Dedicated tier of Azure Event Hubs ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#kinesis-and-event-hubs)).
- **Cardinality:** the number of distinct series a metric produces across its tag values, measured hourly per metric and tag key so a bill spike can be traced to the tag that caused it ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#how-to-build-it)).
- **Cells:** groups of brokers spread across zones to which Confluent's Kora pins tenants, bounding how far one tenant's load reaches ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#kafka-as-a-service)).
- **CKU (Confluent Kafka Unit):** Kora's capacity abstraction, with bandwidth, request and connection limits that are comparable across clouds ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#kafka-as-a-service)).
- **Cloud-native monitoring:** monitoring built into a cloud (CloudWatch, Azure Monitor, Google Cloud Observability), already collecting that cloud's signals and billed on its invoice ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#the-alternatives)).
- **Cloud-native relational:** a familiar relational engine on the provider's own storage (Aurora, AlloyDB, Azure Hyperscale), with fast failover in exchange for storage no other host offers ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#the-alternatives)).
- **Cloudflare D1:** SQLite as a service with a single primary and asynchronous read replicas, billed by rows read, rows written and storage, with a per-database size ceiling that pushes customers toward many small databases ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Cloudflare Queues:** Cloudflare's queue, delivering at least once to a consumer Worker and billing per 64 KB operation ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#serverless-and-http-queues)).
- **CNCF:** the Cloud Native Computing Foundation, part of the Linux Foundation, which holds Kubernetes, Prometheus, OpenTelemetry, NATS and OpenTofu ([Open Source & the Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/open-source-and-the-clouds#foundations-as-the-settlement)).
- **Collection:** the first stage of an observability pipeline (an agent on each host, an SDK in each process, or a cloud's own emitters), where host counts come from and where data can be sampled or dropped before billing ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#how-the-platforms-work)).
- **Compute-unit hours:** Neon's pricing unit for compute, with plans that cap the compute size and bill each CU-hour used ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#serverless-postgres)).
- **Custom metrics:** Datadog metrics the customer defines, included per host up to an allotment and billed beyond it, with each tag combination counting as a separate metric ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#datadog-the-benchmark-business)).
- **Database branch:** a copy of a serverless database at a point in history with its own endpoint, compute, quota and bill, modeled as a resource with a parent, a creator and an expiry ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#how-to-build-it)).
- **Dead-letter queue:** the queue a message moves to after a configured number of failed attempts; target and attempt count are a pair, and each move is an event the customer can alert on ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#how-to-build-it)).
- **Delayed open source:** licenses (BSL, FSL) that restrict competing services until a change date and then convert to an open-source license ([Open Source & the Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/open-source-and-the-clouds#the-license-spectrum)).
- **Delivery semantics:** a queue's guarantee, stored as a column (at least once, deduplicated with its window, or transactional), so documentation, console and support state exactly what was sold ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#how-to-build-it)).
- **Deno KV:** a key-value store backed by FoundationDB in production, offering strong or eventual consistency per read and billed in read and write units ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Diskless Topics:** KIP-1150, led by Aiven and accepted by the Kafka community, which keeps topic data in object storage with no broker replication ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#kafka-as-a-service)).
- **Distributed Processing Units (DPU):** the unit Aurora DSQL bills reads and writes in, alongside storage ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#aurora-the-log-is-the-database)).
- **Distributed SQL:** the provider's own engine replicated across zones or regions with strong consistency (Spanner, DSQL, Cosmos DB), and with no other host to move to ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#the-alternatives)).
- **Durable Objects:** Cloudflare's single-threaded actors, each with a globally unique name, one location and strongly consistent SQLite storage, for state such as a game session, a rate limiter or a document being edited ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **DynamoDB:** AWS's fully managed key-value database, descended from the Dynamo paper, whose original pricing unit was provisioned throughput per table ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#key-value-and-distributed-sql)).
- **Dynatrace Platform Subscription:** an annual platform-level commitment drawn down at rate-card prices, with overage at the same rates ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#new-relic-dynatrace-and-the-consumption-turn)).
- **Edge key-value:** keys and values cached or replicated globally (Workers KV, Deno KV, Upstash): simple, global and cheap to read, with eventual consistency as the usual cost ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#the-alternatives)).
- **Embedded replicas:** Turso's feature that serves reads from a local replica inside the application's own process while writes go to the cloud primary ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#sqlite-at-the-edge)).
- **`engine_versions`:** the table that holds the engine support calendar (end of standard and extended support, the forced-upgrade date, the target version) with the date each was announced ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#how-to-build-it)).
- **Enhanced fan-out:** a Kinesis option that gives each registered consumer its own per-shard read throughput pushed over HTTP/2, instead of sharing the shard's read limit ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#kinesis-and-event-hubs)).
- **Event bus:** rule-based routing of events (EventBridge, Event Grid), with native events from the cloud's own services and a per-event cost ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#the-alternatives)).
- **Event-based SaaS:** observability on wide events with columnar queries (Honeycomb), with no cardinality penalty and fast ad hoc questions ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#the-alternatives)).
- **EventBridge:** AWS's event bus: events from AWS services, SaaS partners and the customer's own code are matched against rules and routed to targets ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#sns-and-eventbridge)).
- **Fair queues:** an SQS feature that uses `MessageGroupId` on standard queues so one queue can be shared among many tenants without one starving the rest ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#sqs)).
- **FIFO queues:** SQS queues that add exactly-once processing with a `MessageDeduplicationId` and per-group ordering by `MessageGroupId` ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#sqs)).
- **Forced upgrade:** the provider announcing a date and upgrading whatever engine versions are left in a maintenance window, which is what the end of extended support becomes ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#extended-support-and-forced-upgrades)).
- **Full-stack SaaS:** observability platforms (Datadog, New Relic, Dynatrace) that take agents or OTLP and integrate everything, at a bill driven by hosts, bytes and series ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#the-alternatives)).
- **Functional Source License (FSL):** Sentry's fixed-term version of the BSL, permitting anything except a "Competing Use," with each release converting to open source after two years ([Open Source & the Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/open-source-and-the-clouds#the-license-spectrum)).
- **Google Cloud Observability:** Google's built-in monitoring, billed per GiB of logs, per MiB of metrics, per million Prometheus samples and per million spans ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Google Cloud Pub/Sub:** one Google service for queues, fan-out and short-retention logs, where a topic has subscriptions that each act as a queue, billed by bytes rather than requests ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#pub-sub-and-service-bus)).
- **Grafana Cloud:** Grafana Labs' managed open-source observability stack, priced by active series for metrics and by GB processed, written and retained for logs, traces and profiles ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Hard-fail limit:** a free-tier limit that makes queries fail when reached (as with D1, KV and Hyperdrive), trading a surprise bill for an outage the customer may not have planned for ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#free-tiers-pauses-and-sunsets)).
- **Head sampling:** deciding whether to keep a trace at its start, for example by trace-ID ratio; cheap and consistent, but unable to keep every trace with an error ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#sampling-and-pipelines)).
- **High availability (Multi-AZ):** a hot standby in a second zone fed by synchronous replication, with DNS flipped to it on failure ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#how-a-managed-relational-database-works)).
- **Honeycomb:** an event-based observability platform built for high-cardinality queries, priced by events per month with unlimited seats and custom fields ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Hyperdrive:** Cloudflare's service that places a connection pool in regions closest to the origin database and caches non-mutating query results for Workers ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#cloudflare-s-data-stack)).
- **I/O-Optimized:** an Aurora cluster configuration with no per-I/O charge and higher instance and storage rates, for customers who can't forecast I/O ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#aurora-the-log-is-the-database)).
- **Idle cost:** what a database bills when nobody uses it; idle D1 costs nothing, idle Turso only storage and suspended Neon compute nothing, against a provisioned instance billing every hour ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#pricing-per-request-and-row)).
- **Inactivity pause:** a free project suspended after a period without activity (Supabase after a week, Neon compute after minutes), modeled as a resource in a stopped state ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#free-tiers-pauses-and-sunsets)).
- **Infrastructure (Datadog):** Datadog's per-host monitoring product, in Pro and Enterprise tiers that include a pooled allotment of custom metrics ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#datadog-the-benchmark-business)).
- **Ingest:** the observability stage where the platform's endpoint receives telemetry, applies the customer's drop and transformation rules, and meters what remains, which makes the rules a pricing feature ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#how-the-platforms-work)).
- **Ingest versus index:** the split every observability platform converged on, billing data received separately from data indexed for query so customers can keep data they rarely read cheaply ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#sampling-and-pipelines)).
- **Input gates:** the Durable Objects mechanism that delivers no new events to an object while its storage operations run, making a single-threaded object correct without locks ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#cloudflare-s-data-stack)).
- **Instance class:** the vCPU and memory size a customer picks when provisioning a managed database, along with storage type and size, engine version and network placement ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#how-a-managed-relational-database-works)).
- **Kafka-compatible engines:** engines that speak the Kafka protocol on a different implementation (Redpanda, WarpStream, Event Hubs, Ursa), for simpler operations or object-storage economics ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#the-alternatives)).
- **Kinesis Data Streams:** AWS's managed log for real-time streaming data, whose unit of capacity is the shard ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#kinesis-and-event-hubs)).
- **Kora:** Confluent's multi-tenant Kafka engine, with tiered storage, CKUs, tenants pinned to cells and dynamic quotas ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#kafka-as-a-service)).
- **Ledgers:** the append-only segments in which BookKeeper stores each Pulsar topic, with sealed segments copied to object storage by tiered storage ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#pulsar)).
- **libSQL:** an open-contribution, MIT-licensed fork of SQLite that keeps the single-writer model and adds embedded replicas and remote access ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#sqlite-at-the-edge)).
- **Linux Foundation:** the foundation where the forks from the relicensing fights landed (Valkey, OpenTofu, the OpenSearch Software Foundation), so no single company can relicense them ([Open Source & the Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/open-source-and-the-clouds#foundations-as-the-settlement)).
- **LiteFS:** Fly.io's open-source replicated SQLite, running in production but pre-1.0 and offered without support ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#sqlite-at-the-edge)).
- **Log (messaging):** a shape in which any consumer reads messages at its own offset and messages are kept until retention expires, ordered per partition or shard (Kafka, Kinesis, Event Hubs) ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#queues-logs-and-buses)).
- **Log is the database:** Aurora's design principle that only redo log records cross the network, with storage nodes producing pages from them in the background and on demand ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#aurora-the-log-is-the-database)).
- **Maintenance window:** the weekly window a customer picks for minor-version patches and OS updates, with a failover to the standby on Multi-AZ, and forced application when the provider deems a patch mandatory ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#how-a-managed-relational-database-works)).
- **Managed broker:** a protocol broker run for the customer (Amazon MQ, CloudAMQP, Service Bus Premium), speaking AMQP, JMS or MQTT for existing applications ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#the-alternatives)).
- **Managed Kafka:** a provisioned or serverless Kafka log run by a provider (Confluent, MSK, Aiven), offering replay, retention and the Kafka ecosystem ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#the-alternatives)).
- **Managed key-value:** a partitioned key-value store, provisioned or on-demand (DynamoDB), with predictable latency at any scale and no servers to run ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#the-alternatives)).
- **Managed open-source engine:** a provider-run open-source engine on attached storage (RDS, Cloud SQL, Flexible Server, Aiven, DigitalOcean), the portable middle option ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#the-alternatives)).
- **Managed open-source stack:** a vendor running open-source observability engines (Grafana Cloud, Elastic Cloud), so data formats stay open ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#the-alternatives)).
- **Messaging unit:** the hourly capacity unit of Azure Service Bus Premium, bought in fixed sizes with no per-operation charge ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#pub-sub-and-service-bus)).
- **Metal:** PlanetScale's databases on locally attached NVMe drives with unlimited I/O, at the cost of fixed-size drives that go read-only when full until resized ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#serverless-postgres)).
- **Metrics without Limits:** Datadog's split of metric billing into tag combinations ingested and tag combinations indexed for query, so customers can send fine-grained tags without indexing them all ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#datadog-the-benchmark-business)).
- **NATS:** a light, fast, edge-friendly messaging system of subjects plus JetStream for persistence, sold by Synadia or self-hosted ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#the-alternatives)).
- **Neki:** PlanetScale's Postgres sharding system, built by the Vitess team and not a fork of Vitess ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#serverless-postgres)).
- **Neon:** serverless Postgres with storage separated from compute, billed in compute-unit hours and GB-months of storage ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Network copyleft:** licenses (AGPLv3) that allow running the software as a service but require a modified version to offer its source to users interacting with it remotely ([Open Source & the Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/open-source-and-the-clouds#the-license-spectrum)).
- **Nile:** Postgres that virtualizes tenants inside one database, each functioning as its own virtual database without Row Level Security ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#serverless-postgres)).
- **Non-compete source-available:** licenses (RSALv2, Elastic License 2.0, Confluent Community License, BSL with an Additional Use Grant, FSL) that forbid offering the software as a competing service ([Open Source & the Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/open-source-and-the-clouds#the-license-spectrum)).
- **Open core:** an open edition of the engine plus a commercial edition with paid features, which protects the features but not the engine ([Open Source & the Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/open-source-and-the-clouds#choosing-a-license)).
- **OpenSearch:** AWS's fork of Elasticsearch 7.10 after Elastic's 2021 relicensing, now held by the OpenSearch Software Foundation under the Linux Foundation ([Open Source & the Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/open-source-and-the-clouds#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **OpenSSF:** the Open Source Security Foundation, which holds OpenBao, the Vault fork, for the security and continuity of critical projects ([Open Source & the Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/open-source-and-the-clouds#foundations-as-the-settlement)).
- **OSI:** the Open Source Initiative, whose position is that source-available licenses are not open source and "fauxpen" labels mislead ([Open Source & the Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/open-source-and-the-clouds#each-side-s-case)).
- **Output gates:** the Durable Objects mechanism that holds outgoing messages until a write completes, so no client sees a result that could still be lost ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#cloudflare-s-data-stack)).
- **Pageserver:** the Neon component that materializes page versions from base images plus WAL, asynchronously, and serves pages to compute on request ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#serverless-postgres)).
- **Permissive license:** licenses (MIT, BSD-3, Apache 2.0) that allow running the software as a service for others with no obligation to share changes ([Open Source & the Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/open-source-and-the-clouds#the-license-spectrum)).
- **Pipelines (telemetry):** components that drop, aggregate and route telemetry before it is billed, such as the OpenTelemetry Collector, Grafana Alloy and Chronosphere's Telemetry Pipeline ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#sampling-and-pipelines)).
- **PlanetScale:** managed MySQL (on Vitess) and Postgres priced by cluster size per month, with a Metal option on local NVMe ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Processing units (Event Hubs):** the Premium-tier capacity unit of Azure Event Hubs, isolated at the CPU and memory level ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#kinesis-and-event-hubs)).
- **Product retirement:** a provider ending an entire deployment option, as Azure did with Azure Database for MySQL Single Server, so every customer on it must migrate ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#extended-support-and-forced-upgrades)).
- **Protection group:** a 10 GB Aurora storage segment replicated six ways, small enough to repair quickly so a second failure rarely hits the same segment ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#aurora-the-log-is-the-database)).
- **Provisioned throughput:** DynamoDB's original pricing and isolation unit, in which customers specify the request capacity a table needs and the service reserves it ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#key-value-and-distributed-sql)).
- **Provisioned versus used storage:** the meter choice between billing what a customer allocated (RDS) and what it consumes (Aurora), which decides whether deleting a table lowers the bill ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#the-meters)).
- **Pub/sub topic or bus:** a shape that delivers each message to every subscription, each of which then acts as a queue (SNS, EventBridge, Pub/Sub) ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#queues-logs-and-buses)).
- **Pulsar:** a messaging system of stateless brokers over BookKeeper storage that offers queue and log semantics in one, with multi-tenancy built into topic names ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#the-alternatives)).
- **Pusher:** a realtime pub/sub service (Pusher Channels) sold by daily message and concurrent-connection allowances ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#realtime-pub-sub)).
- **Query (observability):** the stage of dashboards, alerts and ad hoc queries, which some platforms bill directly, for example by data scanned ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#how-the-platforms-work)).
- **Queue:** a shape in which each message goes to one of many competing consumers and is deleted once processed (SQS, Service Bus queues, RabbitMQ queues, Cloudflare Queues) ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#queues-logs-and-buses)).
- **Queue in the application's database:** a jobs table read with `SKIP LOCKED`, which adds no new system and enqueues transactionally, at limited throughput ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#the-alternatives)).
- **R2:** Cloudflare's S3-compatible object storage with no egress fee, which Workers read as a data source ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#cloudflare-s-data-stack)).
- **RabbitMQ under Broadcom:** RabbitMQ kept its Mozilla Public License 2.0, and what changed under Broadcom was support, with core-team support limited to commercial customers and contributors and each release given an End of Community Support date ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#managed-brokers-rabbitmq-and-activemq)).
- **RDS Extended Support:** a paid option to keep running a major engine version past the end of standard support for a limited time, after which the provider upgrades the database itself ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#extended-support-and-forced-upgrades)).
- **Read replicas:** asynchronous copies of a managed database for read scaling, each a separately billed instance, with replica lag the customer has to handle ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#how-a-managed-relational-database-works)).
- **Realtime channel:** a shape that delivers messages to connected clients subscribed to a channel, kept only for a short history (Ably, Pusher) ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#queues-logs-and-buses)).
- **Redpanda:** a Kafka-compatible engine under the Business Source License, whose Additional Use Grant forbids offering a competing streaming or queueing service ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#kafka-as-a-service)).
- **Safekeepers:** the Neon nodes that replicate the write-ahead log with Paxos; a transaction commits once a quorum of safekeepers acknowledges its WAL record ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#serverless-postgres)).
- **`series_counts`:** the table written hourly per metric name with the distinct count per tag key, so a bill increase can be explained by the tag that caused it ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#how-to-build-it)).
- **Serverless Postgres:** Postgres with separated storage or managed plans (Neon, Supabase, PlanetScale, Xata) that can scale to zero ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#the-alternatives)).
- **Service-source license:** licenses (SSPL) that allow running the software as a service only if the provider releases the source of its whole service stack ([Open Source & the Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/open-source-and-the-clouds#the-license-spectrum)).
- **Shard:** the unit of Kinesis capacity, with fixed write and read throughput limits per shard ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#kinesis-and-event-hubs)).
- **SNS:** AWS's fan-out service, where a publish to a topic is delivered to every subscription (SQS queues, Lambda functions, HTTP endpoints, email, Firehose) ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#sns-and-eventbridge)).
- **Spanner:** Google's globally distributed, synchronously replicated database, the first to offer externally consistent distributed transactions at global scale through an API that exposes clock uncertainty (TrueTime) ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#key-value-and-distributed-sql)).
- **SQLite as a service:** one file per database with a single writer and replicas (D1, Turso), which makes many small databases nearly free ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#the-alternatives)).
- **SSPL (Server Side Public License):** MongoDB's license whose Section 13 requires anyone offering the program as a service to release the source of the whole service stack, in practice a cloud's control plane; internal use is not a service ([Open Source & the Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/open-source-and-the-clouds#the-license-spectrum)).
- **Standard queues:** SQS queues that deliver at least once, possibly more than once, with best-effort ordering and nearly unlimited throughput ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#sqs)).
- **Storage tiers (observability):** where telemetry is kept by kind: metrics in a time-series database keyed by series, logs in a search index or a cheaper archive, traces and events in their own stores ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#how-the-platforms-work)).
- **Supabase:** Postgres plus REST, auth, realtime and storage around it, priced as a plan plus compute add-ons, disk, monthly active users and egress ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Tail sampling:** deciding whether to keep a trace after seeing most of its spans, so errors and slow traces are kept; stateful and harder to run than head sampling ([Observability Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/observability-platforms#sampling-and-pipelines)).
- **Throughput unit (Event Hubs):** the Basic and Standard tier capacity unit of Azure Event Hubs, with fixed ingress and egress limits per unit ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#kinesis-and-event-hubs)).
- **Trademark policy:** protecting a project's name regardless of its license, which proved decisive for Elastic and NATS but needs registration and enforcement ([Open Source & the Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/open-source-and-the-clouds#choosing-a-license)).
- **Turso:** SQLite (libSQL) as a service with embedded replicas in the application, read-your-writes for the writer and sync for others ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Upstash:** a vendor of Redis-protocol storage priced per command and reachable over HTTP from functions that can't hold a TCP connection ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#upstash-and-deno-kv)).
- **Upstash QStash:** serverless messaging and scheduling that sits between a producer and an API: QStash calls the destination URL, retries on failure, and supports delays and cron schedules ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#serverless-and-http-queues)).
- **Upstash Redis:** a serverless Redis-protocol store reached over HTTP or TCP, per region with read regions, billed by commands, storage and bandwidth ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Upstash Vector:** Upstash's per-request vector index product ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#upstash-and-deno-kv)).
- **Ursa:** StreamNative's Kafka-compatible data streaming engine built on lakehouse storage ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#pulsar)).
- **Valkey:** the Linux Foundation fork of Redis launched after Redis left the BSD license, which AWS and Google ship at lower prices than their Redis caches ([Open Source & the Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/open-source-and-the-clouds#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Vendor's own cloud:** the engine's maker running it on a hyperscaler (MongoDB Atlas), giving the real engine and newest versions across clouds ([Managed Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-databases#the-alternatives)).
- **Visibility timeout:** the SQS lease that hides a received message while the consumer works on it; if the message isn't deleted before it expires, it becomes visible again for redelivery ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#sqs)).
- **WarpStream:** a Kafka-compatible engine, bought by Confluent, that writes directly to S3 from agents in the customer's own cloud account, avoiding stateful brokers and broad vendor access to that account ([Managed Queues & Streaming](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-queues-and-streaming#kafka-as-a-service)).
- **Weak copyleft:** licenses (MPL 2.0) that allow running the software as a service but require a covered file distributed in executable form to be made available as source ([Open Source & the Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/open-source-and-the-clouds#the-license-spectrum)).
- **Workers KV:** Cloudflare's global key-value store cached at the edge, eventually consistent with a stated propagation delay, billed by reads, writes and GB-month ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Xata:** a managed Postgres platform built on copy-on-write branching, with anonymized production clones, branches that hibernate when idle, and a provisioning API for platforms ([Serverless & Edge Databases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/serverless-and-edge-databases#serverless-postgres)).

## Deployment

- **Active CPU:** a platform meter that accrues only while code is executing and pauses during I/O, so a function waiting on a database costs little ([PaaS & Frontend Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/paas-and-frontend-clouds#how-the-meters-work)).
- **Blue/green deployment:** running a new version beside the old and switching traffic at once; on platforms an immutable deployment with its own URL is a green environment that already exists, and promotion is the routing change ([Platforms Take Over the Deploy](/books/hosting-and-cloud/platform-deploys#blue-green-and-canary-as-platform-features)).
- **Build, release, run:** the twelve-factor split that is a platform's data model: a build is the artifact from a commit, a release is that build plus a specific configuration, and run is processes started from a release ([Platforms Take Over the Deploy](/books/hosting-and-cloud/platform-deploys#the-twelve-factor-contract)).
- **Buildpacks:** the platform's way of turning source into a runnable artifact without a Dockerfile: detect the language, install the runtime and dependencies, compile and produce something the platform can start ([Platforms Take Over the Deploy](/books/hosting-and-cloud/platform-deploys#git-push-and-the-process-model)).
- **Canary release:** shifting a small share of traffic to a new version before the rest, sold by platforms as percentage traffic weights on slots, revisions or aliases ([Platforms Take Over the Deploy](/books/hosting-and-cloud/platform-deploys#blue-green-and-canary-as-platform-features)).
- **Capistrano:** a deploy tool that scripts SSH to many servers to check out and build a release directory, link shared files and switch a symlink, so rollback moves the pointer ([How Code Got to Servers](/books/hosting-and-cloud/how-code-got-to-servers#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Classic PaaS:** platforms such as Heroku, Render and App Engine standard that run processes from source in fixed sizes per month or hour, simple but priced well above the underlay ([PaaS & Frontend Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/paas-and-frontend-clouds#the-alternatives)).
- **Frontend cloud:** platforms such as Vercel, Netlify and Cloudflare Pages serving static assets, functions and previews on a CDN, billed by plan plus metered CPU, memory, requests and bandwidth ([PaaS & Frontend Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/paas-and-frontend-clouds#the-alternatives)).
- **FTP:** the original file upload protocol (RFC 959) over separate cleartext control and data connections, with cleartext credentials and partial uploads served live ([How Code Got to Servers](/books/hosting-and-cloud/how-code-got-to-servers#the-alternatives)).
- **FTP account (control panel):** an additional login an account holder creates in the panel, confined to one directory, whose maximum number is a package limit ([How Code Got to Servers](/books/hosting-and-cloud/how-code-got-to-servers#the-file-manager-and-the-ftp-account)).
- **FTPS:** FTP with TLS on both channels (RFC 4217), which fixed the cleartext password but kept the two-connection design that troubles firewalls ([How Code Got to Servers](/books/hosting-and-cloud/how-code-got-to-servers#ftp-the-protocol-that-shipped-the-web)).
- **GitOps:** the practice of keeping desired state in Git with an agent that pulls and reconciles it, so a deploy is a merged commit rather than a push from CI, and CI holds no production credentials ([Platforms Take Over the Deploy](/books/hosting-and-cloud/platform-deploys#gitops-the-deploy-as-a-pull)).
- **Hyperscaler PaaS:** containers or code run on a cloud's own capacity (App Runner, Cloud Run, Azure App Service), with no reseller margin and the same bill and IAM as the rest of that cloud ([PaaS & Frontend Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/paas-and-frontend-clouds#the-alternatives)).
- **Image push:** a deploy where the customer builds an OCI image and the platform runs it, giving the same artifact everywhere while the customer owns base-image patching ([Platforms Take Over the Deploy](/books/hosting-and-cloud/platform-deploys#the-alternatives)).
- **Immutable deployment URL and alias:** the model in which every deploy gets a unique, permanent URL and a stable domain is an alias pointing at one, so promotion, rollback and previews are alias moves ([Platforms Take Over the Deploy](/books/hosting-and-cloud/platform-deploys#immutable-urls-and-aliases)).
- **Invocations:** a platform meter counting requests, alongside Active CPU and Provisioned Memory ([PaaS & Frontend Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/paas-and-frontend-clouds#how-the-meters-work)).
- **Isolate platforms:** platforms such as Cloudflare Workers that run code in V8 isolates everywhere, billed by requests and CPU time with runtime limits ([PaaS & Frontend Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/paas-and-frontend-clouds#the-alternatives)).
- **Machine platforms:** platforms such as Fly.io and Railway selling per-second VMs or containers with platform deploys, close to cloud prices with more operations left to the customer ([PaaS & Frontend Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/paas-and-frontend-clouds#the-alternatives)).
- **Panel file manager:** browser-based file operations inside a hosting account, needing no client but editing production directly, with the panel session as the credential ([How Code Got to Servers](/books/hosting-and-cloud/how-code-got-to-servers#the-alternatives)).
- **Preview deployment:** a deployment of every pull request or non-production branch to its own URL, updated on each push and linked from the pull request, turning review into using the change ([Platforms Take Over the Deploy](/books/hosting-and-cloud/platform-deploys#preview-deployments)).
- **Procfile:** a file in the repository naming an app's process types (`web`, `worker`, `clock`) and the command for each, Heroku's process model ([Platforms Take Over the Deploy](/books/hosting-and-cloud/platform-deploys#git-push-and-the-process-model)).
- **Provisioned Memory:** a platform meter that accrues GB-hours for an instance's lifetime until its last in-flight request finishes ([PaaS & Frontend Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/paas-and-frontend-clouds#how-the-meters-work)).
- **Release directory:** the deploy pattern of building each release in a new directory and switching a pointer to it, which made a deploy atomic and rollback instant, unlike copying into the served directory ([How Code Got to Servers](/books/hosting-and-cloud/how-code-got-to-servers#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **rsync:** a delta-transfer tool over SSH or its own daemon that moves only changed blocks, cheap for mirroring a release to many servers but updating in place unless paired with release directories ([How Code Got to Servers](/books/hosting-and-cloud/how-code-got-to-servers#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Scripted push from CI:** a pipeline that runs deploy commands with production credentials, working anywhere but making CI a production credential holder ([Platforms Take Over the Deploy](/books/hosting-and-cloud/platform-deploys#the-alternatives)).
- **SFTP:** the SSH File Transfer Protocol, file transfer inside one SSH channel with one port and key authentication, chrootable but never an RFC and still not atomic ([How Code Got to Servers](/books/hosting-and-cloud/how-code-got-to-servers#ssh-and-sftp)).
- **Slots and revisions:** named or numbered versions of an app with traffic weights (Azure App Service slots, Cloud Run revisions, Lambda aliases), giving percentage canaries inside one product ([Platforms Take Over the Deploy](/books/hosting-and-cloud/platform-deploys#the-alternatives)).
- **Source push:** a deploy where the platform detects, builds and runs the customer's source with buildpacks, needing no build configuration but with opaque builds ([Platforms Take Over the Deploy](/books/hosting-and-cloud/platform-deploys#the-alternatives)).
- **Spend cap:** a platform's limit on metered spend with an explicit action at the threshold (notify, pause production, pause everything); platform caps pause the customer's site, the opposite of a hyperscaler's alert-only default ([PaaS & Frontend Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/paas-and-frontend-clouds#spend-caps-and-pauses)).
- **Spin-down:** a free tier's practice of stopping a service after a period without inbound traffic, because the platform pays for warm capacity ([PaaS & Frontend Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/paas-and-frontend-clouds#what-a-platform-resells)).
- **Twelve-Factor App:** Heroku's written contract for apps (one codebase, declared dependencies, config in the environment, backing services as resources, separate build, release and run, stateless processes and more), whose factor five is a platform's data model ([Platforms Take Over the Deploy](/books/hosting-and-cloud/platform-deploys#the-twelve-factor-contract)).

## CI/CD Platforms

- **24-hour lease (macOS):** Apple's license term allowing Apple hardware to be leased for developer services only for at least 24 consecutive hours to one lessee, which is why hosted macOS minutes carry idle cost ([Runners & Build Infrastructure](/books/hosting-and-cloud/runners-and-build-infrastructure#macos-fleets)).
- **Actions Runner Controller (ARC):** the Kubernetes way to run ephemeral GitHub Actions runners, a community project that became a GitHub project and gained a runner scale sets mode ([Runners & Build Infrastructure](/books/hosting-and-cloud/runners-and-build-infrastructure#ephemeral-runners-and-autoscaling)).
- **Amazon ECR:** AWS's container registry, billed for storage and internet egress with same-region transfer free ([Artifacts & Registries](/books/hosting-and-cloud/artifacts-and-registries#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Argo CD and Argo Rollouts:** CNCF Kubernetes delivery tools that treat Git as desired state and run blue/green and canary rollouts with metric analysis and automatic promotion or rollback ([Continuous Delivery Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/continuous-delivery-platforms#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **AWS CodeBuild:** AWS's build service, with container-based build environments, Lambda compute and reserved capacity (including Mac), billed per minute by compute type ([Runners & Build Infrastructure](/books/hosting-and-cloud/runners-and-build-infrastructure#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **AWS CodeDeploy:** AWS's deployment service, based on Amazon's internal Apollo, doing rolling updates with health checks and automatic rollback, gated through CodePipeline stages ([Continuous Delivery Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/continuous-delivery-platforms#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Azure Pipelines:** CI from Microsoft's Azure DevOps suite for any repository, priced by parallel jobs with unlimited minutes ([From Tinderbox to Jenkins: How CI Began](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ci-history#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Azure Pipelines environments:** Azure DevOps environments whose approvals and checks sit on the resource being deployed to, with deployment strategies in YAML ([Continuous Delivery Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/continuous-delivery-platforms#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Bamboo:** Atlassian's licensed CI server, whose hosted Bamboo Cloud was discontinued and whose Data Center edition has a scheduled end of life ([From Tinderbox to Jenkins: How CI Began](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ci-history#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Bitbucket Pipelines:** Atlassian's CI built into the Bitbucket code host, billed in build minutes scaled by runner size ([From Tinderbox to Jenkins: How CI Began](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ci-history#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Branch rules:** an environment protection that limits which branches or tags may deploy to an environment, such as protected branches only ([Continuous Delivery Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/continuous-delivery-platforms#approvals-and-environment-protection)).
- **Buildkite:** CI sold as the hybrid model: a SaaS control plane with customer-run agents, so source code and secrets stay in the customer's infrastructure ([Runners & Build Infrastructure](/books/hosting-and-cloud/runners-and-build-infrastructure#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Bypass (deployment gate):** an administrator overriding an environment's approvals or checks; Azure Pipelines shows the bypass, and the playbook logs every one ([Continuous Delivery Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/continuous-delivery-platforms#approvals-and-environment-protection)).
- **CircleCI:** independent hosted CI that later added self-hosted runners, priced in credits per minute by resource class ([From Tinderbox to Jenkins: How CI Began](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ci-history#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **CloudBees:** the commercial Jenkins company ([The CI/CD Platform Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ci-cd-platform-business#the-private-vendors)).
- **Code-host CI:** CI built into where the code lives (GitHub Actions, GitLab CI, Bitbucket Pipelines, Azure Pipelines), the kind of hosted CI that mostly survived ([From Tinderbox to Jenkins: How CI Began](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ci-history#the-alternatives)).
- **Codecov Bash Uploader:** the 2021 incident in which a credential exposed by a Docker image build let attackers modify a script that sent CI environment variables out, which argued for short-lived credentials and rotating everything in CI ([Pipeline Supply-Chain Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pipeline-supply-chain-security#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Cost factors:** pricing (GitLab, Bitbucket) that expresses each machine type as a multiple of a base minute drawn from the plan's quota ([The CI/CD Platform Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ci-cd-platform-business#pricing-models)).
- **Credits (CI):** a single currency the customer buys in packs and spends per minute by resource class (CircleCI, Travis), which lets the vendor reprice a class without changing the pack price ([The CI/CD Platform Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ci-cd-platform-business#pricing-models)).
- **CruiseControl:** ThoughtWorks' open-source build loop that polled version control, an early CI server ([From Tinderbox to Jenkins: How CI Began](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ci-history#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Custom deployment protection rules:** GitHub's external checks that let a third-party service approve or reject a deployment to an environment ([Continuous Delivery Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/continuous-delivery-platforms#approvals-and-environment-protection)).
- **Cyber Resilience Act (CRA):** the EU regulation that phases in vulnerability and incident reporting for manufacturers and an Annex I requirement to document components, including a machine-readable SBOM ([Pipeline Supply-Chain Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pipeline-supply-chain-security#sboms-and-the-law)).
- **Digest:** the immutable content address of an image, which deployments should reference because tags move, and which tells a retention policy what is in use ([Artifacts & Registries](/books/hosting-and-cloud/artifacts-and-registries#how-a-registry-works-for-the-operator)).
- **Docker Build Cloud:** Docker's hosted build service, with build minutes included in Docker plans and no on-demand overage ([The CI/CD Platform Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ci-cd-platform-business#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Docker Hub:** Docker's public registry, which rate-limits pulls per account and, for anonymous traffic, per IPv4 address or IPv6 /64 ([Artifacts & Registries](/books/hosting-and-cloud/artifacts-and-registries#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Ephemeral runner:** a runner that gets a machine nobody used before and nobody uses after, the default for hosted jobs and the rule whenever the code is untrusted ([Runners & Build Infrastructure](/books/hosting-and-cloud/runners-and-build-infrastructure#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Executive Order 14028:** the US order that tasked NIST with software supply chain guidance, with SBOMs among the workstreams ([Pipeline Supply-Chain Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pipeline-supply-chain-security#sboms-and-the-law)).
- **Firecracker microVM:** a lightweight VM per job that gives Docker a VM boundary, the alternative to mounting the host's Docker socket or running privileged Docker-in-Docker ([Runners & Build Infrastructure](/books/hosting-and-cloud/runners-and-build-infrastructure#containers-or-microvms)).
- **Flag service:** releasing by feature flag rather than deploy (LaunchDarkly, OpenFeature providers), with per-user rollout and an instant kill switch; a flag flip is a release ([Continuous Delivery Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/continuous-delivery-platforms#the-alternatives)).
- **Fulcio:** Sigstore's certificate authority, which issues short-lived certificates bound to an OIDC identity ([Pipeline Supply-Chain Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pipeline-supply-chain-security#provenance-and-signing)).
- **GhostAction:** an incident in which workflows were pushed into repositories with stolen maintainer access to post secrets out, which argued for branch protection on workflow files and short-lived tokens ([Pipeline Supply-Chain Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pipeline-supply-chain-security#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **GitHub Actions:** workflows in the GitHub code host, run on hosted or self-hosted runners and priced per minute by OS and size ([From Tinderbox to Jenkins: How CI Began](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ci-history#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **GitHub Container Registry (GHCR):** GitHub's registry at `ghcr.io`, private by default, with storage and bandwidth "currently free" ([Artifacts & Registries](/books/hosting-and-cloud/artifacts-and-registries#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **GitHub environments:** Actions' deployment targets carrying required reviewers, a wait timer, branch rules and custom protection rules, set outside the workflow file ([Continuous Delivery Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/continuous-delivery-platforms#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **GitLab CI:** CI built into the GitLab code host, run on runners ([From Tinderbox to Jenkins: How CI Began](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ci-history#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **GitLab protected environments:** GitLab environments that restrict deployment, with deployment approvals on Premium and Ultimate ([Continuous Delivery Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/continuous-delivery-platforms#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **GitLab Runner autoscaling:** GitLab's runner autoscaling through Fleeting, a plugin-based abstraction over a cloud's instance groups, driven by the Docker Autoscaler and Instance executors ([Runners & Build Infrastructure](/books/hosting-and-cloud/runners-and-build-infrastructure#ephemeral-runners-and-autoscaling)).
- **Google Artifact Registry:** Google's registry, the successor to Container Registry, whose shutdown forced a registry migration on customers ([Artifacts & Registries](/books/hosting-and-cloud/artifacts-and-registries#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Google Cloud Build:** Google's per-minute cloud build service ([The CI/CD Platform Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ci-cd-platform-business#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Hudson, then Jenkins:** the open-source CI server with plugins, renamed Jenkins after a trademark dispute; Jenkins sits under the Continuous Delivery Foundation and Hudson went to Eclipse ([From Tinderbox to Jenkins: How CI Began](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ci-history#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Hybrid model:** self-hosted runners on a SaaS control plane: the customer owns hardware, network and cost while the vendor runs orchestration, and platform fees can appear ([The CI/CD Platform Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ci-cd-platform-business#the-alternatives)).
- **Independent hosted CI:** hosted CI sold by a vendor that isn't the code host (CircleCI, Travis CI, Buildkite), selling speed and features and working with several code hosts ([From Tinderbox to Jenkins: How CI Began](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ci-history#the-alternatives)).
- **JFrog Artifactory:** JFrog's universal repository for many package formats, self-hosted or cloud ([Artifacts & Registries](/books/hosting-and-cloud/artifacts-and-registries#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Keyless signing:** signing with short-lived certificates bound to an OIDC identity and recorded in a public log (Sigstore), so there are no signing keys to steal ([Pipeline Supply-Chain Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pipeline-supply-chain-security#the-alternatives)).
- **Kubernetes reconciler:** continuous delivery with Git as desired state and controllers for rollout strategies (Argo CD and Rollouts), with no CD server and built-in drift correction ([Continuous Delivery Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/continuous-delivery-platforms#the-alternatives)).
- **Licensed server:** a commercially licensed CI server with build agents (TeamCity, Bamboo Data Center), with vendor support and vendor end-of-life risk ([From Tinderbox to Jenkins: How CI Began](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ci-history#the-alternatives)).
- **OIDC federation:** a CI job exchanging a signed platform token describing itself for short-lived cloud credentials under a trust policy, so no long-lived cloud keys sit in the CI secret store ([Pipeline Supply-Chain Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pipeline-supply-chain-security#the-alternatives)).
- **Parallel jobs:** pricing (Azure Pipelines) that sells concurrency, unlimited minutes per job slot, so the bill is fixed and slow builds cost waiting instead ([The CI/CD Platform Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ci-cd-platform-business#pricing-models)).
- **Per-minute SKUs:** pricing (GitHub, CodeBuild, Cloud Build) that prices each machine type separately and rounds each job up to a whole minute, a rounding that is part of the margin ([The CI/CD Platform Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ci-cd-platform-business#pricing-models)).
- **Pull accounting:** a registry counting manifest requests per principal and, for anonymous traffic, per IPv4 address or IPv6 /64, in a sliding window; layer caches don't reduce the count ([Artifacts & Registries](/books/hosting-and-cloud/artifacts-and-registries#how-a-registry-works-for-the-operator)).
- **Pull-through cache:** a local mirror of an upstream registry that avoids rate limits and egress and survives upstream outages, at the risk of a stale or poisoned cache ([Artifacts & Registries](/books/hosting-and-cloud/artifacts-and-registries#the-alternatives)).
- **Rekor:** Sigstore's public transparency log, which lets a consumer verify that an artifact was signed by a given workflow at a given time ([Pipeline Supply-Chain Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pipeline-supply-chain-security#provenance-and-signing)).
- **Remote execution:** distributing and caching build actions across many machines over Bazel's Remote Execution API, where a cache hit skips work; it needs hermetic builds ([Runners & Build Infrastructure](/books/hosting-and-cloud/runners-and-build-infrastructure#the-alternatives)).
- **Retention policies (registry):** rules that delete untagged manifests after a period and keep the newest N tags per repository, never deleting digests pinned by a running deployment ([Artifacts & Registries](/books/hosting-and-cloud/artifacts-and-registries#how-a-registry-works-for-the-operator)).
- **Runner labels:** the scheduler's interface: jobs ask for labels such as `linux`, `arm64` or `gpu`, and the control plane matches them to runners that advertise them ([Runners & Build Infrastructure](/books/hosting-and-cloud/runners-and-build-infrastructure#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **SBOM (software bill of materials):** a component inventory per build that answers "are we affected" fast and supports CRA compliance ([Pipeline Supply-Chain Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pipeline-supply-chain-security#the-alternatives)).
- **Self-approval:** a person approving a deployment they started, which GitHub environments can prevent and GitLab disallows by default ([Continuous Delivery Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/continuous-delivery-platforms#approvals-and-environment-protection)).
- **Self-hosted runners:** the customer's machines running the vendor's agent, which polls the control plane for jobs, giving the customer its own hardware, network and cost ([Runners & Build Infrastructure](/books/hosting-and-cloud/runners-and-build-infrastructure#hosted-hybrid-and-self-hosted)).
- **Self-run OCI registry:** registry software (Distribution, Harbor) on the operator's own storage, with full control and no rate limits but storage, garbage collection and availability to run ([Artifacts & Registries](/books/hosting-and-cloud/artifacts-and-registries#the-alternatives)).
- **Self-run open-source server:** a CI server the customer runs (Jenkins), running any workflow through plugins with no per-minute cost, at the price of plugin sprawl and upgrades ([From Tinderbox to Jenkins: How CI Began](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ci-history#the-alternatives)).
- **SHA pinning:** referencing actions by commit rather than tag, enforced by organization policy, so a moved tag can't reach the workflow ([Pipeline Supply-Chain Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pipeline-supply-chain-security#the-alternatives)).
