- **Shai-Hulud:** a self-replicating npm worm that stole tokens and republished victims' packages; its second wave registered infected hosts as self-hosted runners ([Pipeline Supply-Chain Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pipeline-supply-chain-security#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Signed provenance:** the build platform signing a statement of what it built and how, so consumers can verify origin ([Pipeline Supply-Chain Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pipeline-supply-chain-security#the-alternatives)).
- **SLSA:** the supply-chain framework whose Build levels run from L0 (none) through L2 (signed provenance from a hosted platform) to L3 (a hardened platform with isolated runs and an unreachable signing secret) ([Pipeline Supply-Chain Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pipeline-supply-chain-security#provenance-and-signing)).
- **SolarWinds (SUNSPOT and SUNBURST):** the incident in which malware on the build server swapped one source file during the build and restored it afterward, the case for hardened, isolated builds with provenance ([Pipeline Supply-Chain Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pipeline-supply-chain-security#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Sonatype Nexus Repository:** Sonatype's artifact repository, whose free Community Edition has a usage ceiling ([Artifacts & Registries](/books/hosting-and-cloud/artifacts-and-registries#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Spinnaker:** Netflix's open-source multi-cloud continuous delivery tool of pipelines of deployment stages, now under the Continuous Delivery Foundation ([Continuous Delivery Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/continuous-delivery-platforms#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **TeamCity:** JetBrains' licensed CI server with build agents ([From Tinderbox to Jenkins: How CI Began](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ci-history#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Third-party runner vendors:** vendors (Depot, Blacksmith, Namespace, Ubicloud, WarpBuild, Actuated) selling faster or cheaper machines registered to the code host as self-hosted runners, tying their business to its terms ([Runners & Build Infrastructure](/books/hosting-and-cloud/runners-and-build-infrastructure#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Tinderbox:** Netscape's release-engineering system of build machines reporting to a status page, the shape every CI system kept: many agents, one coordinator, a result per commit ([From Tinderbox to Jenkins: How CI Began](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ci-history#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **tj-actions/changed-files (CVE-2025-30066):** the incident in which a popular action's tags were repointed at a commit that dumped secrets into logs, the case for SHA pinning ([Pipeline Supply-Chain Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pipeline-supply-chain-security#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Travis CI:** hosted CI that was free for open source on travis-ci.org until that service shut down, later priced in credits ([From Tinderbox to Jenkins: How CI Began](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ci-history#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Trusted publishing:** a registry accepting uploads only from a named workflow, exchanging its OIDC token for a short-lived upload token so no publish token is stored (npm, PyPI) ([Pipeline Supply-Chain Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pipeline-supply-chain-security#the-alternatives)).
- **Universal repository:** one store for every package format plus proxies of public registries (Artifactory, Nexus), bought for governance ([Artifacts & Registries](/books/hosting-and-cloud/artifacts-and-registries#the-alternatives)).
- **Wait timer:** a delay an environment imposes before a deployment proceeds, such as GitHub's configurable timer or Azure's business-hours check ([Continuous Delivery Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/continuous-delivery-platforms#approvals-and-environment-protection)).

## Infrastructure as Code

- **22 levels of precedence:** the order in which Ansible resolves a variable, from command-line values and role defaults up to extra vars, which always win ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#variables-roles-and-collections)).
- **Account Factory for Terraform (AFT):** AWS's pipeline that vends Control Tower accounts from an account request file and applies customizations to all accounts, a set of them or one ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#landing-zones-and-account-vending)).
- **Account vending:** stamping out a new account, subscription or project through one pipeline that places it in the tree, attaches the baseline and records its owning team ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#landing-zones-and-account-vending)).
- **Action (Event-driven Ansible):** what a matching rule runs, such as `run_playbook`, `run_module` or `run_job_template`, or posting an event or fact to a ruleset ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#awx-the-platform-and-event-driven-ansible)).
- **Advisory (policy):** Sentinel's default enforcement level, where a failed policy is allowed and logged as a warning ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#policy-as-code-at-plan-time)).
- **Aminator:** Netflix's open-source AMI baker, which built an image from a volume created from a base AMI's snapshot, later driven by Netflix's Bakery API ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#golden-images-and-immutable-servers)).
- **Ancestry (images):** HCP Packer's record of each image's parent, used to warn when a child is built on an outdated parent and to cascade revocation ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#hcp-packer-channels-and-revocation)).
- **Ansible:** push-based configuration management over SSH from a control node, in YAML, with no agent on managed nodes and ordering across hosts built in ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#the-alternatives)).
- **Ansible Automation Platform:** Red Hat's commercial Ansible platform: a job server with RBAC and an API, execution environments and Event-driven Ansible ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#awx-the-platform-and-event-driven-ansible)).
- **Ansible Galaxy:** the public hub from which Ansible collections are installed, alongside private Galaxy-compatible servers ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#variables-roles-and-collections)).
- **ansible-core:** Ansible's runtime and CLI (`ansible`, `ansible-playbook`), which the `ansible` package extends with curated community collections ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **ansible-pull:** Ansible run in reverse, pulling content from Git on cron, which needs no server but puts credentials on every node and keeps no central record ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#the-alternatives)).
- **Artifact (Packer):** the output of one Packer build, for example the AMI ids in each region ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#how-packer-works)).
- **Atlantis:** self-hosted, open-source pull-request automation for Terraform that costs nothing but the server, the floor hosted IaC platforms have to justify ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **AWS CDK:** AWS's framework for defining infrastructure in general-purpose languages, which synthesizes CloudFormation templates so CloudFormation still does the provisioning ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#general-purpose-languages-cdk-cdktf-and-pulumi)).
- **AWS Config:** AWS's recorded configuration items per resource, with organization aggregators, rule evaluations and retention up to seven years, the inventory under its detective controls ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#the-resource-catalog)).
- **AWS Control Tower:** AWS's landing zone service, generally available June 24, 2019, with an Account Factory and mandatory, strongly recommended and elective controls that don't bind the management account ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#how-it-got-here)).
- **AWS Controllers for Kubernetes (ACK):** one Kubernetes controller per AWS service, managing AWS objects as custom resources on any cluster ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#kubernetes-as-the-control-plane-crossplane-and-ack)).
- **AWS OpsWorks:** AWS's retired service that provided managed Chef and Puppet servers ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **AWS Systems Manager State Manager:** AWS's cloud-native convergence, where an association applies an SSM document (such as one that runs Ansible playbooks) to tagged nodes on a schedule ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **AWX:** the open-source upstream of Ansible's controller (web UI, REST API and task engine), whose releases are paused during a refactoring ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Azure Automation State Configuration:** Microsoft's built-in PowerShell DSC pull server, from which nodes pull configurations and report compliance ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Azure Machine Configuration:** configurations assigned through Azure Policy to Azure and Arc-enabled machines and evaluated by the machine's agent ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Azure Policy effects:** the single effect each Azure Policy definition carries (`deny`, `audit`, `modify`, `deployIfNotExists` and others), evaluated in a fixed order, with assignments at different scopes combined as cumulative most restrictive ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#organization-policy)).
- **Azure Resource Graph:** Azure's KQL-queryable index of resources, policy states and 14 days of changes, updated with short latency but not strongly consistent ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#the-resource-catalog)).
- **Azure VM Image Builder:** Azure's managed image-building service built on HashiCorp Packer, driven by image templates ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Backends:** where Terraform stores and locks the state document, such as the S3 backend with server-side encryption, a KMS key and bucket versioning ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#state-as-a-data-model)).
- **Backstage:** the software catalog Spotify open-sourced on March 16, 2020, now CNCF Incubating, whose entities live in `catalog-info.yaml` files beside the code with a required owner on each Component ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#the-resource-catalog)).
- **Baked image:** an image with the software and its configuration already in it, which removes drift by construction but makes every change a rebuild ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#baking-vs-frying)).
- **Beacons:** Salt monitors run by a minion that fire an event onto the bus when they see something, such as a file change or high load ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#reactor-and-beacons)).
- **Berkshelf:** the tool that managed Chef cookbooks and their dependencies with a `Berksfile` and a lockfile, before Policyfiles ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#cookbooks-berkshelf-and-supermarket)).
- **Bicep / ARM:** Azure's DSL compiled to ARM JSON, with service-held state and `what-if` previews, and new Azure resource types on day one ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#the-alternatives)).
- **Bodies:** CFEngine's reusable blocks holding the complex attribute sets of promises ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#how-cfengine-works)).
- **Buckets and versions:** HCP Packer's registry structure: a bucket per image, and an immutable version for each completed build ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#hcp-packer-channels-and-revocation)).
- **Build (Packer):** one source run with its provisioners and post-processors; builds in a template run in parallel unless told otherwise ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#how-packer-works)).
- **Builder:** the Packer plugin component behind a source that creates a machine on one platform and turns it into an image, such as `amazon-ebs` or `googlecompute` ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#how-packer-works)).
- **Bundles:** CFEngine's reusable groups of promises ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#how-cfengine-works)).
- **Catalog:** the graph of resources and their relationships that the Puppet server compiles for one node, plain data that keeps other nodes' code and data off the machine ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#the-puppet-run)).
- **CDKTF:** HashiCorp's CDK equivalent that synthesized Terraform configuration from general-purpose languages, since sunset and archived ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#general-purpose-languages-cdk-cdktf-and-pulumi)).
- **CFEngine:** the original convergence tool, in which an agent on every host pulls policy from a policy server and converges itself on an interval ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Changed:** the run status for a convergence run that repaired something ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#the-convergence-vocabulary)).
- **Channels:** named, movable pointers to image versions in HCP Packer, such as `production`, so promotion and rollback move a channel while versions stay immutable ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Checkov:** an Apache-2.0 policy scanner with a large built-in policy set for AWS, Azure and Google Cloud that scans Terraform and other configuration ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#policy-as-code-at-plan-time)).
- **Chef 360 Platform:** Progress's SaaS or self-managed successor to Infra Server, Automate and Supermarket, covering node management, Courier job orchestration, skills and licensing ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Chef Automate:** Chef's dashboards, compliance reporting and workflow product, deprecated with a set end of life ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Chef Infra:** pull-based configuration management in which the node compiles Ruby recipes and converges them in order ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#the-alternatives)).
- **Chef Infra Client:** the Chef agent on every node, which compiles and converges the node's run-list ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Chef Infra Server:** the open-source server that stores cookbooks, node objects, roles, environments and policies and answers search, now deprecated ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **chef-solo:** a way to run Chef Infra Client without Chef Infra Server, with cookbooks from a local directory or a tarball URL ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#chef-solo-and-local-mode)).
- **chef-zero:** a lightweight, in-process implementation of the Chef server API backed by the local repository, which local mode points the client at ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#chef-solo-and-local-mode)).
- **ChefSpec:** a unit testing framework for Chef cookbooks that runs a converge in memory and asserts what each resource would have done ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#testing-chefspec-test-kitchen-and-inspec)).
- **Cinc:** community rebuilds of Chef's open-source code (Client, Workstation, Auditor, Server) without Progress's EULA ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Classification:** the Puppet run step in which the server decides which classes a node gets, from node definitions or an External Node Classifier ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#the-puppet-run)).
- **Cloud Asset Inventory:** Google Cloud's inventory of resource metadata, IAM and organization policies, with 35 days of history and export to BigQuery ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#the-resource-catalog)).
- **cloud-init:** the boot-time agent most Linux cloud images carry, running in stages (Detect, Local, Network, Config, Final) to configure a generic image at first boot ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#baking-vs-frying)).
- **CloudFormation:** AWS's templating service of JSON or YAML templates with service-held stack state and change sets, AWS only and with no charge ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#the-alternatives)).
- **CloudQuery:** a tool that syncs cloud and SaaS APIs into a SQL destination on a schedule, priced on rows synced ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#the-resource-catalog)).
- **CMDB (configuration management database):** the IT service management record of configuration items and their relationships, filled from cloud APIs by discovery, the older form of a resource catalog ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#the-resource-catalog)).
- **Collection (Ansible):** the distribution format for playbooks, roles, modules and plugins together ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#variables-roles-and-collections)).
- **Communicator:** the mechanism Packer uses to upload files and run scripts on the machine being built, usually `ssh` or `winrm` ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#how-packer-works)).
- **Compile phase:** the first phase of a Chef run, in which recipe code runs and each resource it encounters is added to the ordered resource collection ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#the-chef-run)).
- **Compile versus converge:** desired state is compiled into a concrete resource list for one machine and then converged; where the compile happens (server, node or control node) is the biggest architectural difference between tools ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#the-convergence-vocabulary)).
- **Conditions (Event-driven Ansible):** expressions in a rule evaluated against incoming events, such as `event.payload.status == "OK"` ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#awx-the-platform-and-event-driven-ansible)).
- **`config_revision`:** the column recording which desired-state revision a convergence run applied: a Policyfile `revision_id`, a playbook or state-tree commit, or a hash where no id exists ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#how-to-build-it)).
- **Conformance pack:** a collection of AWS Config rules and remediation actions deployed as one template to an account and region or across an organization ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#runtime-and-detective-controls)).
- **Conftest:** a wrapper around Open Policy Agent for testing configuration files against Rego policies, with rules named `deny` and `conftest verify` for testing the policies ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#policy-as-code-at-plan-time)).
- **Container images (alternative to baking):** building an OCI image and running it on a generic VM host, with fast builds and one artifact ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#the-alternatives)).
- **Control (InSpec):** a check that defines a regulatory recommendation or requirement for the state of a system, with an impact score ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#testing-chefspec-test-kitchen-and-inspec)).
- **Control node:** the machine from which the Ansible CLI tools run against managed nodes ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#how-an-ansible-run-works)).
- **Convergence:** repeated runs that move a machine toward its desired state and then hold it there, acting only on the difference ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#the-convergence-vocabulary)).
- **`convergence_runs`:** the table with one row per convergence run per machine: a normalized status, counts, the revision applied and a pointer to the full report ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#how-to-build-it)).
- **Cookbook:** Chef's unit of distribution: recipes, attribute files, templates, files, custom resources, libraries and `metadata.rb` with name, version and dependencies ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#cookbooks-berkshelf-and-supermarket)).
- **Cookbook version constraints:** limits on an environment that keep a new cookbook version moving through test environments before production ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#roles-environments-and-policyfiles)).
- **Corrective control:** a guardrail that fixes a resource after the fact, such as Azure Policy remediation or AWS Config remediation, safe for a tag and dangerous for a security group a service depends on ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#guardrails-by-layer)).
- **Courier:** Chef 360's job orchestration, in which jobs are JSON documents split into steps per node and delivered by a pull model ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#licensing-chef-360-and-cinc)).
- **Crossplane:** a CNCF project that installs providers whose managed resources are Kubernetes custom resources for external objects, reconciled continuously instead of on each apply ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#kubernetes-as-the-control-plane-crossplane-and-ack)).
- **CSPM (cloud security posture management):** detective controls and posture scores run over a cloud's inventory, such as Security Hub CSPM, Defender for Cloud and Security Command Center ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#runtime-and-detective-controls)).
- **Custom resource (Chef):** the recommended way to write a Chef resource since 12.5: one file with `property` declarations and `action` blocks, replacing the resource-plus-provider (LWRP) split ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#resources-guards-and-notifications)).
- **Data bags:** Chef's global JSON data, each item a document with an `id`, read by recipes ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#cookbooks-berkshelf-and-supermarket)).
- **Data source (Packer):** a lookup at build time, such as the newest AMI matching a filter or a secret from a secrets manager ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#how-packer-works)).
- **Dependency lock file:** Terraform's `.terraform.lock.hcl`, which records for each provider the exact version selected, the constraints considered and its checksums ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#how-terraform-works-core-providers-and-the-graph)).
- **Deprecate (image):** hiding an image from other accounts' listings and the console while it stays launchable by id, the step before deregistering it ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Deregister:** removing an image for good; the owner pays for its snapshots until it does ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Desired state:** a description of what a machine should be (packages, files, services, users), not the steps to get there ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#the-convergence-vocabulary)).
- **Detective control:** a guardrail that finds what already exists out of policy, only as good as the inventory under it ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#guardrails-by-layer)).
- **Distribution configuration:** the EC2 Image Builder setting for the regions, KMS keys, cross-account sharing and launch template updates an image goes to ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#cloud-image-builders)).
- **Drift:** a changed convergence run with no change to the desired state, meaning someone changed the machine and the tool undid it ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#the-convergence-vocabulary)).
- **Drift finding:** a detected difference between code and what runs, opened with an owner and closed one of three ways: revert it, write it into code, or move the resource out of that state on purpose ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#exceptions-drift-and-evidence)).
- **Driver (Test Kitchen):** the platform Test Kitchen creates test machines on, such as Vagrant, EC2, Azure or Docker ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#testing-chefspec-test-kitchen-and-inspec)).
- **Dry run:** a run that reports what it would change and changes nothing: Puppet's noop, Chef's why-run, Ansible's check mode, Salt's `test=True` ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#the-convergence-vocabulary)).
- **EC2 Image Builder:** AWS's managed pipeline producing AMIs and container images from image recipes, with infrastructure and distribution configurations ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#cloud-image-builders)).
- **Environment (Chef):** a workflow stage such as staging or production, with default and override attributes and cookbook version constraints ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#roles-environments-and-policyfiles)).
- **error-cleanup-provisioner:** an optional Packer provisioner that runs only when normal provisioning fails, before the machine shuts down ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#provisioners)).
- **Event-driven Ansible:** Ansible's event reaction feature, whose unit is a rulebook of rulesets with sources, rules and actions ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#awx-the-platform-and-event-driven-ansible)).
- **Execution environment:** a container image that acts as the Ansible control node, bundling ansible-core, ansible-runner, Python and dependencies ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#variables-roles-and-collections)).
- **Execution phase:** the second phase of a Chef run, which walks the resource collection in order and changes only the difference from desired state ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#the-chef-run)).
- **Exemption (Azure Policy):** Azure's exception object, a waiver or mitigated category with requester, approver and ticket metadata and an optional expiry, kept for the record after it expires ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#exceptions-drift-and-evidence)).
- **Expected lag (inventory):** how stale an inventory source may be and still be trusted; a resource unseen for longer is unconfirmed, not present ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#how-to-build-it)).
- **Exported resources:** Puppet's cross-node configuration, in which a node publishes a resource with `@@` for other nodes to collect through PuppetDB ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#puppetdb-and-exported-resources)).
- **External Node Classifier (ENC):** a program that takes a host name and returns the Puppet classes and parameters the node gets ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#the-puppet-run)).
- **`external_ref`:** the provider's identifier for an object, unique per kind and never reused, so an import, a refresh and an abuse report all resolve to one resource ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#how-to-build-it)).
- **Extra vars:** Ansible variables passed with `-e`, which always win precedence ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#variables-roles-and-collections)).
- **Fact gathering:** the agent inventorying a machine (OS, kernel, addresses, hardware, custom values) before deciding anything; facts select which desired state applies ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#the-convergence-vocabulary)).
- **Failed:** the run status for a convergence run that couldn't repair something ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#the-convergence-vocabulary)).
- **Fingerprint (Packer):** the identifier each `packer build` run gets in HCP Packer; a build fails when its fingerprint matches an existing, complete version ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#hcp-packer-channels-and-revocation)).
- **Free strategy:** the Ansible strategy that lets each host run to the end of a play as fast as it can ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#how-an-ansible-run-works)).
- **Fried image:** a generic image configured at boot, or continuously, by configuration management ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#baking-vs-frying)).
- **Golden image:** the approved, built and tested image everything launches from ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#golden-images-and-immutable-servers)).
- **Google Cloud VM Manager OS policies:** OS policy assignments that target VMs by labels, OS and zone, enforced by the OS Config agent ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Grains:** facts about a Salt minion (OS, domain, IP address, kernel, custom values), set by the minion and therefore a claim, not proof ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#how-salt-works)).
- **Graph (Terraform):** the dependency graph built from configuration and state together, with a node per resource and provider configuration and edges from references and `depends_on` ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#how-terraform-works-core-providers-and-the-graph)).
- **Guard:** a Chef condition on a resource whose effect isn't knowable, `not_if` to skip when true and `only_if` to run only when true ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#resources-guards-and-notifications)).
- **Guardrail:** one rule written in plain words, enforced or checked by some engine at one or more layers: organization, plan time, admission and runtime ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#guardrails-by-layer)).
- **Guardrail exception:** a waiver or mitigation recorded with a category, a reason, an approver who isn't the requester and an end date; an expired one reopens its findings ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#exceptions-drift-and-evidence)).
- **Habitat:** Chef's application packaging plus a runtime supervisor, which packaged the application rather than converging the host ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#from-opscode-to-progress)).
- **Handlers (Ansible):** tasks that run only when a task reporting "changed" notifies them, once regardless of how many tasks notify them ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#how-an-ansible-run-works)).
- **Hard mandatory:** Sentinel's strictest enforcement level, with no override; the only bypass is removing the policy ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#policy-as-code-at-plan-time)).
- **HCP Packer:** HashiCorp's registry of image metadata (not images): buckets of versions, channels as named pointers, ancestry and revocation ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **HCP Terraform:** HashiCorp's hosted Terraform platform for state, runs, policy and registry, self-managed as Terraform Enterprise, billed hourly per managed resource ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **HCSEC-2021-12:** the HashiCorp advisory after the Codecov compromise exposed the GPG key that signs its download checksums, which argued for keeping signing keys out of CI that runs third-party code ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#when-state-and-signing-keys-leak)).
- **HCSEC-2026-23:** the HashiCorp advisory for flaws in terraform-mcp-server, the server that lets AI assistants drive Terraform, which could expose or reuse users' Terraform tokens ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#when-state-and-signing-keys-leak)).
- **Health assessments:** HCP Terraform's scheduled drift detection and continuous validation of conditions and `check` blocks, which never change infrastructure ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#hashicorp-s-governance-layer)).
- **Hiera:** Puppet's key-value configuration data, looked up during compilation through a defaults-with-overrides hierarchy ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#hiera-modules-and-the-forge)).
- **Highstate:** everything a Salt top file assigns to a minion, applied by running `state.apply` with no SLS targets ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#how-salt-works)).
- **Idempotence:** the property that applying the same desired state twice changes nothing the second time, which each resource implementation must have ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#the-convergence-vocabulary)).
- **Idempotent create:** the provider API requirement that a retried apply after a timeout doesn't produce a twin, met by a client-supplied idempotency key or an enforced unique name ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#generated-providers-and-what-a-provider-api-must-expose)).
- **Image families:** Google Cloud's pointer to the most recent image in a family; deprecating the newest image rolls the family back to the previous one ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Image recipe:** an EC2 Image Builder definition of a base image plus components, YAML or JSON documents run in build, validate and test phases ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#cloud-image-builders)).
- **Immutable server:** a server that, once deployed, is never modified, only replaced ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#golden-images-and-immutable-servers)).
- **Import (Terraform):** adopting an existing object into state with an `import` block naming the address and the vendor's identifier or provider-defined identity ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#import-moved-and-removed)).
- **Infracost:** a tool that parses Terraform, CloudFormation and CDK projects or a plan and posts the monthly cost difference on each pull request ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#cost-in-the-pull-request)).
- **Infrastructure configuration:** the EC2 Image Builder setting for the instance type, subnet and IAM role a build runs with ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#cloud-image-builders)).
- **InSpec:** compliance checks as code, organized as profiles of controls, run separately from the tool that converged the machine ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#from-opscode-to-progress)).
- **Integration tests (IaC):** applying a module to a real account and checking the result from outside, which catches provider bugs, API defaults and missing permissions that mocks can't ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#testing-infrastructure-code)).
- **Inventory (Ansible):** the list of managed nodes from one or more sources, with groups for targeting and assigning variables ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#how-an-ansible-run-works)).
- **Kept / repaired:** CFEngine's outcomes for an evaluated promise (already true, or fixed), after which it is locked for a period so repeated runs don't re-evaluate it ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#how-cfengine-works)).
- **Landing zone:** the multi-account structure plus the process that decides where new accounts go and what they inherit ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#landing-zones-and-account-vending)).
- **Landing Zone Accelerator:** AWS's pipeline, installed from a CloudFormation template, that deploys a regulated estate's baseline from configuration files ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#landing-zones-and-account-vending)).
- **Lineage and serial:** a Terraform state's fixed identifier and its write counter, which act as optimistic concurrency control: a different lineage is a different state, a lower serial a stale copy ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#state-as-a-data-model)).
- **Linear strategy:** Ansible's default strategy, which runs each task on all hosts in a play before starting the next task ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#how-an-ansible-run-works)).
- **Local mode:** running Chef Infra Client with `-z` against an in-process chef-zero server backed by the local repository ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#chef-solo-and-local-mode)).
- **Managed nodes:** the servers and devices an Ansible control node targets, which normally don't have Ansible installed ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#how-an-ansible-run-works)).
- **Managed resource:** HCP Terraform's billing unit: a resource with `mode = "managed"` in HCP-managed state, counted from its first plan or apply and billed hourly ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#the-plan-and-apply-platforms)).
- **Management group:** Azure's scope above subscriptions, up to six levels deep, where policy assignments attach and are inherited ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Mandatory (run task):** the run task enforcement level whose failure blocks the run, against advisory; the most restrictive wins when several apply ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#policy-as-code-at-plan-time)).
- **Master (Salt):** the machine running `salt-master` that orchestrates minions over the event bus ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#how-salt-works)).
- **Masterfiles Policy Framework (MPF):** the default policy CFEngine ships, which schedules an agent run every five minutes with a splay ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#how-cfengine-works)).
- **Minion:** any system or device managed by Salt, usually running `salt-minion` ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#how-salt-works)).
- **Module (Ansible):** the code or binaries Ansible copies to and executes on each managed node for a task ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#how-an-ansible-run-works)).
- **Moved (Terraform):** a `moved` block that rewrites a resource's state address before planning, so a rename doesn't destroy and recreate the object ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#import-moved-and-removed)).
- **No-code module:** a private-registry module version marked No-code Ready, from which a user creates a workspace and starts its first run without writing Terraform ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#hashicorp-s-governance-layer)).
- **Node object:** the Chef server's record of a node: its run-list, environment, `normal` attributes and last Ohai data ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#roles-environments-and-policyfiles)).
- **Noop:** Puppet's mode that partially simulates a run, reporting what each resource would change without touching anything ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#the-resource-abstraction-layer)).
- **Ohai:** Chef's system profiler, which supplies node data such as platform, kernel, network, memory and cloud metadata at the start of a run ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#the-chef-run)).
- **Open Policy Agent (OPA):** a CNCF policy engine that evaluates Rego policies against JSON input, such as a Terraform plan ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#policy-as-code-at-plan-time)).
- **OpenBao:** the community fork of the last MPL-licensed Vault: a secrets manager with encrypted key-value storage, dynamic secrets, encryption as a service, leases and revocation ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#secrets-out-of-code-out-of-state)).
- **OpenTofu:** the MPL-2.0 fork of Terraform under the CNCF, with the same backends and client-side state encryption ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **OpenVox:** a community soft fork of Puppet by Vox Pupuli, with the same model under Apache 2.0 ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Organization Policy (Google Cloud):** constraints on what configurations are allowed, managed or custom in CEL, in active or dry-run mode, inherited down the hierarchy and not retroactive ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#organization-policy)).
- **Owner (team reference):** ownership recorded as a reference to a team that exists, assigned at vending and inherited down, with owner tags generated from it rather than typed ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#tags-and-ownership)).
- **Packer:** HashiCorp's tool for building machine images from a template across platforms, whose core is under the BSL and whose plugins are MPL ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#golden-images-and-immutable-servers)).
- **Partner provider:** a Terraform provider written and maintained by the company whose API it wraps, in the partner's own registry namespace ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#providers-the-registry-and-who-maintains-what)).
- **Pillar:** Salt data compiled on the master and mapped to minions by a top file, readable only by the minion it is targeted to ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#how-salt-works)).
- **Plan (Terraform):** the graph walk with every provider asked what it would do, listing each resource change with its address and actions for review before apply ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#how-terraform-works-core-providers-and-the-graph)).
- **Platform and workload landing zones:** Azure's split: one platform landing zone per Entra tenant for governance, security and shared networking, and one workload landing zone per workload ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#landing-zones-and-account-vending)).
- **Platforms (Test Kitchen):** the operating systems a Test Kitchen suite is tested on ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#testing-chefspec-test-kitchen-and-inspec)).
- **Playbook:** an Ansible YAML file of plays, each mapping a set of hosts to an ordered list of tasks ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#how-an-ansible-run-works)).
- **Plays:** the units of an Ansible playbook, each mapping a set of hosts to an ordered list of tasks ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#how-an-ansible-run-works)).
- **Policy group:** a set of Chef nodes that share the same run-list and cookbooks, to which a Policyfile lock is pushed ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#roles-environments-and-policyfiles)).
- **Policy set:** a collection of policies HCP Terraform checks against every run's plan, scoped to the organization, projects, workspaces, Stacks or tagged workspaces ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#hashicorp-s-governance-layer)).
- **`Policyfile.lock.json`:** the Policyfile lock recording every cookbook's version, content identifier and source, the attributes, and the policy's `revision_id` ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#roles-environments-and-policyfiles)).
- **Policyfiles:** Chef's answer to roles, environments and Berkshelf: a policy resolved into a lock and promoted unchanged from policy group to policy group ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#roles-environments-and-policyfiles)).
- **Post-processor:** a Packer step that runs on the result after the build and provisioners: compress, checksum, import or record it ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#how-packer-works)).
- **Precedence (Chef attributes):** how Chef resolves a key set by several sources: six types and fifteen levels applied from lowest to highest, with the last one winning ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#attributes-and-precedence)).
- **Preventive control:** a guardrail that refuses the action at request time, such as an SCP or RCP, and needs nothing stored ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#guardrails-by-layer)).
- **Proactive control:** a guardrail that checks a resource before it is provisioned and refuses it, such as a CloudFormation hook ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#guardrails-by-layer)).
- **Profile (InSpec):** a reusable, versioned artifact that organizes multiple InSpec controls, with dependencies ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#testing-chefspec-test-kitchen-and-inspec)).
- **Progress:** the company that acquired Chef Software and now owns Chef's products ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#from-opscode-to-progress)).
- **Promise:** CFEngine's basic statement, made by a promiser (the object it affects) optionally to promisees (the stakeholders) ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#how-cfengine-works)).
- **Promisees:** the stakeholders a CFEngine promise is made to ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#how-cfengine-works)).
- **Promiser:** the object a CFEngine promise affects, such as a file or a port ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#how-cfengine-works)).
- **Provisioner (Packer):** software that installs and configures the running machine before Packer captures it as an image ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#how-packer-works)).
- **Provisioner (Test Kitchen):** the component that applies configuration to a Test Kitchen machine, such as the Chef provisioner resolving cookbooks with Policyfiles or Berkshelf ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#testing-chefspec-test-kitchen-and-inspec)).
- **Proxy minion:** a Salt minion process that manages a device unable to run a minion itself ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#how-salt-works)).
- **Pull agent and run interval:** a daemon on each machine that fetches its desired state and applies it on a timer, so drift is corrected automatically every interval ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#the-convergence-vocabulary)).
- **Pulumi:** infrastructure as code in general-purpose languages, with state in Pulumi Cloud by default or in a customer-chosen backend, and secrets in state encrypted per stack ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Pulumi Cloud:** Pulumi's hosted state, deployments and secrets (ESC), billed in credits drawn by resource-hours ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Puppet:** pull-based configuration management in which the agent sends facts, the server compiles a catalog and the agent applies it on an interval ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Puppet Forge:** the public site where Puppet modules are shared and installed from ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#hiera-modules-and-the-forge)).
- **PuppetDB:** the store of the latest facts, catalog and optionally reports for every Puppet node, which also enables exported resources ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#puppetdb-and-exported-resources)).
- **Puppetfile:** the file that pins Puppet modules for sites deploying code with r10k or Code Manager ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#hiera-modules-and-the-forge)).
- **Reactor:** the Salt master component that maps event tags to reactor SLS files, so an event can trigger a function on minions or the master ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#reactor-and-beacons)).
- **Refresh (Puppet):** a resource reacting when a resource it `subscribe`s to or is `notify`'d by changes, the classic case being a service restarting when its config file changes ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#the-puppet-run)).
- **Refresh (Terraform):** a plan asking each provider to read the real object and compare it with the cached attributes in state ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#state-as-a-data-model)).
- **Remediation task:** Azure Policy's job that applies `modify` or `deployIfNotExists` to existing resources, running as the assignment's managed identity ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#organization-policy)).
- **Removed (Terraform):** a `removed` block that drops an object from state while leaving it running, the declarative way to stop managing something ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#import-moved-and-removed)).
- **Report (convergence):** what a run did per resource (previous and new values, success or failure, timing), the evidence of convergence ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#the-convergence-vocabulary)).
- **Required tags:** AWS tag policy keys marked mandatory for resource creation (`report_required_tag_for`, November 20, 2025), enforced in CloudFormation by a managed hook in warn or fail mode ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#tags-and-ownership)).
- **Resource Abstraction Layer (RAL):** Puppet's split of each resource into a type that names it and its properties, and providers that implement it for one platform ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#the-resource-abstraction-layer)).
- **Resource address:** the key of each object in Terraform state (module path, mode, type, name and index), which `moved` blocks rewrite ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#state-as-a-data-model)).
- **Resource catalog:** the list of what exists, who owns it and how fresh that knowledge is, joined from IaC state, the cloud inventory and the software catalog; its most useful output is where they disagree ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#the-resource-catalog)).
- **Resource control policy (RCP):** an AWS Organizations policy, since November 13, 2024, that sets the maximum permissions on resources even for principals outside the organization and grants nothing ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#organization-policy)).
- **Resource Explorer:** AWS's organization-wide search for resources by name, tag and ID, eventually consistent and free ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#the-resource-catalog)).
- **Restricted channel:** an HCP Packer channel hidden from viewers, so a version can be tested in a channel such as `candidate` before promotion ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#hcp-packer-channels-and-revocation)).
- **Revision ID (`revision_id`):** the identifier of a Chef Policyfile lock, so a node's configuration is an artifact with an id that can be promoted and rolled back ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#roles-environments-and-policyfiles)).
- **Revocation (images):** marking an image version unusable, immediately or on a schedule, so no new builds or launches use it, cascading to descendants ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Role (Ansible):** a limited distribution of reusable Ansible content (tasks, handlers, variables, templates, files) imported into a play ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#variables-roles-and-collections)).
- **Role (Chef):** a job function, with a run-list plus default and override attributes; a node can have several ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#roles-environments-and-policyfiles)).
- **Roster:** the Salt SSH file listing target machines, `/etc/salt/roster` by default ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#how-salt-works)).
- **Rulebook:** the unit of Event-driven Ansible, a set of rulesets each with event sources and rules ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#awx-the-platform-and-event-driven-ansible)).
- **Rules (Event-driven Ansible):** the parts of a ruleset whose conditions are evaluated against incoming events and which run an action on a match ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#awx-the-platform-and-event-driven-ansible)).
- **Rulesets:** groups within an Event-driven Ansible rulebook, each with one or more event sources and rules ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#awx-the-platform-and-event-driven-ansible)).
- **Run tasks:** HCP Terraform's integration point that calls an external service at pre-plan, post-plan, pre-apply or post-apply and takes its pass or fail ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#policy-as-code-at-plan-time)).
- **Run-list:** the ordered list of recipes, named `cookbook::recipe`, that a Chef node converges ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#cookbooks-berkshelf-and-supermarket)).
- **Salt:** configuration management with a master and minions on an event bus, and agentless operation over SSH ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Salt SSH:** running Salt commands and states over SSH without installing a minion, by copying a thin Salt tarball to the target; substantially slower than minions ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#how-salt-works)).
- **SCARLETEEL:** an attack in which credentials taken from a cluster node's metadata service read a `terraform.tfstate` holding a clear-text access key for a second account, the case for keeping state locked down and secret-free ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#when-state-and-signing-keys-leak)).
- **Scripts against a panel API:** imperative calls to a hosting panel's API (such as cPanel's WHM API), with no plan, no state, no graph and invisible drift ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#before-plans-scripts-against-a-panel-api)).
- **Sentinel:** HashiCorp's policy language in HCP Terraform and Terraform Enterprise, with advisory, soft mandatory and hard mandatory enforcement levels ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#policy-as-code-at-plan-time)).
- **Service catalog:** a list of what may be created, such as AWS Service Catalog portfolios of CloudFormation or Terraform products, or HCP Terraform no-code modules ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#the-resource-catalog)).
- **Service control policy (SCP):** an AWS Organizations policy that limits what principals in member accounts can do, with a deny at any level of the tree winning ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#organization-policy)).
- **Skills (Chef 360):** agents that act on a node in Chef 360, some packaged as Habitat packages ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#habitat-and-automate)).
- **Soft mandatory:** a Sentinel enforcement level that must pass unless an authorized operator overrides it, which records who overrode a failed policy ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#policy-as-code-at-plan-time)).
- **Software catalog:** a list of what runs and who owns it, with relations such as `ownedBy` and `dependsOn` connecting services to their resources and teams ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#the-resource-catalog)).
- **SOPS:** a tool that encrypts values inside YAML, JSON, ENV, INI and binary files with a KMS, age or PGP, so an encrypted file can live in the repository ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#secrets-out-of-code-out-of-state)).
- **Spacelift:** a hosted orchestration platform for OpenTofu, Terraform, Terragrunt, Pulumi and Kubernetes, with private workers in the customer's own cloud ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Splay:** a per-host delay added to a pull agent's run so a fleet doesn't hit the server in the same second ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#the-convergence-vocabulary)).
- **Stacks:** an HCP Terraform layer above workspaces that declares several components and their deployments across environments or regions in one configuration ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#workspaces-and-stacks)).
- **State encryption:** OpenTofu's client-side encryption of state and plan files before they reach any backend, its clearest divergence from Terraform ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#opentofu)).
- **States (Salt):** Salt's desired state, written in SLS files (YAML with Jinja) with requisites such as `require` and `watch` ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#how-salt-works)).
- **Static checks:** credential-free checks against configuration (`validate`, formatting, policy scanners) that cost nothing to run ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#testing-infrastructure-code)).
- **Steampipe:** an AGPL-3.0 CLI that exposes 150+ APIs as Postgres foreign tables queried live, with Powerpipe adding open controls ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#the-resource-catalog)).
- **Suites (Test Kitchen):** run-lists with attributes that Test Kitchen converges and verifies on each platform ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#testing-chefspec-test-kitchen-and-inspec)).
- **Supermarket:** Chef's cookbook registry, public or run privately by a company ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#cookbooks-berkshelf-and-supermarket)).
- **Supervisor (Habitat):** Habitat's runtime with deployment coordination and service discovery built in, under which Habitat packages run ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#habitat-and-automate)).
- **Tag policy:** an AWS Organizations policy that standardizes tag keys, case and allowed values; untagged resources aren't evaluated by it ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#tags-and-ownership)).
- **Tag (Salt event):** the string each event on the Salt event bus carries for filtering, alongside a dictionary of data ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#reactor-and-beacons)).
- **Tags versus labels (Google Cloud):** labels are per-resource metadata usable to filter billing; tags are separate, inherited resources usable in IAM and Organization Policy conditions ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#tags-and-ownership)).
- **Tasks (Ansible):** the ordered steps of a play, each calling a module ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#how-an-ansible-run-works)).
- **Template (Packer):** a Packer configuration, in HCL2 or legacy JSON ([Baking Images: Packer](/books/hosting-and-cloud/packer-and-images#how-packer-works)).
- **Terraform:** HashiCorp's provisioning tool: HCL configuration, providers over gRPC, customer-held state, and plan then apply, with the largest provider and module ecosystem ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#the-alternatives)).
- **Terraform policy (tfpolicy):** HashiCorp's HCL policy framework, generally available July 16, 2026, that checks actual infrastructure state as well as planned changes ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#hashicorp-s-governance-layer)).
- **Test Kitchen:** a test harness that runs infrastructure code on one or more platforms in isolation, configured by a driver, provisioner, platforms, verifier and suites ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#testing-chefspec-test-kitchen-and-inspec)).
- **tfsec:** a Terraform security scanner now folded into Trivy, which carries the same scanning engine ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#policy-as-code-at-plan-time)).
- **Top file:** Salt's `top.sls`, which maps groups of machines to the states they get, per environment ([Push & Events: Ansible and Salt](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ansible-and-salt#how-salt-works)).
- **The transaction (Puppet):** the agent converting a catalog into a dependency graph, sorting it and converging each resource; it isn't atomic, so a failure halfway leaves earlier changes ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#the-puppet-run)).
- **Trivy:** Aqua's scanner, which absorbed tfsec's Terraform scanning engine ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#policy-as-code-at-plan-time)).
- **Type (Puppet):** the part of a Puppet resource that names it and its properties, implemented per platform by providers ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#the-resource-abstraction-layer)).
- **Unchanged:** the run status for a convergence run that found every resource already correct ([Convergence: CFEngine to Puppet](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cfengine-and-puppet#the-convergence-vocabulary)).
- **Unified Mode:** a Chef custom-resource mode that compiles and converges each action block in one pass, so resources run in the order written; the default from Chef Infra Client 18 ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#the-chef-run)).
- **Unit tests (IaC):** tests against a plan or mocked providers that prove a module's logic without creating anything ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#testing-infrastructure-code)).
- **Values in the state file:** whatever a provider returns is stored in Terraform state and plan files in plain text, and `sensitive` only redacts it from output, so state must be treated as secret ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#secrets-out-of-code-out-of-state)).
- **Vault:** HashiCorp's secrets manager, moved to the BSL with the rest of HashiCorp's products, each release converting to MPL 2.0 after four years ([Testing, Policy, Secrets & IaC Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/iac-testing-policy-and-secrets#secrets-out-of-code-out-of-state)).
- **Verifier (Test Kitchen):** the tool that checks a converged Test Kitchen machine, such as InSpec, Cinc Auditor or Serverspec ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#testing-chefspec-test-kitchen-and-inspec)).
- **Waiver versus mitigated:** the two exception categories: non-compliance temporarily accepted, or the rule's intent met another way through a compensating control ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#exceptions-drift-and-evidence)).
- **Well-Architected review:** a check of a workload against a provider's architecture framework, a conversation about decisions rather than an audit, and not a guardrail ([Governance, Guardrails & the Resource Catalog](/books/hosting-and-cloud/governance-and-catalog#landing-zones-and-account-vending)).
- **Why-run mode:** Chef's dry run, which runs Chef on a system without the providers modifying it ([Chef](/books/hosting-and-cloud/chef#from-opscode-to-progress)).
- **Workspaces (Terraform CLI):** several named states for one configuration in one backend, which the docs say are not appropriate for system decomposition ([Provisioning: Terraform, OpenTofu & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/terraform-and-opentofu#workspaces-and-stacks)).

## Web Hosting

- **aaPanel:** a server panel with a free Core edition and a paid Pro edition ([Control Panel Licensing & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/control-panel-licensing#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Absorb, pass through or replace:** the three ways a host handles panel license cost: absorb it in shared plan prices, pass it through as an invoice line on VPS and dedicated, or replace the panel ([Control Panel Licensing & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/control-panel-licensing#how-panel-cost-flows-into-plan-prices)).
- **Ancestor DCV:** AutoSSL's rule that a successful domain-control check on `example.com` also covers its subdomains, such as `store.example.com` ([cPanel & WHM](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpanel-and-whm#autossl)).
- **Apache SpamAssassin:** the spam scorer in panel mail, where the customer sets a threshold for marking and an optional threshold for deletion ([Email Hosting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-hosting#how-panel-mail-works)).
- **Automattic's hosted WordPress:** WordPress.com plans, Pressable and WP Cloud, run by the WordPress project's main steward ([Managed WordPress](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-wordpress#the-alternatives)).
- **AutoSSL:** cPanel & WHM's built-in certificate automation, which issues and renews a DV certificate for every domain on every account without the customer asking, with Let's Encrypt as the default provider ([cPanel & WHM](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpanel-and-whm#autossl)).
- **Backup (cPanel):** the customer-side interface offering a full backup for download and partial backups (home directory, databases, forwarders, filters) the customer can restore; a full backup can't be restored automatically from cPanel ([Backups, Restores & Migrations](/books/hosting-and-cloud/backups-and-migrations#how-panel-backups-work)).
- **Backup Configuration:** the root-level WHM service, set once per server, that defines backup type, schedule, retention, database handling and destinations ([Backups, Restores & Migrations](/books/hosting-and-cloud/backups-and-migrations#how-panel-backups-work)).
- **Backup User Selection:** the WHM setting that excludes accounts from scheduled backups; accounts are included by default ([Backups, Restores & Migrations](/books/hosting-and-cloud/backups-and-migrations#how-panel-backups-work)).
- **Backup Wizard:** the cPanel tool that sends a full backup to the home directory or a remote FTP or SCP server ([Backups, Restores & Migrations](/books/hosting-and-cloud/backups-and-migrations#how-panel-backups-work)).
- **Bati Courtage:** an OVH ruling in which backups described as physically isolated from the VPS were stored in the same building as the server ([Backups, Restores & Migrations](/books/hosting-and-cloud/backups-and-migrations#when-backups-are-a-promise-the-ovh-rulings)).
- **Block-level snapshots:** snapshots of a server or volume by the hypervisor or storage layer: fast, crash-consistent and whole-machine rather than per account ([Backups, Restores & Migrations](/books/hosting-and-cloud/backups-and-migrations#the-alternatives)).
- **Bluepad:** an OVH ruling in which production ran in one datacenter building and backups in another, although the customer had been told both were in the same one ([Backups, Restores & Migrations](/books/hosting-and-cloud/backups-and-migrations#when-backups-are-a-promise-the-ovh-rulings)).
- **Bookings (hosting):** the value of terms sold in a period, which leads revenue because long prepaid terms are recognized over time ([How Web Hosting Makes Money](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-hosting-business#ratable-revenue)).
- **Bot classification:** excluding suspected bots from a visit count, which makes the classifier's errors billing errors and is why its version is recorded per day ([Managed WordPress](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-wordpress#visit-limits)).
- **BoxTrapper:** cPanel's challenge-response spam option, which sends a verification email to unknown senders ([Email Hosting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-hosting#how-panel-mail-works)).
- **Browser cache:** assets kept on the visitor's device, the outermost cache layer of a WordPress site ([Managed WordPress](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-wordpress#caching)).
- **Bundle (hosting):** the free items attached to a hosting plan (domain, privacy, certificate, mailboxes, backups, CDN), each with its own renewal clock and its own standalone price ([How Web Hosting Makes Money](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-hosting-business#the-bundle)).
- **CAA preflight:** AutoSSL adding a CAA record to a domain's zone before ordering a certificate, so the chosen CA is allowed to issue ([cPanel & WHM](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpanel-and-whm#autossl)).
- **Cloud Solution Provider (CSP):** Microsoft's program for reselling Microsoft 365, through distributors (indirect) or by billing customers directly (direct-bill) ([Email Hosting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-hosting#google-workspace-and-microsoft-365-resale)).
- **CloudPanel:** a free but proprietary server panel for NGINX, PHP, Node.js and Python sites, "licensed and not sold," whose terms restrict resale ([Control Panel Licensing & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/control-panel-licensing#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **cPanel & WHM:** WebPros' hosting control panel, licensed per server and priced by account count, with root, resellers, packages, feature lists and accounts ([cPanel & WHM](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpanel-and-whm#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **cPanel account:** one Unix user with a primary domain, its addon, parked and sub domains, databases, mailboxes and FTP users, created from a package ([cPanel & WHM](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpanel-and-whm#root-resellers-packages-and-accounts)).
- **Custom copy:** a WP Engine environment copy that moves files, all tables or selected tables, from the current state or a backup point ([Managed WordPress](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-wordpress#staging)).
- **Customer-held offsite copies:** exports to the customer's own bucket or a plugin's storage, which survive the host's failure or the end of the relationship ([Backups, Restores & Migrations](/books/hosting-and-cloud/backups-and-migrations#the-alternatives)).
- **CyberPanel:** a free GPL-3.0 server panel built around OpenLiteSpeed, with paid add-ons ([Control Panel Licensing & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/control-panel-licensing#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **DirectAdmin:** a hosting panel licensed per server and priced by account count, with admin, reseller and user levels ([cPanel & WHM](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpanel-and-whm#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **DKIM:** a TXT record at `selector._domainkey` holding the public key that verifies a domain's mail signatures; the host generates a key per domain, signs outbound mail and rotates ([Email Hosting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-hosting#the-dns-a-mailbox-host-owns)).
- **DMARC:** a TXT record at `_dmarc` stating the domain's policy when SPF and DKIM don't align and where to send reports ([Email Hosting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-hosting#the-dns-a-mailbox-host-owns)).
- **Dovecot:** the IMAP and POP3 server in panel mail, serving mailboxes in `mdbox` format ([Email Hosting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-hosting#how-panel-mail-works)).
- **EasyApache 4:** cPanel's tool that installs, configures, updates and validates Apache and PHP as system packages, with preconfigured profiles ([cPanel & WHM](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpanel-and-whm#easyapache-4-and-multiphp)).
- **Edge full-page cache:** an optional cache of HTML at the CDN, following `Cache-Control` and bypassed on the same paths as the page cache ([Managed WordPress](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-wordpress#caching)).
- **Email Deliverability:** the cPanel page that checks a domain's DKIM, SPF and PTR records ([Email Hosting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-hosting#the-dns-a-mailbox-host-owns)).
- **Email Routing:** the cPanel setting that decides whether the server accepts mail for a domain: Local, Backup (hold until a lower-numbered MX is available) or Remote ([Email Hosting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-hosting#how-panel-mail-works)).
- **Environments (WordPress):** at least a production and a staging copy of each site, often a development one too ([Managed WordPress](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-wordpress#the-product-shape)).
- **Exim:** the mail transfer agent cPanel & WHM includes, configured in WHM for spam rejection, DKIM verification and sender checks ([Email Hosting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-hosting#how-panel-mail-works)).
- **Feature list:** a cPanel list of which tools an account using a package can see; it controls tools, not limits ([cPanel & WHM](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpanel-and-whm#root-resellers-packages-and-accounts)).
- **Full copy:** a WP Engine environment copy that moves all files and all tables ([Managed WordPress](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-wordpress#staging)).
- **General migration plugin:** a plugin such as All-in-One WP Migration or Duplicator that moves one WordPress site from any host to any host ([Backups, Restores & Migrations](/books/hosting-and-cloud/backups-and-migrations#migrations)).
- **Google Workspace resale:** reselling Workspace as an authorized reseller with a signed reseller contract, through the Partner Sales Console and the Reseller API ([Email Hosting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-hosting#google-workspace-and-microsoft-365-resale)).
- **HestiaCP:** a free, community GPL-3.0 hosting panel based on VestaCP that installs a web, mail, DNS and database stack ([Control Panel Licensing & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/control-panel-licensing#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Intro price:** a hosting plan's headline price, which is a discount for prepaying a long term; the plan renews at a higher list price for the same term ([How Web Hosting Makes Money](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-hosting-business#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Isolation tiers:** shared versus isolated managed WordPress plans, where isolation and stronger SLAs come with the higher tiers ([Managed WordPress](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-wordpress#the-product-shape)).
- **ISPConfig:** a free BSD-licensed panel that manages multiple servers from one panel, with paid modules ([Control Panel Licensing & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/control-panel-licensing#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Live Transfer:** cPanel-to-cPanel migration that also updates A, nameserver and MX records so the cutover happens with the copy ([Backups, Restores & Migrations](/books/hosting-and-cloud/backups-and-migrations#migrations)).
- **Managed WordPress:** a WordPress-only platform with a tuned stack, environments, backups, managed updates and caching, usually priced by visits ([Managed WordPress](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-wordpress#the-alternatives)).
- **Microsoft 365 (Exchange Online):** Microsoft's mail service, which caps recipients per day and messages per minute per mailbox ([Email Hosting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-hosting#outbound-limits-and-port-25)).
- **MultiPHP:** cPanel's feature that lets several PHP versions coexist, setting a server default and each virtual host's version ([cPanel & WHM](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpanel-and-whm#easyapache-4-and-multiphp)).
