- **MX:** the DNS record naming which servers receive mail for a domain, lowest preference first ([Email Hosting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-hosting#the-dns-a-mailbox-host-owns)).
- **New Commerce Experience (NCE):** Microsoft's purchasing model for license-based subscriptions, with monthly, annual or three-year terms that can be canceled only in a short window after purchase ([Email Hosting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-hosting#google-workspace-and-microsoft-365-resale)).
- **Newfold Digital:** the hosting group behind Bluehost, HostGator, Network Solutions and other brands ([How Web Hosting Makes Money](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-hosting-business#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Object cache:** a cache of database query results and computed values for WordPress, often optional or an upsell ([Managed WordPress](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-wordpress#caching)).
- **Open-source hosting panels:** panels free per server with paid add-ons or support (Virtualmin, HestiaCP, CyberPanel and others), with no license fee ([Control Panel Licensing & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/control-panel-licensing#the-alternatives)).
- **Package (cPanel):** a named set of resource options and settings plus one feature list, owned by root or a reseller and applied to accounts ([cPanel & WHM](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpanel-and-whm#root-resellers-packages-and-accounts)).
- **Page cache:** the cache (Varnish at WP Engine) of a page's generated HTML, bypassed for admin, login, cart and checkout pages and logged-in cookies ([Managed WordPress](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-wordpress#caching)).
- **Panel transfer:** migrating whole accounts, including mail, with WHM's Transfer Tool or Live Transfer, which needs root on both ends ([Backups, Restores & Migrations](/books/hosting-and-cloud/backups-and-migrations#migrations)).
- **Panel-native backups:** per-account archives on a schedule (WHM, Plesk) to local and remote destinations, restorable per account ([Backups, Restores & Migrations](/books/hosting-and-cloud/backups-and-migrations#the-alternatives)).
- **Pantheon:** a container platform for WordPress with Dev, Test and Live environments, metered by site visits and pages served ([Managed WordPress](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-wordpress#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Partner (NOC) agreement:** how hosts buy cPanel licenses at prices below the store's and resell them to VPS and dedicated customers ([Control Panel Licensing & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/control-panel-licensing#how-account-based-licensing-works)).
- **PHP handler:** the panel setting that decides which user a PHP request runs as (CGI, DSO, suPHP, PHP-FPM, FCGId, LSAPI), and so whether one account can read another's files ([cPanel & WHM](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpanel-and-whm#easyapache-4-and-multiphp)).
- **Platform checkpoints:** managed WordPress's daily site copies in the platform's own storage, restorable from the dashboard ([Backups, Restores & Migrations](/books/hosting-and-cloud/backups-and-migrations#the-alternatives)).
- **Plesk:** WebPros' hosting panel, licensed per server in editions by domain count, organized as subscriptions and service plans per customer ([cPanel & WHM](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpanel-and-whm#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Pressable:** Automattic's managed WordPress, built on WP Cloud and metered by visits and storage ([Managed WordPress](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-wordpress#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Pro email:** a paid mailbox product with its own intro and renewal prices, bigger mailboxes and the mail moved off the web server, often white-labeled from one vendor ([Email Hosting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-hosting#pro-email)).
- **PTR:** the reverse DNS name of a sending IP, which should match the server's forward name and belongs to the host, not the customer ([Email Hosting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-hosting#the-dns-a-mailbox-host-owns)).
- **Replication instead of backup:** live copies in a second location, with low recovery time but deletions and corruption replicated too ([Backups, Restores & Migrations](/books/hosting-and-cloud/backups-and-migrations#the-alternatives)).
- **Reseller (cPanel):** a cPanel account with WHM access, created by root and given a privilege list and account limits to sell hosting under ([cPanel & WHM](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpanel-and-whm#root-resellers-packages-and-accounts)).
- **Reseller API:** Google's API for Workspace resellers, covering resellers, customers, payment plans, product SKUs and licenses ([Email Hosting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-hosting#google-workspace-and-microsoft-365-resale)).
- **Reseller privilege groups:** cPanel's grouping of reseller ACLs into Basic, Package, Global, Super and Root Access, from managing one's own accounts to complete access to the server ([cPanel & WHM](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpanel-and-whm#root-resellers-packages-and-accounts)).
- **Resource options:** the numeric limits a cPanel package sets, such as disk quota, bandwidth, mail and FTP accounts, databases and hourly outbound mail ([cPanel & WHM](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpanel-and-whm#packages-and-feature-lists)).
- **Retention rises with tenure:** a hosting customer who survives the first renewal is more likely to survive the next, so the business is designed around the first renewal ([How Web Hosting Makes Money](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-hosting-business#retention)).
- **Rocket.net:** a managed WordPress host that leaves visitors unmetered and bills storage and bandwidth ([Managed WordPress](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-wordpress#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Root (cPanel):** the server owner in cPanel & WHM, holding every account, service and setting, global packages and the limits every reseller lives under ([cPanel & WHM](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpanel-and-whm#root-resellers-packages-and-accounts)).
- **Roundcube:** the webmail in panel mail, which replaced Horde and SquirrelMail in cPanel ([Email Hosting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-hosting#how-panel-mail-works)).
- **Settings (cPanel package):** the switches a package sets, such as dedicated IP, shell access, CGI, theme and locale ([cPanel & WHM](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpanel-and-whm#packages-and-feature-lists)).
- **SPF:** a TXT record listing which servers may send as a domain, with a limit on DNS-querying terms that the host should warn about ([Email Hosting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-hosting#the-dns-a-mailbox-host-owns)).
- **Standalone price:** the regular price each bundled item still has, used to allocate revenue and to deduct from refunds ([How Web Hosting Makes Money](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-hosting-business#the-bundle)).
- **Standardized Hooks:** cPanel's mechanism to run the host's code when the panel does something, registered per hookable event with `manage_hooks` ([cPanel & WHM](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpanel-and-whm#whm-api-1-uapi-and-hooks)).
- **Suspension (cPanel):** the panel's enforcement verb, redirecting web traffic, invalidating the password and acting on incoming mail, optionally locked so resellers can't lift it ([cPanel & WHM](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpanel-and-whm#creating-and-suspending-an-account)).
- **Transfer Tool:** WHM's tool that copies cPanel accounts, packages and configurations from a source server to a destination server ([Backups, Restores & Migrations](/books/hosting-and-cloud/backups-and-migrations#migrations)).
- **Tweak Settings:** WHM's server-wide settings, including the maximum hourly emails per domain and the failed or deferred percentage before outbound mail is blocked ([Email Hosting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-hosting#how-panel-mail-works)).
- **UAPI:** cPanel's API for operations inside one cPanel account, callable from the shell or over HTTPS ([cPanel & WHM](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpanel-and-whm#whm-api-1-uapi-and-hooks)).
- **Unique IP per day:** a visit-counting rule that undercounts behind carrier-grade NAT and overcounts with IPv6 privacy addresses ([Managed WordPress](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-wordpress#visit-limits)).
- **Usage-metered plans:** hosting with an allowance and overage by visits, bandwidth or compute, so revenue tracks usage ([How Web Hosting Makes Money](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-hosting-business#the-alternatives)).
- **Virtualmin:** a panel with a free GPL edition and a Professional edition licensed by domains ([Control Panel Licensing & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/control-panel-licensing#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Visits:** the managed WordPress pricing unit, a count of visitors the customer understands and the host can map to PHP work, which has to be publicly defined and explainable ([Managed WordPress](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-wordpress#visit-limits)).
- **Webmin:** a BSD-licensed web-based system administration tool for Unix-like servers, which administers a server rather than selling hosting accounts ([Control Panel Licensing & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/control-panel-licensing#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **White-labeled pro email:** a mail platform vendor's mailboxes sold under the host's brand, as Titan powers Bluehost's and GoDaddy's ([Email Hosting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-hosting#pro-email)).
- **WHM API 1:** cPanel's API for server and reseller operations, such as `listaccts`, `createacct` and `suspendacct`, authenticated with an API token ([cPanel & WHM](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpanel-and-whm#whm-api-1-uapi-and-hooks)).
- **Wholesale and reseller:** hosting sold to resellers who set their own retail price ([How Web Hosting Makes Money](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-hosting-business#the-alternatives)).
- **WordPress.com:** Automattic's hosted WordPress, which doesn't meter visits and allows plugins from the Personal plan ([Managed WordPress](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-wordpress#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **WP Engine v. Automattic:** the dispute in which Automattic cut WP Engine off from WordPress.org and WP Engine sued, showing that a host's update channel, directory and trademark use are supplier dependencies ([Managed WordPress](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-wordpress#platform-dependency-wp-engine-v-automattic)).
- **WP Squared:** cPanel's WordPress-focused panel, licensed by WordPress site ([Control Panel Licensing & the Alternatives](/books/hosting-and-cloud/control-panel-licensing#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **WP-CLI:** the command line for WordPress, which hosts use to run backups, search-replace for pushes and migrations, cache flushes and updates at scale ([Managed WordPress](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-wordpress#updates-cron-and-wp-cli)).
- **WP-Cron:** WordPress's scheduler, which runs due tasks only on page load and so doesn't run on a site behind a page cache unless system cron triggers it ([Managed WordPress](/books/hosting-and-cloud/managed-wordpress#updates-cron-and-wp-cli)).

## Domains and DNS

- **.ai (Anguilla):** Anguilla's ccTLD, whose registration sales Identity Digital manages under an agreement with the government ([New gTLDs & ccTLDs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/new-gtlds-and-cctlds#ai-and-io)).
- **.Brand:** a TLD applied for by a company for its own brand, which can't be open to the public or run a sunrise and is a long operating commitment ([New gTLDs & ccTLDs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/new-gtlds-and-cctlds#the-alternatives)).
- **.io (British Indian Ocean Territory):** the ccTLD whose future became uncertain when the UK agreed to transfer sovereignty over the Chagos Archipelago, the example of a ccTLD's country code itself being at risk ([New gTLDs & ccTLDs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/new-gtlds-and-cctlds#ai-and-io)).
- **ACPA in rem:** an action under the US Anticybersquatting Consumer Protection Act against the domain name itself, filed where the registrar or registry is located when personal jurisdiction over the registrant can't be obtained ([Domain Disputes](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-disputes#court-orders)).
- **Actionable evidence:** information readily available to the registrar sufficient to make a reasonable determination of DNS Abuse, the point from which prompt action is measured ([DNS Abuse at the Registrar](/books/hosting-and-cloud/dns-abuse#what-the-amendments-require)).
- **Add Grace Period (AGP):** the five days after a registration during which a registrar delete credits the fee, capped per registrar per month by the AGP Limits Policy since domain tasting ([The Domain Lifecycle](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-lifecycle#grace-periods)).
- **AGP Limits Policy:** the consensus policy that bars registries from refunding Add Grace Period deletes above a monthly threshold per registrar per TLD ([The Domain Lifecycle](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-lifecycle#grace-periods)).
- **Alias record / CNAME flattening:** provider features that answer at the zone apex with addresses resolved from a target name, working around the rule that a CNAME can't sit at the apex; they aren't zone data and don't transfer ([DNS Hosting & DNSSEC](/books/hosting-and-cloud/dns-hosting-and-dnssec#authoritative-dns)).
- **Ambiguous outcome:** an EPP command with no or a malformed response, whose success must not be assumed and has to be resolved by asking the registry ([The Domain Data Model & EPP](/books/hosting-and-cloud/epp-and-domain-data#commands-and-responses)).
- **Apex CNAME problem:** a CNAME can't coexist with other data, and the zone apex must hold SOA and NS records, so `example.com` itself can't be a CNAME to a load balancer or CDN ([DNS Hosting & DNSSEC](/books/hosting-and-cloud/dns-hosting-and-dnssec#authoritative-dns)).
- **Auth code (AuthInfo):** the per-domain secret the registrant gives the gaining registrar to authorize a transfer, which RFC 9154 says should be random, set only during a transfer and stored by the registry only as a hash ([Transfers, Locks & Registrar Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/transfers-and-locks#the-transfer-flow)).
- **Auto-Renew Grace Period:** the period after expiry, when the registry has renewed the name automatically and billed the registrar, during which a registrar delete credits the renewal ([The Domain Lifecycle](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-lifecycle#grace-periods)).
- **Backorder:** an order with a service that tries to register a pending-delete name the moment it drops ([The Domain Lifecycle](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-lifecycle#drop-catching-and-the-aftermarket)).
- **Bundled DNS:** DNS given away free (Cloudflare, DigitalOcean, most registrars and hosts) as a way to win the zone for another product ([DNS Hosting & DNSSEC](/books/hosting-and-cloud/dns-hosting-and-dnssec#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **CDS and CDNSKEY:** records a signed child zone publishes to tell its parent which DS to hold, so the parent can scan and update the DS automatically ([DNS Hosting & DNSSEC](/books/hosting-and-cloud/dns-hosting-and-dnssec#dnssec-operations)).
- **Change of Registrant:** Part II of the Transfer Policy: a material change to a registrant's name, organization or email needs confirmation from both the prior and new registrant ([Transfers, Locks & Registrar Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/transfers-and-locks#change-of-registrant)).
- **Change of Registrant lock:** the 60-day inter-registrar transfer lock imposed after a change of registrant unless the registrant opted out ([Transfers, Locks & Registrar Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/transfers-and-locks#change-of-registrant)).
- **Claims period:** at least the first 90 days of general registration, when anyone registering a name matching a Trademark Clearinghouse mark gets a claims notice and the mark holder is told if they proceed ([New gTLDs & ccTLDs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/new-gtlds-and-cctlds#launch-phases)).
- **`clientHold`:** the EPP status a registrar adds so the registry stops publishing the name in the DNS, its suspension lever for non-payment and abuse; nothing below the name resolves ([DNS Abuse at the Registrar](/books/hosting-and-cloud/dns-abuse#from-intake-to-mitigation)).
- **Cloudflare Registrar:** Cloudflare's retail registrar for its own customers, selling names at cost, at or below what registries and ICANN charge ([ICANN, Registries & Registrars](/books/hosting-and-cloud/icann-registries-and-registrars#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Consensus policies:** ICANN policies that bind registries and registrars through their contracts, such as the Transfer Policy, the ERRP, the AGP Limits Policy, the UDRP and the Registration Data Policy ([ICANN, Registries & Registrars](/books/hosting-and-cloud/icann-registries-and-registrars#the-contracts)).
- **Contact object:** the EPP object (RFC 5733) for a person or organization, with a client-chosen ID, postal info, phone, email and an `authInfo` ([The Domain Data Model & EPP](/books/hosting-and-cloud/epp-and-domain-data#the-objects)).
- **Contention (gTLD round):** applicants for the same string in one set, resolved in the 2026 round only by ICANN's auction or Community Priority Evaluation, with private auctions barred ([New gTLDs & ccTLDs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/new-gtlds-and-cctlds#the-2026-round)).
- **DNS Abuse:** in the amended RAA and registry agreement, malware, botnets, phishing, pharming, and spam when it delivers one of the others ([DNS Abuse at the Registrar](/books/hosting-and-cloud/dns-abuse#what-the-amendments-require)).
- **DNS failover:** answering with a secondary when the primary's health check fails, bounded by caching because resolvers keep the cached answer until its TTL expires ([DNS Hosting & DNSSEC](/books/hosting-and-cloud/dns-hosting-and-dnssec#routing-policies)).
- **DNSSEC chain:** a signed zone's DNSKEY and RRSIG records, its NSEC or NSEC3 denial records, and the DS at the parent that links its key into the chain of trust ([DNS Hosting & DNSSEC](/books/hosting-and-cloud/dns-hosting-and-dnssec#dnssec-operations)).
- **Domain object:** the EPP object (RFC 5731) for a registered name, whose attributes a registrar mirrors into its own tables ([The Domain Data Model & EPP](/books/hosting-and-cloud/epp-and-domain-data#the-objects)).
- **Domain tasting:** registering names en masse, keeping the few that earned advertising revenue within the Add Grace Period and deleting the rest for a refund ([The Domain Lifecycle](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-lifecycle#how-it-got-here)).
- **Drop catching:** registering a name the instant the registry purges it, where the first create wins, which drop catchers pursue by multiplying accreditations to multiply connections ([The Domain Lifecycle](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-lifecycle#drop-catching-and-the-aftermarket)).
- **DS record:** the hash of a zone's key-signing key published at the parent, sent by the registrar to the registry over EPP's secDNS extension; a stale DS after a provider move is the most common DNSSEC outage ([DNS Hosting & DNSSEC](/books/hosting-and-cloud/dns-hosting-and-dnssec#dnssec-operations)).
- **EPDP:** the expedited policy development process ICANN ran to replace the Temporary Specification, whose Phase 1 became the basis of the Registration Data Policy ([Registration Data & RDAP](/books/hosting-and-cloud/registration-data-and-rdap#how-it-got-here)).
- **EPP:** the Extensible Provisioning Protocol, the registry-registrar protocol (RFC 5730 and its object mappings) over TLS with mutual authentication ([The Domain Data Model & EPP](/books/hosting-and-cloud/epp-and-domain-data#how-it-got-here)).
- **Expired name auction:** a registrar selling a name it still sponsors during the auto-renew grace period, which should wait until the standard renewal window closes ([The Domain Lifecycle](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-lifecycle#drop-catching-and-the-aftermarket)).
- **Expired Registration Recovery Policy (ERRP):** the consensus policy governing registrars between expiry and delete: notices before and after expiry, DNS interruption, and redemption ([The Domain Lifecycle](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-lifecycle#expiry-notices-and-dns-interruption)).
- **External and internal hosts:** EPP host objects outside the registry's namespace (external) or subordinate to a domain in the registry (internal), whose superordinate domain must be known to the registry ([The Domain Data Model & EPP](/books/hosting-and-cloud/epp-and-domain-data#the-objects)).
- **Form of Authorization (FOA):** the notice the losing registrar must send the registrant within 24 hours of a transfer request; the gaining registrar's FOA has gone unenforced since redaction hid the registrant's email ([Transfers, Locks & Registrar Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/transfers-and-locks#the-transfer-flow)).
- **Host object:** the EPP object (RFC 5732) for a nameserver, with a name, a ROID, statuses and any IP addresses ([The Domain Data Model & EPP](/books/hosting-and-cloud/epp-and-domain-data#the-objects)).
- **ICANN accreditation fee:** the fixed yearly fee each accredited registrar pays ICANN per accreditation ([ICANN, Registries & Registrars](/books/hosting-and-cloud/icann-registries-and-registrars#the-fee-stack)).
- **ICANN transaction fee:** the per-transaction-year fee a registrar pays ICANN on each new registration, renewal and transfer ([ICANN, Registries & Registrars](/books/hosting-and-cloud/icann-registries-and-registrars#the-fee-stack)).
- **ICANN variable shared fee:** a fixed yearly sum divided among all registrars, with forgiveness for qualified registrars ([ICANN, Registries & Registrars](/books/hosting-and-cloud/icann-registries-and-registrars#the-fee-stack)).
- **KSK, ZSK and CSK:** DNSSEC keys: the key-signing key signs only the DNSKEY set and its hash is the DS at the parent, the zone-signing key signs everything else and rolls without the parent, and a combined signing key does both ([DNS Hosting & DNSSEC](/books/hosting-and-cloud/dns-hosting-and-dnssec#dnssec-operations)).
- **Launch phases:** the sequence a new gTLD opens in: sunrise for trademark holders, claims notices, then general availability, often after an early-access period ([New gTLDs & ccTLDs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/new-gtlds-and-cctlds#launch-phases)).
- **Lock (UDRP):** the state a registrar must apply within two business days of a provider's verification request, blocking any change of registrant or registrar while the proceeding runs ([Domain Disputes](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-disputes#how-it-got-here)).
- **Lock (URS):** the registry's lock within 24 hours of a URS complaint passing review, blocking all changes to the registration data, including transfer and deletion ([Domain Disputes](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-disputes#the-urs)).
- **Metered DNS:** DNS priced by a small per-zone fee and a per-million query price, with premium query types for routing (Route 53, Google, Azure) ([DNS Hosting & DNSSEC](/books/hosting-and-cloud/dns-hosting-and-dnssec#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Name-server reset:** a registrar replacing a domain's host objects with its own so resolution moves to servers it controls, to serve a notice page or sinkhole ([DNS Abuse at the Registrar](/books/hosting-and-cloud/dns-abuse#from-intake-to-mitigation)).
- **NetBeacon:** a free, centralized abuse reporting service from the DNS Abuse Institute that standardizes and enriches reports and routes them to registrars ([DNS Abuse at the Registrar](/books/hosting-and-cloud/dns-abuse#how-it-got-here)).
- **Notice of Breach:** ICANN Compliance's formal breach notice with a cure deadline, the step before termination of a registrar's accreditation ([ICANN, Registries & Registrars](/books/hosting-and-cloud/icann-registries-and-registrars#compliance-and-enforcement)).
- **Pending Delete Period:** the days after redemption ends without a restore, during which nothing can be done before the registry purges the name and it becomes available ([The Domain Lifecycle](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-lifecycle#grace-periods)).
- **Poll queue:** the EPP message queue through which the registry tells the registrar about things it didn't do itself, such as transfer requests and registry actions ([The Domain Data Model & EPP](/books/hosting-and-cloud/epp-and-domain-data#commands-and-responses)).
- **Premium names:** names a registry prices in higher tiers, often with premium renewals, breaking the flat registry fee ([ICANN, Registries & Registrars](/books/hosting-and-cloud/icann-registries-and-registrars#the-fee-stack)).
- **Primaries and secondaries:** running a zone on two providers, one primary and one secondary pulling the zone by AXFR or IXFR on NOTIFY, with TSIG authenticating transfers ([DNS Hosting & DNSSEC](/books/hosting-and-cloud/dns-hosting-and-dnssec#authoritative-dns)).
- **RAA (Registrar Accreditation Agreement):** the contract between ICANN and each accredited registrar, setting fees, registration agreement terms, records, abuse duties and termination grounds ([ICANN, Registries & Registrars](/books/hosting-and-cloud/icann-registries-and-registrars#the-contracts)).
- **RDAP:** the Registration Data Access Protocol, HTTP and JSON lookups of registration data found through IANA bootstrap files, which replaced WHOIS as the definitive source for gTLD data ([Registration Data & RDAP](/books/hosting-and-cloud/registration-data-and-rdap#rdap)).
- **RDAP bootstrap:** IANA's JSON registries pairing TLDs, IP prefixes and AS ranges with base RDAP URLs, matched right to left with the longest match winning ([Registration Data & RDAP](/books/hosting-and-cloud/registration-data-and-rdap#rdap)).
- **RDDS:** registration data directory services: RDAP, and WHOIS where it still runs, which must publish the domain, registrar, abuse contacts, statuses, dates and nameservers ([Registration Data & RDAP](/books/hosting-and-cloud/registration-data-and-rdap#the-registration-data-policy)).
- **Redacted member:** the RFC 9537 RDAP extension that lists each redacted field with its name, a JSONPath to it and the redaction method ([Registration Data & RDAP](/books/hosting-and-cloud/registration-data-and-rdap#rdap)).
- **Redemption Grace Period:** the period after a registrar deletes a name outside the Add Grace Period, during which only the registrar can restore it ([The Domain Lifecycle](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-lifecycle#grace-periods)).
- **Registrar lock:** `clientTransferProhibited`, usually with update and delete prohibitions, set by the registrar and removable by the account holder; it stops accidental transfers but not anyone who can log in ([Transfers, Locks & Registrar Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/transfers-and-locks#registrar-lock-and-registry-lock)).
- **Registration agreement:** the contract between the registrar and its registrant, which must carry the terms the RAA and the registry require ([ICANN, Registries & Registrars](/books/hosting-and-cloud/icann-registries-and-registrars#the-contracts)).
- **Registration Data Policy:** the consensus policy governing what registrars collect, transfer to registries, publish and redact, disclose on request and retain ([Registration Data & RDAP](/books/hosting-and-cloud/registration-data-and-rdap#the-registration-data-policy)).
- **Registration Data Request Service (RDRS):** ICANN's voluntary request router that passes disclosure requests to registrars that take part, deciding nothing and holding no data ([Registration Data & RDAP](/books/hosting-and-cloud/registration-data-and-rdap#disclosure-requests)).
- **Registry agreement:** the contract between ICANN and a registry operator, requiring every registration to go through an ICANN-accredited registrar with non-discriminatory access ([ICANN, Registries & Registrars](/books/hosting-and-cloud/icann-registries-and-registrars#the-contracts)).
- **Registry fee:** the registry's wholesale price per name-year, paid by the registrar from its payment security at the transaction ([ICANN, Registries & Registrars](/books/hosting-and-cloud/icann-registries-and-registrars#the-fee-stack)).
- **Registry lock:** the `serverTransferProhibited`, `serverUpdateProhibited` and `serverDeleteProhibited` statuses set by the registry, released only after an out-of-band check, which protect against a compromised registrar account ([Transfers, Locks & Registrar Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/transfers-and-locks#registrar-lock-and-registry-lock)).
- **Registry Registrar Protocol (RRP):** EPP's predecessor, a text protocol for the thin .com registry ([ICANN, Registries & Registrars](/books/hosting-and-cloud/icann-registries-and-registrars#how-it-got-here)).
- **Registry service provider (RSP):** the company that operates a TLD's technical back end for the brand on the contract, a separate business from the registry ([New gTLDs & ccTLDs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/new-gtlds-and-cctlds#registry-economics)).
- **Registry-Registrar Agreement (RRA):** the contract between a registry and each registrar covering the protocol, billing and the payment security that sets the registrar's credit ([ICANN, Registries & Registrars](/books/hosting-and-cloud/icann-registries-and-registrars#the-contracts)).
- **Renew/Extend Grace Period:** the days after an explicit renewal during which a registrar delete credits the renewal fee ([The Domain Lifecycle](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-lifecycle#grace-periods)).
- **Reseller (registrar):** a seller of domain names under a wholesale registrar's accreditation, who pays no ICANN fees while the registrar answers to ICANN for it ([ICANN, Registries & Registrars](/books/hosting-and-cloud/icann-registries-and-registrars#the-alternatives)).
- **Restore:** recovering a name from redemption in two EPP steps, a restore request and then a restore report with the registration data before and after ([The Domain Lifecycle](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-lifecycle#redemption-and-pending-delete)).
- **Result codes (EPP):** four-digit EPP response codes whose first digit is success (1) or failure (2), which a registrar's code must branch on rather than on messages ([The Domain Data Model & EPP](/books/hosting-and-cloud/epp-and-domain-data#commands-and-responses)).
- **Routing policies:** DNS answer strategies such as simple, weighted, latency, geolocation, geoproximity, IP-based, failover and multivalue, priced as premium query types ([DNS Hosting & DNSSEC](/books/hosting-and-cloud/dns-hosting-and-dnssec#routing-policies)).
- **SAC115:** the Security and Stability Advisory Committee report proposing an interoperable approach to abuse handling, whose definition of DNS Abuse the contracts adopted ([DNS Abuse at the Registrar](/books/hosting-and-cloud/dns-abuse#how-it-got-here)).
- **SC-085v2:** the CA/Browser Forum ballot requiring CAs to validate DNSSEC, when present, for CAA and domain validation lookups, so a broken signed zone fails certificate issuance ([DNS Hosting & DNSSEC](/books/hosting-and-cloud/dns-hosting-and-dnssec#how-it-got-here)).
- **Sessions (EPP):** EPP over TCP port 700 with TLS and mutual authentication, the registrar presenting a client certificate, under per-registry session limits ([The Domain Data Model & EPP](/books/hosting-and-cloud/epp-and-domain-data#registry-integration)).
- **Signed Mark Data (SMD):** the file from the Trademark Clearinghouse that a trademark holder needs to register during sunrise ([New gTLDs & ccTLDs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/new-gtlds-and-cctlds#launch-phases)).
- **SOA record:** the record at a zone's apex naming the primary server, contact, serial and the refresh, retry, expire and minimum timers ([DNS Hosting & DNSSEC](/books/hosting-and-cloud/dns-hosting-and-dnssec#authoritative-dns)).
- **Sunrise:** the launch phase restricted to trademark holders with a Signed Mark Data file, before general registration opens ([New gTLDs & ccTLDs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/new-gtlds-and-cctlds#launch-phases)).
- **TDRP (Transfer Dispute Resolution Policy):** the dispute policy for transfers, which only the gaining or losing registrar can file, with remedies that approve or reverse the transfer ([Domain Disputes](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-disputes#transfer-disputes)).
- **Temporary Specification:** the ICANN Board's 2018 interim rule that redacted personal data from public registration data for the GDPR, later replaced by policy from the EPDP ([Registration Data & RDAP](/books/hosting-and-cloud/registration-data-and-rdap#how-it-got-here)).
- **Transaction identifiers (EPP):** the client's `<clTRID>` and the server's `<svTRID>`, which together identify an EPP transaction and should both be logged and retained ([The Domain Data Model & EPP](/books/hosting-and-cloud/epp-and-domain-data#commands-and-responses)).
- **Transfer Authorization Code (TAC):** the Transfer Policy Review's name for the auth code: generated only on request, one-time use, valid for a fixed life and announced to the registrant when issued ([Transfers, Locks & Registrar Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/transfers-and-locks#the-transfer-policy-review)).
- **Transfer Grace Period:** the days after a completed transfer during which a registrar delete credits the transfer fee ([The Domain Lifecycle](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-lifecycle#grace-periods)).
- **Transfer Pending Period:** the days after a transfer request during which the losing registrar may approve or deny; if it does neither, the registry approves automatically ([The Domain Lifecycle](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-lifecycle#grace-periods)).
- **Transfer Policy:** ICANN's consensus policy for inter-registrar transfers and changes of registrant, which gives registrants portability under fixed notice and approval clocks ([Transfers, Locks & Registrar Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/transfers-and-locks#how-it-got-here)).
- **Transfer Policy Review:** the GNSO review whose recommendations, adopted by ICANN's Board, rename and tighten the auth code, drop the gaining FOA and rename the losing FOA a Transfer Confirmation ([Transfers, Locks & Registrar Security](/books/hosting-and-cloud/transfers-and-locks#the-transfer-policy-review)).
- **TTL:** the time a resolver may cache a DNS record; a short TTL makes changes and failover fast and costs more queries ([DNS Hosting & DNSSEC](/books/hosting-and-cloud/dns-hosting-and-dnssec#authoritative-dns)).
- **UDRP:** ICANN's Uniform Domain-Name Dispute-Resolution Policy, a mandatory administrative proceeding in which a trademark owner can win cancellation or transfer of a name, nothing else ([Domain Disputes](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-disputes#what-the-registrar-must-do)).
- **UDRP elements:** the three things a complainant must prove: the name is identical or confusingly similar to its mark, the registrant has no rights or legitimate interests, and the name was registered and used in bad faith ([Domain Disputes](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-disputes#the-udrp)).
- **UDRP freeze:** the bar on transferring a disputed name to another holder or registrar while a UDRP is pending and for a period after it concludes ([Domain Disputes](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-disputes#the-udrp)).
- **UDRP providers:** the approved dispute providers (ADNDRC, CIIDRC, the Czech Arbitration Court, Forum and WIPO) with which UDRP complaints are filed ([Domain Disputes](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-disputes#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **UDRP wait:** the ten business days a registrar waits after a decision ordering transfer or cancellation, implementing it unless the registrant shows a file-stamped court complaint ([Domain Disputes](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-disputes#the-udrp)).
- **URS:** the Uniform Rapid Suspension System, a faster, cheaper route for clear-cut infringement in new gTLDs, decided on clear and convincing evidence, with suspension as the only remedy ([Domain Disputes](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-disputes#what-the-registrar-must-do)).
- **URS remedy:** the registry suspending the name for the rest of its registration period and pointing its nameservers at the provider's informational page ([Domain Disputes](/books/hosting-and-cloud/domain-disputes#the-urs)).
- **WHOIS:** the original public directory of registration data over TCP port 43, whose contractual obligation for gTLDs ended when RDAP became the definitive source ([Registration Data & RDAP](/books/hosting-and-cloud/registration-data-and-rdap#how-it-got-here)).
- **Zone (DNS):** a contiguous part of the DNS namespace served from one set of name servers, with a single SOA and NS records at its apex ([DNS Hosting & DNSSEC](/books/hosting-and-cloud/dns-hosting-and-dnssec#authoritative-dns)).

## Certificates

- **24-hour and 5-day revocation deadlines:** the BR deadlines for revoking a certificate: 24 hours for a subscriber request, key compromise or unreliable validation, 5 days for misuse, changed information or non-compliant issuance ([Shorter Lifetimes, Revocation & Distrust](/books/hosting-and-cloud/certificate-lifetimes-and-distrust#revocation)).
- **Account and method pinning (CAA):** the RFC 8657 CAA parameters `accounturi`, which limits issuance to one ACME account, and `validationmethods`, which limits the validation methods allowed ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#caa-the-domain-s-say)).
- **ACME:** the protocol a client uses to get certificates from a CA automatically: a directory, an account, an order with authorizations and challenges, then finalize and download ([ACME & Certificates at Hosting Scale](/books/hosting-and-cloud/acme-at-scale#how-acme-works)).
- **ACME Renewal Information (ARI):** RFC 9773's mechanism that moves the decision of when to renew from the client to the CA, which returns a suggested renewal window per certificate ([ACME & Certificates at Hosting Scale](/books/hosting-and-cloud/acme-at-scale#renewal-information)).
- **Agreed-Upon Change to Website:** the BR validation methods that check a file on the website, under `/.well-known/pki-validation` or through ACME's `http-01` ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#validation-proving-control-of-a-name)).
- **Amazon Trust Services:** the CA behind AWS Certificate Manager, issuing free DV certificates for use on AWS services ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **AWS Certificate Manager (ACM):** AWS's managed certificates from Amazon Trust Services, validated by DNS (with automatic renewal), email or HTTP through CloudFront, usable on AWS services ([ACME & Certificates at Hosting Scale](/books/hosting-and-cloud/acme-at-scale#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Baseline Requirements (BRs):** the CA/Browser Forum's shared floor of rules for publicly trusted TLS certificates, covering validation, lifetimes and revocation ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#root-programs-and-the-ca-b-forum)).
- **CA distrust:** a root program ceasing to trust a CA's roots, usually from a cutoff date keyed on the earliest SCT, so existing certificates keep working until expiry and renewals become the emergency ([Shorter Lifetimes, Revocation & Distrust](/books/hosting-and-cloud/certificate-lifetimes-and-distrust#ca-distrust)).
- **`ca_distrust_events`:** the table with one row per root program distrust decision (roots covered, basis, cutoff, effective date, source), with successor rows when a date moves ([Shorter Lifetimes, Revocation & Distrust](/books/hosting-and-cloud/certificate-lifetimes-and-distrust#a-host-s-migration-playbook)).
- **CA/Browser Forum:** the body in which issuers and browsers vote together on the TLS Baseline Requirements ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#root-programs-and-the-ca-b-forum)).
- **CAA:** a DNS record naming the CAs allowed to issue for a domain, with `issue`, `issuewild` and `iodef` tags, found by climbing from the requested name to each parent ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#caa-the-domain-s-say)).
- **Caddy:** web server software many platforms embed, which obtains certificates over ACME from Let's Encrypt with ZeroSSL as fallback, including on demand at the first handshake ([ACME & Certificates at Hosting Scale](/books/hosting-and-cloud/acme-at-scale#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Certificate Transparency (CT):** public, append-only, untrusted logs of every publicly trusted certificate, which don't stop misissuance but make it impossible to hide ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#certificate-transparency)).
- **Certificates per registered domain:** the Let's Encrypt rate limit that counts every name under one registered domain together, so a platform giving customers subdomains of its own domain shares one allowance ([ACME & Certificates at Hosting Scale](/books/hosting-and-cloud/acme-at-scale#rate-limits)).
- **Challenge (ACME):** a way to satisfy an authorization, with a token and a status; the client publishes the response and tells the CA to check ([ACME & Certificates at Hosting Scale](/books/hosting-and-cloud/acme-at-scale#how-acme-works)).
- **Cloud-managed DV:** certificates a cloud or CDN issues and renews for its own services (ACM, a CDN's certificates), with no renewal work but usable only there ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#the-alternatives)).
- **Consecutive authorization failures:** the Let's Encrypt rate limit that pauses an identifier after repeated failed validations, punishing hosts that retry for domains pointed elsewhere ([ACME & Certificates at Hosting Scale](/books/hosting-and-cloud/acme-at-scale#rate-limits)).
- **CRLite:** Firefox's compact, frequently refreshed encoding of every revoked certificate, downloaded by the browser instead of fetching CRLs per connection ([Shorter Lifetimes, Revocation & Distrust](/books/hosting-and-cloud/certificate-lifetimes-and-distrust#crls-and-how-browsers-use-them)).
- **CRLs:** lists of revoked serials a CA publishes for clients to download, and the only revocation data Let's Encrypt still publishes ([Shorter Lifetimes, Revocation & Distrust](/books/hosting-and-cloud/certificate-lifetimes-and-distrust#the-alternatives)).
- **CRLSets:** Chrome's browser-pushed set of revocations, covering a small share of all revocations ([Shorter Lifetimes, Revocation & Distrust](/books/hosting-and-cloud/certificate-lifetimes-and-distrust#crls-and-how-browsers-use-them)).
- **DigiCert:** a commercial CA selling DV, OV and EV certificates, with ACME through EAB credentials created per certificate type ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Directory (ACME):** the single ACME URL that lists the others (`newNonce`, `newAccount`, `newOrder`, `revokeCert`, `keyChange`) and a `meta` object, including whether external account binding is required ([ACME & Certificates at Hosting Scale](/books/hosting-and-cloud/acme-at-scale#how-acme-works)).
- **DNS Change:** the BR validation method in which the CA checks a random value in a CNAME, TXT or CAA record, the basis of ACME's `dns-01` ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#validation-proving-control-of-a-name)).
- **DNS Labeled with Account ID:** the BR validation method for ACME's draft `dns-account-01` challenge, which can cover wildcards ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#validation-proving-control-of-a-name)).
- **DNS TXT Record with Persistent Value:** the BR validation method of a standing TXT record at `_validation-persist.<name>` naming the CA and an account ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#validation-proving-control-of-a-name)).
- **`dns-01`:** the ACME challenge answered with a TXT record at `_acme-challenge.<name>` holding a hash of the key authorization; the only challenge that covers wildcards ([ACME & Certificates at Hosting Scale](/books/hosting-and-cloud/acme-at-scale#challenge-types)).
- **`dns-persist-01`:** an ACME challenge using a standing TXT record at `_validation-persist.<name>` that names the CA and the ACME account, so validation needn't be repeated per order ([ACME & Certificates at Hosting Scale](/books/hosting-and-cloud/acme-at-scale#challenge-types)).
- **DV from a free ACME CA:** domain-validated certificates issued automatically for free, supported by every host, where one CA's outage or distrust hits every site on it ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#the-alternatives)).
- **Earliest SCT:** the timestamp Chrome keyed its Entrust distrust on, which the certificate carries and which a host can check only if it stored its certificates' SCTs ([Shorter Lifetimes, Revocation & Distrust](/books/hosting-and-cloud/certificate-lifetimes-and-distrust#ca-distrust)).
- **External Account Binding (EAB):** a key identifier and MAC key issued out of band that bind a new ACME account to a customer account the CA already has, required by commercial CAs and some free ones ([ACME & Certificates at Hosting Scale](/books/hosting-and-cloud/acme-at-scale#how-acme-works)).
- **Finalize and download:** the ACME step in which, once every authorization is valid, the client submits a CSR naming exactly the order's identifiers and downloads the certificate ([ACME & Certificates at Hosting Scale](/books/hosting-and-cloud/acme-at-scale#how-acme-works)).
- **Google Trust Services:** Google's CA, issuing free DV certificates through Google Cloud's Public CA over ACME with External Account Binding ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **`http-01`:** the ACME challenge answered by serving a file at `/.well-known/acme-challenge/<token>` over HTTP, which validates exactly the requested name and can't cover a wildcard ([ACME & Certificates at Hosting Scale](/books/hosting-and-cloud/acme-at-scale#challenge-types)).
- **Key authorization:** the string `token || '.' || base64url(Thumbprint(accountKey))` that token-based ACME challenges build their response from, binding the proof to the account ([ACME & Certificates at Hosting Scale](/books/hosting-and-cloud/acme-at-scale#challenge-types)).
- **Let's Encrypt:** ISRG's nonprofit CA issuing free DV certificates over ACME with no external account, the default CA almost everywhere ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Maximum Merge Delay:** the time within which a CT log promises, through an SCT, to include a submitted entry in its tree ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#certificate-transparency)).
- **Merkle tree (CT log):** the structure of a CT log, whose signed tree head commits to its size and root hash, with inclusion and consistency proofs showing entries are present and the log only appends ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#certificate-transparency)).
- **Monitors and auditors:** CT participants that read every new log entry to watch for certificates on names they care about, and check that logs behave honestly ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#certificate-transparency)).
- **Multi-perspective issuance corroboration:** the BR rule that a CA repeat each validation and CAA check from several remote network perspectives and issue only if enough agree, against BGP hijacks ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#multi-perspective-issuance-corroboration)).
- **OCSP:** the protocol in which a client asks the CA about one certificate's status, which leaks browsing to the CA and soft-fails when unreachable, and which Let's Encrypt has turned off ([Shorter Lifetimes, Revocation & Distrust](/books/hosting-and-cloud/certificate-lifetimes-and-distrust#the-alternatives)).
- **OCSP stapling and Must-Staple:** the server attaching a CA-signed OCSP response to the handshake, made mandatory by Must-Staple, which removes the privacy leak but has no place in defaults for CRL-only CAs ([Shorter Lifetimes, Revocation & Distrust](/books/hosting-and-cloud/certificate-lifetimes-and-distrust#the-alternatives)).
- **On-demand issuance:** obtaining a certificate at a TLS terminator during the first handshake for a name, which must be gated or it orders for any name a client sends ([ACME & Certificates at Hosting Scale](/books/hosting-and-cloud/acme-at-scale#when-dns-isn-t-pointed-yet)).
- **Order (ACME):** the ACME object created with `newOrder` for a list of identifiers, carrying status, expiry, authorizations and the finalize URL ([ACME & Certificates at Hosting Scale](/books/hosting-and-cloud/acme-at-scale#how-acme-works)).
- **OV and EV:** certificates that add vetted organization identity to domain control, putting identity in the certificate for those who check it, at the cost of manual vetting ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#the-alternatives)).
- **Per-customer SAN bundle:** one certificate covering all of a customer's names, convenient for panel accounts but one failing name blocks the renewal of all ([ACME & Certificates at Hosting Scale](/books/hosting-and-cloud/acme-at-scale#the-alternatives)).
- **Precertificate:** a certificate a CA logs before issuance, carrying a poison extension so no client accepts it, so the SCTs can be embedded in the real certificate ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#certificate-transparency)).
- **Preflight (ACME):** checking from outside that a name points at the provider and that CAA allows its CA before creating an ACME order, so customer misconfiguration costs no failure budget ([ACME & Certificates at Hosting Scale](/books/hosting-and-cloud/acme-at-scale#when-dns-isn-t-pointed-yet)).
- **Private CA:** an organization's own root, trusted only by its devices, allowing any lifetime or internal name but not trusted by browsers ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#the-alternatives)).
- **`replaces`:** the ACME order field naming the certificate a new order succeeds, which the CA checks belongs to the same account ([ACME & Certificates at Hosting Scale](/books/hosting-and-cloud/acme-at-scale#renewal-information)).
- **Root program:** a trust store with a policy attached, such as the Chrome Root Program, which enforces its rules by removing CAs ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#root-programs-and-the-ca-b-forum)).
- **SC-081:** the CA/Browser Forum ballot that set the schedule shortening certificate lifetimes and validation reuse periods in steps toward 47 days ([Shorter Lifetimes, Revocation & Distrust](/books/hosting-and-cloud/certificate-lifetimes-and-distrust#the-sc-081-schedule)).
- **Sectigo:** a commercial CA selling DV, OV and EV certificates under commercial accounts ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Short-lived certificates:** certificates that expire within days, replacing revocation because there is nothing to check ([Shorter Lifetimes, Revocation & Distrust](/books/hosting-and-cloud/certificate-lifetimes-and-distrust#the-alternatives)).
- **Signed Certificate Timestamp (SCT):** a CT log's immediate signed promise, with its log ID and a timestamp, to include a submitted certificate within its Maximum Merge Delay ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#certificate-transparency)).
- **Signed Tree Head:** a CT log's periodic signature over its tree's size and root hash ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#certificate-transparency)).
- **Temporally sharded logs:** CT logs that each accept only certificates expiring within a range of at most a year, so a log can be retired once everything in it has expired ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#static-ct-and-the-cost-of-logging)).
- **TLS Using ALPN:** the BR validation method behind ACME's `tls-alpn-01` ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#validation-proving-control-of-a-name)).
- **`tls-alpn-01`:** the ACME challenge answered in a TLS handshake on port 443 using a dedicated ALPN protocol (RFC 8737), suited to TLS-terminating proxies ([ACME & Certificates at Hosting Scale](/books/hosting-and-cloud/acme-at-scale#challenge-types)).
- **`unknown_sct`:** the classification for a certificate whose SCTs were never parsed, which can't be judged against an SCT-keyed distrust rule and is treated as a finding ([Shorter Lifetimes, Revocation & Distrust](/books/hosting-and-cloud/certificate-lifetimes-and-distrust#how-to-build-it)).
- **Validation reuse period:** how long a completed domain validation can back later certificates, shrinking in steps with certificate lifetimes ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#validation-proving-control-of-a-name)).
- **Wildcard certificate:** a certificate covering one level of subdomains, which needs a DNS validation method and so ties wildcards to running the customer's DNS ([ACME & Certificates at Hosting Scale](/books/hosting-and-cloud/acme-at-scale#the-alternatives)).
- **ZeroSSL:** a CA issuing free DV certificates over ACME with EAB credentials and paid certificates through its API ([The Web PKI & Certificate Transparency](/books/hosting-and-cloud/web-pki#how-the-providers-do-it)).

## The Edge

- **0-RTT:** TLS 1.3 early data that lets a returning client send a request in its first flight, which can be replayed, so it stays off until the origin handles replay ([TLS & Protocols at the Edge](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tls-and-protocols#http-3-and-quic)).
- **421 Misdirected Request:** the status a server returns when a request arrives on a reused connection it doesn't want to serve for that hostname ([TLS & Protocols at the Edge](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tls-and-protocols#http-2-at-the-edge)).
- **425 Too Early:** the status an origin returns for a request sent as early data that it won't risk replaying ([TLS & Protocols at the Edge](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tls-and-protocols#http-3-and-quic)).
- **AI traffic monetization:** AWS WAF's feature on CloudFront that answers a monetized resource with a 402 carrying pricing and accepted payment networks, so crawlers can pay for access ([WAF, Bots & Rate Limits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/waf-bots-and-rate-limits#ai-crawlers-and-pay-per-crawl)).
- **Akamai (Prolexic):** Akamai's DDoS scrubbing network, receiving customer traffic by routed GRE or BGP diversion, cleaning it and forwarding the rest ([DDoS Mitigation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ddos-mitigation#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Alt-Svc discovery:** how a client learns that an edge offers HTTP/3: the first TCP response advertises it in an `Alt-Svc` header, or an HTTPS DNS record announces it ([TLS & Protocols at the Edge](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tls-and-protocols#http-3-and-quic)).
- **Amazon CloudFront:** AWS's CDN, sold pay as you go, on flat-rate plans or with a savings bundle, with DNS-based steering to its POPs ([The Edge Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/edge-business#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Amplification limits (QUIC):** QUIC's rule that a server may send only a small multiple of the bytes it received before the client's address is validated, which stops it being used as a reflector ([TLS & Protocols at the Edge](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tls-and-protocols#http-3-and-quic)).
- **Anomaly scoring:** the OWASP Core Rule Set's approach in which matching rules add their severity to a running score instead of blocking, and the request is blocked only when the score crosses a threshold ([WAF, Bots & Rate Limits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/waf-bots-and-rate-limits#anomaly-scoring-and-paranoia-levels)).
- **Anycast:** every location announcing the same addresses in BGP so internet routing picks the location for each packet, which spreads attacks across locations ([Networks, Peering & IP Addresses](/books/hosting-and-cloud/networks-peering-and-ip-addresses#how-a-request-finds-a-pop)).
- **Attestation (bots):** a device vendor vouching for a client instead of the site interrogating it, as with Private Access Tokens ([WAF, Bots & Rate Limits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/waf-bots-and-rate-limits#bots-from-captcha-to-scores)).
- **Authenticated origin pulls:** mutual TLS from edge to origin, the edge presenting a client certificate so the origin can prove a request came through the edge ([TLS & Protocols at the Edge](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tls-and-protocols#edge-to-origin)).
- **AWS Shield:** AWS's DDoS protection: Standard on CloudFront, Route 53 and load balancers at no charge, Advanced per payer account with credits for attack-driven charges ([DDoS Mitigation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ddos-mitigation#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **AWS WAF:** AWS's WAF of web ACLs made of rules and rule groups (AWS Managed Rules, Marketplace sellers, the customer's own), measured in WCUs ([WAF, Bots & Rate Limits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/waf-bots-and-rate-limits#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Azure DDoS Protection:** Azure's L3/4 DDoS protection, sold per virtual network (Network Protection) or per public IP (IP Protection) ([DDoS Mitigation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ddos-mitigation#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Azure Front Door:** Microsoft's CDN and edge, sold in Standard and Premium tiers plus usage ([The Edge Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/edge-business#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Azure Front Door WAF:** Front Door's WAF of a Default Rule Set with anomaly scoring, a Bot Manager rule set and custom rules, with a Detection mode before blocking ([WAF, Bots & Rate Limits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/waf-bots-and-rate-limits#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **BCP 38:** the practice of a network dropping outgoing packets whose source address isn't one of its own prefixes, which eliminates source address spoofing at its origin ([DDoS Mitigation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ddos-mitigation#volumetric-attacks-and-reflection)).
- **BGP diversion:** a protector announcing a customer's own prefix from its network so all its traffic passes through scrubbing, always on or only during an attack ([DDoS Mitigation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ddos-mitigation#where-mitigation-happens)).
- **Bot score:** a per-request likelihood of automation computed from many signals, against which customers write rules; the scales differ by provider ([WAF, Bots & Rate Limits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/waf-bots-and-rate-limits#bots-from-captcha-to-scores)).
- **Bought IPv4:** IPv4 space transferred through an RIR and owned, which amortizes against leasing ([Networks, Peering & IP Addresses](/books/hosting-and-cloud/networks-peering-and-ip-addresses#the-alternatives)).
- **Bunny:** a low-cost CDN priced per GB by region on a standard network or flat on a volume network, with no request fees ([The Edge Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/edge-business#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Bunny (Shield):** Bunny's edge security product combining WAF, rate limiting, bot detection and DDoS protection at the CDN edge ([DDoS Mitigation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ddos-mitigation#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **BYOIP:** bringing a customer's own IP prefix to a provider, which announces it once control is proven through the RIR's records and a letter of authorization is on file ([Networks, Peering & IP Addresses](/books/hosting-and-cloud/networks-peering-and-ip-addresses#byoip)).
- **Byte hit ratio:** bytes served from cache over bytes served, the hit ratio that maps to the origin's egress bill ([Caching & Invalidation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/caching-and-invalidation#hit-ratio-and-cache-analytics)).
- **Cache key:** what makes two requests the same cached object (host, scheme, query strings, headers, cookies, device class), kept separate from what is forwarded to the origin ([Caching & Invalidation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/caching-and-invalidation#the-cache-key)).
- **Cache poisoning:** an attack that sends an input the cache doesn't key on but the origin reflects, so a harmful response is cached and served to everyone ([Caching & Invalidation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/caching-and-invalidation#what-must-never-be-shared)).
- **Cache policy:** CloudFront's object defining the cache key, paired with an origin request policy for what the origin receives ([Caching & Invalidation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/caching-and-invalidation#the-cache-key)).
- **Carpet bombing:** a flood spread across many destination addresses or ports so per-target thresholds don't trip ([DDoS Mitigation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ddos-mitigation#volumetric-attacks-and-reflection)).
- **Characteristics (rate limiting):** the key a rate-limiting rule counts by, such as IP, ASN, country, header, cookie, path, JA3/JA4 or a body field ([WAF, Bots & Rate Limits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/waf-bots-and-rate-limits#rate-limiting-at-the-edge)).
- **Cloud-attached CDN:** a CDN in front of the same cloud's origins (CloudFront, Cloud CDN, Front Door), with free or cheap origin fill ([The Edge Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/edge-business#the-alternatives)).
- **CloudFront flat-rate plans:** CloudFront plans that bundle CDN, WAF, DDoS protection, DNS and edge compute per distribution for a monthly price with request and transfer allowances ([The Edge Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/edge-business#flat-rate-plans)).
- **CloudFront Security Savings Bundle:** a one-year monthly spend commitment that discounts all CloudFront usage, including Functions and Lambda@Edge, and includes AWS WAF usage ([The Edge Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/edge-business#commitments-and-bundles)).
- **Clustering:** forwarding each object's fetch to the one server in a POP responsible for it, so request collapsing works across the whole POP ([Caching & Invalidation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/caching-and-invalidation#request-collapsing)).
- **Connection reuse (coalescing):** a client sending requests for another hostname over an existing HTTP/2 or HTTP/3 connection when the server's certificate is authoritative for it ([TLS & Protocols at the Edge](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tls-and-protocols#http-2-at-the-edge)).
- **Contract with commitment:** CDN pricing at a negotiated rate with a minimum commitment (Akamai, enterprise tiers everywhere) ([The Edge Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/edge-business#the-alternatives)).
- **Counters are local:** edge rate-limit counters are kept per data center, so a limit applies per location a client reaches, not across the network ([WAF, Bots & Rate Limits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/waf-bots-and-rate-limits#rate-limiting-at-the-edge)).
- **Credited after the fact:** the DDoS billing model in which attack-driven charges are billed and then credited on request (AWS Shield Advanced, Azure DDoS Network Protection) ([DDoS Mitigation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ddos-mitigation#attack-traffic-and-the-bill)).
- **Custom hostnames for SaaS:** an edge product that terminates TLS for a SaaS platform's customers' own domains under the platform's account, through claim, proof of control, issuance, serving and release ([TLS & Protocols at the Edge](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tls-and-protocols#custom-hostnames-for-saas)).
- **Custom rules (WAF):** the customer's own WAF rules: IP and country lists, path and header matches, size constraints, method restrictions and bot conditions ([WAF, Bots & Rate Limits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/waf-bots-and-rate-limits#managed-rulesets-and-custom-rules)).
- **Dedicated IP certificates:** one address per certificate for clients that don't send SNI, sold at a high monthly price ([TLS & Protocols at the Edge](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tls-and-protocols#the-alternatives)).
- **DNS-based steering:** the provider's authoritative DNS answering each lookup with the address of a location chosen for the resolver that asked ([Networks, Peering & IP Addresses](/books/hosting-and-cloud/networks-peering-and-ip-addresses#how-a-request-finds-a-pop)).
- **Edge assembly:** caching page fragments separately and assembling them per request at the edge (ESI or edge functions), so personal pages can still use the cache ([Caching & Invalidation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/caching-and-invalidation#the-alternatives)).
- **Edge compute:** code run at the CDN's locations, priced on requests and CPU time rather than bytes, the revenue line every edge provider is growing into ([The Edge Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/edge-business#revenue-mix-and-customer-metrics)).
- **Egress can't be anycast:** traffic an edge sends on a customer's behalf needs replies to return to the sending server, so it can't use anycast addresses ([Networks, Peering & IP Addresses](/books/hosting-and-cloud/networks-peering-and-ip-addresses#anycast)).
- **Embedded capacity:** a content network's servers placed inside an access network, so traffic never crosses an interconnect, serving only that network's customers ([Networks, Peering & IP Addresses](/books/hosting-and-cloud/networks-peering-and-ip-addresses#transit-peering-and-interconnection)).
- **Exceptions (WAF):** rules that skip a managed rule for a given path or condition, taking priority over overrides ([WAF, Bots & Rate Limits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/waf-bots-and-rate-limits#managed-rulesets-and-custom-rules)).
- **Excluded at metering:** the DDoS billing model in which attack traffic never reaches the bill because it is excluded before rating (Fastly DDoS Protection) ([DDoS Mitigation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ddos-mitigation#attack-traffic-and-the-bill)).
- **Executing and blocking paranoia levels:** CRS's separation of the level of rules that run from the level that counts toward the anomaly score, so higher-level rules can be measured before they block ([WAF, Bots & Rate Limits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/waf-bots-and-rate-limits#anomaly-scoring-and-paranoia-levels)).
- **Fastly packages:** Fastly's monthly-priced bundles with included requests, turning usage into a fixed price ([The Edge Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/edge-business#flat-rate-plans)).
- **Flat-rate plan (CDN):** a monthly price with allowances instead of metered bytes and requests (Cloudflare, CloudFront flat rate, Fastly packages), limited by a content rule rather than a meter ([The Edge Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/edge-business#the-alternatives)).
- **Google Cloud Armor:** Google's protection at its edge in front of its load balancers, automatic at L3/4 and with security policies and preconfigured WAF rules at L7 ([DDoS Mitigation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ddos-mitigation#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Google Cloud CDN:** Google's CDN behind the global external Application Load Balancer, on global anycast addresses, billed by usage ([The Edge Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/edge-business#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Google Global Cache:** Google's embedded caches, for which Google provides the hardware and the ISP provides space, power, a connection and a BGP feed ([Networks, Peering & IP Addresses](/books/hosting-and-cloud/networks-peering-and-ip-addresses#embedded-caches-in-access-networks)).
- **Hijack (routing):** a network originating a prefix it doesn't hold, redirecting traffic for someone else's addresses ([Networks, Peering & IP Addresses](/books/hosting-and-cloud/networks-peering-and-ip-addresses#route-security)).
- **Hit-for-pass:** a marker left when a response can't be shared, so later requests stop queueing behind a collapsed fetch ([Caching & Invalidation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/caching-and-invalidation#request-collapsing)).
- **HTTP/2 Rapid Reset:** an attack that opens and immediately cancels HTTP/2 streams, so the server does work for each while cancelled streams don't count against the concurrency limit ([DDoS Mitigation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ddos-mitigation#http-2-rapid-reset)).
- **Invalidation (Cloudflare):** Cloudflare's soft purge, which marks content stale so it is revalidated on the next request and reused if unchanged ([Caching & Invalidation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/caching-and-invalidation#purge-and-invalidation)).
- **Invisible challenges:** bot checks run as small JavaScript probes without a puzzle, such as Turnstile ([WAF, Bots & Rate Limits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/waf-bots-and-rate-limits#bots-from-captcha-to-scores)).
- **IoT botnets:** botnets of compromised real devices that complete handshakes, such as Mirai, which made attack sources look like ordinary clients ([DDoS Mitigation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ddos-mitigation#botnets)).
- **IPv6-first with shared IPv4:** IPv6 everywhere and IPv4 only shared or by SNI, which removes most address cost ([Networks, Peering & IP Addresses](/books/hosting-and-cloud/networks-peering-and-ip-addresses#the-alternatives)).
- **Leak (routing):** a network passing on routes it should have kept to itself ([Networks, Peering & IP Addresses](/books/hosting-and-cloud/networks-peering-and-ip-addresses#route-security)).
- **Leased IPv4:** IPv4 addresses rented from a holder, with no capital outlay but the lessor's reputation history inherited ([Networks, Peering & IP Addresses](/books/hosting-and-cloud/networks-peering-and-ip-addresses#the-alternatives)).
- **Low-cost per-GB network:** a CDN selling very low per-GB prices with no request fees (Bunny standard and volume) ([The Edge Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/edge-business#the-alternatives)).
- **Managed rulesets:** WAF rules written and updated by the provider or a third-party seller, which the customer controls only through overrides and exceptions ([WAF, Bots & Rate Limits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/waf-bots-and-rate-limits#managed-rulesets-and-custom-rules)).
- **MANRS:** a routing-security initiative supported by the Global Cyber Alliance, asking networks to take four actions starting with filtering ([Networks, Peering & IP Addresses](/books/hosting-and-cloud/networks-peering-and-ip-addresses#route-security)).
- **Match Viewer:** CloudFront's origin protocol setting under which one cached copy serves both HTTP and HTTPS requests ([Caching & Invalidation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/caching-and-invalidation#the-cache-key)).
- **Metered usage (CDN):** CDN pricing per GB by region and volume tier plus requests (CloudFront, Fastly, Cloud CDN, Front Door) ([The Edge Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/edge-business#the-alternatives)).
- **Mitigation timeout:** how long a rate-limiting rule's action lasts once it triggers ([WAF, Bots & Rate Limits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/waf-bots-and-rate-limits#rate-limiting-at-the-edge)).
- **Multi-CDN:** traffic split across two or more CDNs by a steering layer, for price leverage and resilience ([The Edge Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/edge-business#the-alternatives)).
- **Net retention:** revenue from last year's customers now over their revenue then, a metric each edge provider defines its own way ([The Edge Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/edge-business#revenue-mix-and-customer-metrics)).
- **Netflix Open Connect:** Netflix's program offering ISPs free embedded appliances in their data centers or peering at exchanges ([Networks, Peering & IP Addresses](/books/hosting-and-cloud/networks-peering-and-ip-addresses#embedded-caches-in-access-networks)).
- **Never metered:** the DDoS billing model in which attack size can't affect price because bandwidth isn't the plan's meter (Cloudflare plans) ([DDoS Mitigation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ddos-mitigation#attack-traffic-and-the-bill)).
- **Origin certificates:** certificates from a provider-run private CA for the edge-to-origin leg, valid only between the edge and the origin ([TLS & Protocols at the Edge](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tls-and-protocols#edge-to-origin)).
- **Origin fill:** the transfer from origin to edge when the cache misses, billed by some providers and free from same-cloud origins at others ([The Edge Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/edge-business#shields-fill-and-tiers)).
- **Origin offload:** one minus origin requests (or bytes) over edge requests (or bytes), the metric that maps to origin capacity ([Caching & Invalidation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/caching-and-invalidation#hit-ratio-and-cache-analytics)).
- **Origin request policy:** CloudFront's object defining what the origin receives, separate from the cache key ([Caching & Invalidation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/caching-and-invalidation#the-cache-key)).
- **Override (WAF):** the customer's control over a managed ruleset, changing an action or disabling rules at the ruleset, tag or rule level rather than editing the rules ([WAF, Bots & Rate Limits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/waf-bots-and-rate-limits#managed-rulesets-and-custom-rules)).
- **Own delivery network:** caches in rented colocation and inside access networks, the lowest-cost delivery for the largest content companies ([The Edge Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/edge-business#the-alternatives)).
- **Paranoia levels:** the OWASP CRS setting that decides which rules run, from level 1 baseline protection with few false positives to level 4 for critical assets ([WAF, Bots & Rate Limits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/waf-bots-and-rate-limits#anomaly-scoring-and-paranoia-levels)).
- **Pay per crawl:** Cloudflare's feature letting a publisher Allow, Charge or Block each crawler with a flat per-request price, Cloudflare acting as Merchant of Record ([WAF, Bots & Rate Limits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/waf-bots-and-rate-limits#ai-crawlers-and-pay-per-crawl)).
- **Peering:** two networks exchanging traffic between their own customers directly, usually settlement-free, at exchanges or over private interconnects ([Networks, Peering & IP Addresses](/books/hosting-and-cloud/networks-peering-and-ip-addresses#transit-peering-and-interconnection)).
- **PeeringDB:** the free public directory, run by volunteers, where networks publish their ASNs, peering policies, exchanges and facilities ([Networks, Peering & IP Addresses](/books/hosting-and-cloud/networks-peering-and-ip-addresses#transit-peering-and-interconnection)).
- **POP counts:** the location numbers CDNs publish, which don't compare because units, designs and capacity claims differ, so they are treated as claims ([Networks, Peering & IP Addresses](/books/hosting-and-cloud/networks-peering-and-ip-addresses#pop-counts-and-why-they-don-t-compare)).
- **Pricing region:** the region delivery is priced by, where the serving location sits, the main driver of CDN cost ([The Edge Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/edge-business#bandwidth-by-region-and-volume)).
- **Private network interconnect (PNI):** a dedicated cable between two networks' routers in the same building, used when traffic between them is large enough to fill ports ([Networks, Peering & IP Addresses](/books/hosting-and-cloud/networks-peering-and-ip-addresses#transit-peering-and-interconnection)).
- **Purge methods:** the ways an edge invalidates cached content: by URL, tag (surrogate key), prefix, hostname or everything ([Caching & Invalidation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/caching-and-invalidation#purge-and-invalidation)).
- **Reflection and amplification:** an attack sending small UDP requests with the victim's address forged as the source, so third-party servers send their larger answers to the victim ([DDoS Mitigation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ddos-mitigation#volumetric-attacks-and-reflection)).
- **Remote signing (Keyless):** running the TLS handshake's private-key operation on the customer's own key server, so the edge never holds the key ([TLS & Protocols at the Edge](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tls-and-protocols#the-alternatives)).
- **Remotely triggered blackholing (RTBH):** the attacked network announcing the target address with the BLACKHOLE community so neighbors drop all traffic to it, which protects shared links but takes the address offline ([DDoS Mitigation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ddos-mitigation#where-mitigation-happens)).
- **Request collapsing:** an edge holding concurrent requests for the same cache key while the first fetch is in flight and answering them all from one response ([Caching & Invalidation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/caching-and-invalidation#request-collapsing)).
- **Request hit ratio:** edge hits over all cacheable requests, inflated by collapsed requests and tiny objects ([Caching & Invalidation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/caching-and-invalidation#hit-ratio-and-cache-analytics)).
- **Revalidation:** replacing a full fetch with a conditional one (`If-None-Match` or `If-Modified-Since`) that the origin answers with a `304` and no body when unchanged ([Caching & Invalidation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/caching-and-invalidation#freshness-validation-and-stale-serving)).
- **Route origin validation:** routers checking each BGP announcement against validated ROAs and marking it valid, invalid or not found ([Networks, Peering & IP Addresses](/books/hosting-and-cloud/networks-peering-and-ip-addresses#route-security)).
- **RPKI and ROAs:** the resource PKI in which an address holder signs a Route Origin Authorization allowing a single AS to originate routes to its prefixes, with an optional maximum length ([Networks, Peering & IP Addresses](/books/hosting-and-cloud/networks-peering-and-ip-addresses#route-security)).
- **Scrubbing centers:** a few large locations with filtering capacity that receive a customer's traffic, clean it and forward the remainder ([DDoS Mitigation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ddos-mitigation#where-mitigation-happens)).
- **Shield (origin shield):** an upper cache tier near the origin that edge caches ask first, so the origin sees roughly one miss in total, at an extra per-request charge at some providers ([The Edge Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/edge-business#shields-fill-and-tiers)).
- **SNI:** the TLS extension that lets clients reach many virtual servers on one address, so the edge picks the certificate and per-hostname settings by name ([TLS & Protocols at the Edge](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tls-and-protocols#termination-at-the-edge)).
- **Soft versus hard purge:** a hard purge makes objects unusable so the next request goes to the origin; a soft purge marks them stale so they can still be served while revalidating or when the origin is down ([Caching & Invalidation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/caching-and-invalidation#purge-and-invalidation)).
- **Soft-unicast:** Cloudflare's egress design of one IPv4 address per data center shared by its servers through port slicing ([Networks, Peering & IP Addresses](/books/hosting-and-cloud/networks-peering-and-ip-addresses#anycast)).
- **Stale serving:** serving an expired copy for availability, with `stale-while-revalidate` refreshing it in the background and `stale-if-error` covering origin errors ([Caching & Invalidation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/caching-and-invalidation#freshness-validation-and-stale-serving)).
- **SYN cache:** a SYN-flood defense that keeps a small hashed table of half-open connections and evicts the oldest per bucket ([DDoS Mitigation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ddos-mitigation#syn-floods-and-syn-cookies)).
- **SYN cookie:** a SYN-flood defense in which the server allocates nothing for a half-open connection and encodes what it needs in the sequence number it sends back ([DDoS Mitigation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ddos-mitigation#syn-floods-and-syn-cookies)).
- **SYN flood:** an attack that sends SYNs, usually spoofed, and never completes the handshake, filling the backlog so real clients can't connect ([DDoS Mitigation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ddos-mitigation#syn-floods-and-syn-cookies)).
- **Throttle versus block:** the rate-limiting choice between allowing requests up to the limit and acting on the excess, and blocking a client entirely once it crosses the threshold ([WAF, Bots & Rate Limits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/waf-bots-and-rate-limits#rate-limiting-at-the-edge)).
