- **Tiered caches:** a second cache tier the edge caches ask before the origin, cutting origin load but making purges cover two tiers and adding inter-tier traffic to the bill ([Caching & Invalidation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/caching-and-invalidation#tiered-caches-and-shields)).
- **TLS fingerprints:** identifiers of client software from how it opens a TLS connection, such as JA3's hash of the ClientHello and JA4's sorted variant ([WAF, Bots & Rate Limits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/waf-bots-and-rate-limits#bot-scores-fingerprints-and-verified-bots)).
- **TLS terminated at the origin:** L4 pass-through in which the edge forwards TCP or QUIC without decrypting, so the edge can't cache or inspect ([TLS & Protocols at the Edge](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tls-and-protocols#the-alternatives)).
- **Transit:** paying an upstream network to carry traffic to every destination on the internet, universal and simple but billed by capacity used ([Networks, Peering & IP Addresses](/books/hosting-and-cloud/networks-peering-and-ip-addresses#transit-peering-and-interconnection)).
- **UDP flood:** a volumetric attack of UDP packets with any payload to any port, which needs no handshake and fills the target's link ([DDoS Mitigation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/ddos-mitigation#volumetric-attacks-and-reflection)).
- **`Vary`:** the origin's response header naming which request headers changed the response, so the cache keeps separate variants ([Caching & Invalidation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/caching-and-invalidation#the-cache-key)).
- **Verified bots:** the allowlist of bots transparent about who they are and what they do, verified by published IP lists, reverse DNS or Web Bot Auth ([WAF, Bots & Rate Limits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/waf-bots-and-rate-limits#bot-scores-fingerprints-and-verified-bots)).
- **Versioned URLs:** content-hashed asset names with long lifetimes that are never purged, preferred over invalidation for deploys because they also reach browser and proxy caches ([Caching & Invalidation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/caching-and-invalidation#purge-and-invalidation)).
- **Virtual patching:** a WAF rule matching an exploit's shape, shipped in a managed ruleset with a default action to buy time for the real fix, which it never replaces ([WAF, Bots & Rate Limits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/waf-bots-and-rate-limits#emergency-rules-and-virtual-patching)).
- **Volume tier:** the falling per-GB price bands within a month at most CDNs ([The Edge Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/edge-business#bandwidth-by-region-and-volume)).
- **Web Bot Auth:** a scheme in which a crawler signs each request with HTTP Message Signatures so the edge can know who it is, which charging for crawls requires ([WAF, Bots & Rate Limits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/waf-bots-and-rate-limits#ai-crawlers-and-pay-per-crawl)).
- **Web cache deception:** an attack that gets a logged-in victim to open a private page under a static-looking suffix (such as `/account/foo.css`), so the edge caches and shares the private response ([Caching & Invalidation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/caching-and-invalidation#what-must-never-be-shared)).

## Communications Platforms

- **10DLC (A2P ten-digit long code):** registration for application-to-person texting from ordinary local numbers, in which every sender registers a brand and a campaign before carriers accept its traffic ([Business Texting Registration](/books/hosting-and-cloud/texting-registration#10dlc-brands-and-campaigns)).
- **8-bit data encoding:** the SMS encoding for binary payloads, 140 octets in a single segment and 134 per concatenated segment, rare in application-to-person traffic ([Sending a Text](/books/hosting-and-cloud/sending-a-text#segments-and-encoding)).
- **911 for VoIP:** the duty of interconnected VoIP to carry emergency calls with a location dispatchers can use, which a platform meets by validating an emergency address when it is set, not when the call is made ([911, CPNI & Telecom Duties](/books/hosting-and-cloud/telecom-duties#911-for-voip)).
- **Account-level thresholds:** pausing a whole customer account when its bounce or complaint rate crosses a limit; simple, but a broadcast problem then stops password resets ([Inbox Rules](/books/hosting-and-cloud/inbox-rules#the-alternatives)).
- **Aging (phone numbers):** the rest a released number takes before returning to inventory, so calls, texts and reputation meant for the last holder fade first; the period is a published rule per number type ([Phone Numbers & Porting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/phone-numbers-and-porting#the-number-lifecycle)).
- **Attestation (A, B, C):** the STIR/SHAKEN level an originating provider signs onto a call: A (full) when it knows the customer and the customer's right to the number, B (partial) when it knows only the customer, C (gateway) when it can't authenticate the source ([Voice Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/voice-platforms#stir-shaken-signing-and-attestation)).
- **Authentication+:** The Campaign Registry's check that the person registering a brand actually controls it, billed per attempt including failed ones ([Business Texting Registration](/books/hosting-and-cloud/texting-registration#authentication)).
- **BIMI (Brand Indicators for Message Identification):** the standard that shows a sender's logo next to authenticated mail, requiring DMARC at enforcement, a VMC or CMC certificate and an SVG Tiny PS logo ([Inbox Rules](/books/hosting-and-cloud/inbox-rules#bimi)).
- **Blocklist (email):** a list of IPs and domains that many receivers consult, such as Spamhaus's SBL, XBL, CSS, PBL, ZEN and DBL; a listing of a shared IP blocks every customer on it ([Inbox Rules](/books/hosting-and-cloud/inbox-rules#blocklists)).
- **Blocks (email):** a third delivery outcome, distinct from hard and soft bounces, caused by the sender's own authentication or reputation; a block never creates a suppression of the recipient's address ([Email Sending Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-sending-platforms#how-to-build-it)).
- **Brand (10DLC):** the registration record of who is sending, tied to a tax identity, legal name, address, website and entity type, under which campaigns and numbers sit ([Business Texting Registration](/books/hosting-and-cloud/texting-registration#10dlc-brands-and-campaigns)).
- **Branded calling:** a verified name, logo and call reason added to the called party's display; for the provider it is a vetting workflow plus signing with extra claims, shown only on networks that have joined ([Voice Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/voice-platforms#call-labeling-and-branded-calling)).
- **Bring your own number (hosted messaging):** text-enabling a number whose voice service stays with its existing carrier, which needs the number owner's recorded authorization and no port ([Phone Numbers & Porting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/phone-numbers-and-porting#where-a-platform-s-numbers-come-from)).
- **Call labeling:** the "Spam Likely" style labels a terminating carrier and its analytics vendor attach to calls from patterns, complaints and attestation, which an originating provider can't buy its customers out of ([Voice Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/voice-platforms#call-labeling-and-branded-calling)).
- **Campaign (10DLC):** the registration record of what is sent: use case, description, sample messages, opt-in method, opt-out and help replies and content flags; each number belongs to one campaign at a time ([Business Texting Registration](/books/hosting-and-cloud/texting-registration#10dlc-brands-and-campaigns)).
- **The Campaign Registry (TCR):** the registry through which 10DLC brands and campaigns are registered and vetted, part of the private regime run by carriers and written down in CTIA's guidelines ([Business Texting Registration](/books/hosting-and-cloud/texting-registration#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Campaign Service Provider (CSP):** a platform registered with The Campaign Registry that submits its customers' brands and campaigns, its dashboard a front end for review queues it can't speed up ([Business Texting Registration](/books/hosting-and-cloud/texting-registration#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Carrier fee (pass-through):** the recipient carrier's surcharge on each message, billed to the customer as a pass-through line that raises revenue and cost by the same amount and so lowers the margin percentage ([The CPaaS Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpaas-business#pass-through-fees-and-the-margin)).
- **`carrier_fee_rules`:** the effective-dated table of carrier surcharges keyed by carrier, direction, number type, channel and registration status, so a new carrier fee shape is a row rather than code ([The CPaaS Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpaas-business#how-to-build-it)).
- **`carrier_limits`:** the throughput each carrier granted a brand or campaign at approval, stored as granted because the sender enforces those numbers, not the vetting score ([Business Texting Registration](/books/hosting-and-cloud/texting-registration#how-to-build-it)).
- **CMC (Common Mark Certificate):** a BIMI certificate type that doesn't require a registered trademark, displayed without the verified checkmark Gmail shows for VMCs ([Inbox Rules](/books/hosting-and-cloud/inbox-rules#bimi)).
- **Contribution factor (USF):** projected Universal Service program costs divided by projected contributable revenue, set quarterly and applied as a percentage of a provider's interstate and international end-user revenue ([911, CPNI & Telecom Duties](/books/hosting-and-cloud/telecom-duties#universal-service-and-regulatory-fees)).
- **Conversion monitoring:** tracking codes sent against codes verified per country and prefix, and throttling a destination for a customer when conversion falls, the main detector of SMS pumping ([Telecom Fraud](/books/hosting-and-cloud/telecom-fraud#sms-pumping)).
- **Country allowlist (geo permissions):** sending texts or calls only to countries the customer serves, defaulting to the home country, the cheapest and most immediate control against pumping and revenue share fraud ([Telecom Fraud](/books/hosting-and-cloud/telecom-fraud#sms-pumping)).
- **CPaaS (communications platform as a service):** a provider that rents phone numbers and carries texts, calls and email for other companies through an API, billed by the unit ([The CPaaS Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpaas-business)).
- **CPNI (customer proprietary network information):** what a carrier knows about a customer's use of its service (call detail records, numbers called, services subscribed), released only after authentication and certified as protected every year ([911, CPNI & Telecom Duties](/books/hosting-and-cloud/telecom-duties#cpni)).
- **CPNI annual certification:** the compliance certificate an officer signs each year under 47 CFR 64.2009(e), stating the provider's procedures, actions against data brokers and a summary of complaints ([911, CPNI & Telecom Duties](/books/hosting-and-cloud/telecom-duties#cpni)).
- **Dedicated IP (email):** an IP that carries one sender's traffic, so its reputation is that sender's alone: better for a large consistent sender, worse for a small or irregular one that never builds history ([Email Sending Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-sending-platforms#shared-and-dedicated-ips)).
- **Deferral:** a 4yz SMTP reply that slows a sender down; a rising deferral rate at one mailbox provider is the earliest sign of a reputation problem on an IP or domain ([Inbox Rules](/books/hosting-and-cloud/inbox-rules#how-mailbox-providers-enforce)).
- **Delivery receipt (DLR):** the asynchronous report from a carrier or upstream of what happened to a message, sometimes per segment, sometimes late and occasionally never; kept raw and mapped to the platform's own codes ([Sending a Text](/books/hosting-and-cloud/sending-a-text#delivery-receipts-and-errors)).
- **Destination prefix blocking:** blocking number ranges that show fraud patterns across all customers, kept private because a published list tells fraudsters which ranges are open ([Telecom Fraud](/books/hosting-and-cloud/telecom-fraud#sms-pumping)).
- **Direct-connect aggregator:** a CPaaS with contracts and SMPP or SIP connections to many carriers, giving breadth across countries and the ability to route around a failed or expensive carrier ([The CPaaS Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpaas-business#owned-network-versus-aggregator)).
- **DMARC at enforcement:** a DMARC policy of `p=quarantine` or `p=reject` (Gmail also wants `pct=100`), the precondition for BIMI ([Inbox Rules](/books/hosting-and-cloud/inbox-rules#bimi)).
- **ESP (email service provider):** a platform that sends email for other companies and whose product is reputation, pooled and divided across shared IPs, dedicated IPs, streams and onboarding ([Email Sending Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-sending-platforms)).
- **Feedback loop:** a mailbox provider's program that reports recipients' spam complaints back to the sender, such as Yahoo's Complaint Feedback Loop and Microsoft's JMRP; enrollments expire and need tracking ([Inbox Rules](/books/hosting-and-cloud/inbox-rules#feedback-loops)).
- **Feedback-ID:** the Gmail header that carries customer and campaign identifiers so Postmaster Tools can attribute complaint rates to them ([Inbox Rules](/books/hosting-and-cloud/inbox-rules#feedback-loops)).
- **Firm Order Commitment (FOC):** the losing provider's confirmation of a port date after validating a Local Service Request ([Phone Numbers & Porting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/phone-numbers-and-porting#porting-in)).
- **Form 499-A:** the yearly FCC revenue report on which telecommunications and VoIP providers base Universal Service contributions ([911, CPNI & Telecom Duties](/books/hosting-and-cloud/telecom-duties#universal-service-and-regulatory-fees)).
- **Growth numbering:** applying for more numbering resources, which needs a Months-to-Exhaust Worksheet of utilization by rate center and caps inventory per rate center ([Phone Numbers & Porting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/phone-numbers-and-porting#growth-numbering)).
- **GSM-7:** the default SMS encoding, 160 characters in one segment and 153 per concatenated segment, used when every character is in the GSM 7-bit alphabet ([Sending a Text](/books/hosting-and-cloud/sending-a-text#segments-and-encoding)).
- **Hard bounce:** a permanent delivery failure (the address doesn't exist or will never accept mail, an SMTP 5yz for the recipient), which suppresses the address for the sender and counts toward its bounce rate ([Email Sending Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-sending-platforms#bounces-blocks-and-suppression)).
- **HELP keyword:** the inbound keyword the platform answers with the campaign's registered help text ([Sending a Text](/books/hosting-and-cloud/sending-a-text#stop-at-the-platform)).
- **Ineligibility for mitigation:** Gmail's refusal to help bulk senders above a spam-rate threshold fix delivery problems, the top rung of its enforcement ladder ([Inbox Rules](/books/hosting-and-cloud/inbox-rules#how-mailbox-providers-enforce)).
- **IP pool:** a group of sending IPs (shared, dedicated or managed dedicated) for a traffic class; shared pools are tiered `new`, `trusted` and `quarantine` so one sender's problem doesn't spread ([Email Sending Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-sending-platforms#how-to-build-it)).
- **IRSF (international revenue share fraud):** the voice counterpart of SMS pumping: calls pumped to premium or high-cost numbers abroad whose termination revenue is shared with the fraudster ([Telecom Fraud](/books/hosting-and-cloud/telecom-fraud#toll-fraud-and-international-revenue-share)).
- **ISV model (texting):** a software platform registering each of its customers as its own brand under one account, attesting for their details, with a number per customer because a number belongs to one campaign ([Business Texting Registration](/books/hosting-and-cloud/texting-registration#registering-your-customers-the-isv-model)).
- **JMRP (Junk Email Reporting Program):** Microsoft's complaint feedback loop, part of SNDS, which reports when Outlook.com users mark a sender's mail as junk ([Inbox Rules](/books/hosting-and-cloud/inbox-rules#feedback-loops)).
- **Kari's Law:** the federal rule for multi-line telephone systems (offices, hotels, campuses, and hosted or cloud PBXs) requiring direct 911 dialing and notification ([911, CPNI & Telecom Duties](/books/hosting-and-cloud/telecom-duties#kari-s-law-and-hosted-phone-systems)).
- **Local Service Request (LSR):** the gaining provider's port request to the losing provider, validated against the customer's account and either confirmed with a date or rejected with a code ([Phone Numbers & Porting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/phone-numbers-and-porting#porting-in)).
- **Low-Volume Standard brand:** a 10DLC brand type with a tax ID, up to five campaigns and a low daily cap on T-Mobile, for senders with small volumes ([Business Texting Registration](/books/hosting-and-cloud/texting-registration#10dlc-brands-and-campaigns)).
- **Mailbox provider:** a receiver such as Gmail, Yahoo or Outlook.com that decides what reaches the inbox and judges an ESP's IPs and its customers' domains by everyone who sends through them ([Email Sending Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-sending-platforms)).
- **Message lifecycle (SMS):** accepted, queued, sent, delivered, and undelivered or failed, each status carrying the message ID and a sequence number so an older status can be discarded ([Sending a Text](/books/hosting-and-cloud/sending-a-text#delivery-receipts-and-errors)).
- **Message stream:** a separation of transactional and broadcast mail onto their own IP pools and suppression lists, as in Postmark's Message Streams, so marketing can't spoil receipts ([Email Sending Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-sending-platforms#streams-and-tenants)).
- **MLTS (multi-line telephone system):** a phone system serving many lines in an office, hotel or campus, including the hosted and cloud PBXs a platform runs, covered by Kari's Law ([911, CPNI & Telecom Duties](/books/hosting-and-cloud/telecom-duties#kari-s-law-and-hosted-phone-systems)).
- **NANP number:** a ten-digit number in the North American Numbering Plan: a three-digit area code (NPA), a three-digit central office code (NXX) and a four-digit line number ([Phone Numbers & Porting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/phone-numbers-and-porting#the-numbering-plan)).
- **Non-fixed VoIP:** service the user can move (a softphone, mobile app or laptop client), for which the provider must supply automated dispatchable location where technically feasible ([911, CPNI & Telecom Duties](/books/hosting-and-cloud/telecom-duties#911-for-voip)).
- **Number lifecycle:** available, reserved, assigned, ported out or released, aging, quarantined and returned; an assigned number is a held `phone_number` resource and inventory nobody holds lives in its own table ([Phone Numbers & Porting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/phone-numbers-and-porting#the-number-lifecycle)).
- **Onboarding and vetting (email):** constraining who may send at all (a sandbox, a review, a daily cap) because an ESP's reputation is the sum of its customers' ([Email Sending Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-sending-platforms#onboarding-and-vetting)).
- **Opt-out at the platform:** suppressing a recipient after STOP at the platform as a backstop, while still forwarding the message so the customer's consent ledger stays the system of record ([Sending a Text](/books/hosting-and-cloud/sending-a-text#stop-at-the-platform)).
- **Origination (voice):** inbound calls to the customer's numbers arriving at the provider from the public network and sent to the customer's SIP endpoint or application ([Voice Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/voice-platforms#sip-trunking)).
- **Owned network:** a CPaaS model holding carrier authorization, its own numbering, switches and interconnects, for lower cost per unit and control of porting at the price of every telecom duty ([The CPaaS Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpaas-business#owned-network-versus-aggregator)).
- **Port-out lock:** a customer-controlled switch that refuses port-out requests until turned off from an authenticated session, with notice ([Phone Numbers & Porting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/phone-numbers-and-porting#porting-out-and-number-takeover)).
- **Port-out PIN:** a secret set by the customer per account or number, stored as a hash and required on every port-out validation, so support sees only that validation failed ([Phone Numbers & Porting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/phone-numbers-and-porting#porting-out-and-number-takeover)).
- **Porting:** moving a number's service from one carrier to another; simple wireline and intermodal ports complete within one business day and non-simple ports within four under 47 CFR 52.35 ([Phone Numbers & Porting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/phone-numbers-and-porting#porting-in)).
- **Postmaster Tools:** Gmail's sender dashboard reporting spam rate and bulk-sender compliance, with an API a platform can poll for every customer domain ([Inbox Rules](/books/hosting-and-cloud/inbox-rules#how-mailbox-providers-enforce)).
- **Pre-send gating:** blocking bulk sending from domains that lack aligned DMARC and one-click unsubscribe, preventing rejections instead of reacting to them, with checks that rerun as DNS changes ([Inbox Rules](/books/hosting-and-cloud/inbox-rules#the-alternatives)).
- **Prepaid balance:** a CPaaS account's funds kept as a sum of ledger entries with the recharge that funded them, never as a cached number, so a double charge can be proved or disproved ([The CPaaS Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpaas-business#how-to-build-it)).
- **Quarantined number:** a number tied to abuse, a carrier complaint or a law enforcement request, held out of inventory until a person clears it with a reason ([Phone Numbers & Porting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/phone-numbers-and-porting#the-number-lifecycle)).
- **Rate center:** the geographic unit that decides whether a call is local; carriers get numbers in blocks per rate center, and utilization is measured by it ([Phone Numbers & Porting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/phone-numbers-and-porting#the-numbering-plan)).
- **RCS (Rich Communication Services):** business messaging with branding, rich cards, suggested replies and read receipts in the phone's default messaging app, falling back to SMS where unavailable ([Business Texting Registration](/books/hosting-and-cloud/texting-registration#rcs-business-messaging)).
- **Rejection (email):** a 5yz SMTP reply that refuses the message, such as Outlook.com's 5.7.515 for failed authentication ([Inbox Rules](/books/hosting-and-cloud/inbox-rules#how-mailbox-providers-enforce)).
- **Representative volume:** SES's per-account measurement base for bounce and complaint rates, used instead of a fixed window so a small sender's bad day doesn't trip a pause and a large sender's bad hour does ([Inbox Rules](/books/hosting-and-cloud/inbox-rules#enforcing-on-your-own-customers)).
- **RespOrg (responsible organization):** the entity a toll-free subscriber chooses to manage its records in the toll-free registry (47 CFR 52.101) ([Phone Numbers & Porting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/phone-numbers-and-porting#the-numbering-plan)).
- **RMD (Robocall Mitigation Database):** the FCC's public list of voice providers, each with a STIR/SHAKEN certification and a robocall mitigation plan; listing is the license to send traffic onto the US network ([Voice Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/voice-platforms#the-robocall-mitigation-database)).
- **Robocall mitigation plan:** the filing in the RMD describing how a provider knows its customers and avoids carrying illegal robocalls, updated within ten business days of a change and recertified yearly ([Voice Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/voice-platforms#the-robocall-mitigation-database)).
- **Segment:** the billing and throughput unit of SMS: a long message or one containing a non-GSM character is several segments, counted at accept time and billed per segment ([Sending a Text](/books/hosting-and-cloud/sending-a-text#segments-and-encoding)).
- **SES sandbox:** the per-Region starting state of an Amazon SES account, sending only to verified addresses or the mailbox simulator at a low cap until production access is granted ([Email Sending Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-sending-platforms#onboarding-and-vetting)).
- **SES tenants:** an SES feature that divides one AWS account's sending into tenants with automated reputation policies (Standard, Strict or None) that pause sending on findings ([Email Sending Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-sending-platforms#streams-and-tenants)).
- **Short code:** a five- or six-digit number leased from the Common Short Code Administration, with the highest throughput and recognition and the longest certification ([Business Texting Registration](/books/hosting-and-cloud/texting-registration#short-codes)).
- **SIP trunking:** voice service in which a customer's phone system or application sends and receives calls over SIP to the provider, with media (RTP) flowing separately, billed per minute, channel or trunk and number ([Voice Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/voice-platforms#sip-trunking)).
- **SMS pumping (artificially inflated traffic, AIT):** bots abusing any form that texts a typed phone number, triggering passcodes to blocks of numbers on a carrier that shares the termination revenue ([Telecom Fraud](/books/hosting-and-cloud/telecom-fraud#sms-pumping)).
- **SNDS:** Microsoft's sender data program for IP reputation, which includes the Junk Email Reporting Program ([Inbox Rules](/books/hosting-and-cloud/inbox-rules#feedback-loops)).
- **Soft bounce:** a temporary delivery problem (mailbox full, server busy, a 4yz deferral), retried with backoff and suppressed only after repeated failures ([Email Sending Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-sending-platforms#bounces-blocks-and-suppression)).
- **Software aggregator:** a CPaaS that is a routing and orchestration layer over owned-network providers and aggregators, fastest to build with the thinnest margin and the most upstream risk ([The CPaaS Business](/books/hosting-and-cloud/cpaas-business#owned-network-versus-aggregator)).
- **Sole Proprietor brand:** a 10DLC brand type for individuals with no tax ID, limited to one campaign and the lowest throughput ([Business Texting Registration](/books/hosting-and-cloud/texting-registration#10dlc-brands-and-campaigns)).
- **SPC token:** the token from the STI Policy Administrator (iConectiv) that proves a provider is authorized to get STIR/SHAKEN certificates ([Voice Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/voice-platforms#stir-shaken-signing-and-attestation)).
- **Special-services blocks:** default blocks on premium-rate and shared-cost destinations, part of the platform's defense against toll fraud ([Telecom Fraud](/books/hosting-and-cloud/telecom-fraud#toll-fraud-and-international-revenue-share)).
- **Spend and concurrency limits:** caps on simultaneous international calls and hourly international spend per account, with alerts, modeled as quotas ([Telecom Fraud](/books/hosting-and-cloud/telecom-fraud#toll-fraud-and-international-revenue-share)).
- **START keyword:** the inbound keyword (or the campaign's opt-in keyword) that lifts the platform's opt-out for that recipient and sender ([Sending a Text](/books/hosting-and-cloud/sending-a-text#stop-at-the-platform)).
- **STIR/SHAKEN:** caller ID authentication: STIR (the IETF's Secure Telephone Identity Revisited) defines the cryptography and SHAKEN (ATIS-1000074) how US carriers use it to sign and verify calls ([Voice Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/voice-platforms#stir-shaken-signing-and-attestation)).
- **STOP keyword:** the inbound keyword that records an opt-out for that recipient against that sender, sends the campaign's confirmation and rejects later sends with a permanent error ([Sending a Text](/books/hosting-and-cloud/sending-a-text#stop-at-the-platform)).
- **Stream- and tenant-level thresholds:** pausing only the stream or tenant that caused a reputation problem, keeping transactional mail flowing at the cost of more state ([Inbox Rules](/books/hosting-and-cloud/inbox-rules#the-alternatives)).
- **Suppression:** a recorded refusal to send to an address (after a hard bounce, complaint or unsubscribe), scoped to platform, account, tenant or stream and honored whatever the API call says ([Email Sending Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-sending-platforms#bounces-blocks-and-suppression)).
- **Termination (voice):** outbound calls the customer's system sends to the provider as SIP INVITEs, which the provider routes to the destination network ([Voice Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/voice-platforms#sip-trunking)).
- **Throughput scopes:** the separate limits a text must clear (campaign on AT&T, brand on T-Mobile, MMS per carrier, toll-free number, upstream route, account), each in its own unit ([Sending a Text](/books/hosting-and-cloud/sending-a-text#throughput)).
- **Toll fraud:** running up calls on a customer's account to expensive destinations, usually through an open SIP trunk or a stolen key; an open trunk is an open wallet ([Telecom Fraud](/books/hosting-and-cloud/telecom-fraud#toll-fraud-and-international-revenue-share)).
- **Toll-free verification:** the review a toll-free number must pass before it can text at volume, which now requires a business registration number and privacy policy and terms URLs ([Business Texting Registration](/books/hosting-and-cloud/texting-registration#toll-free-verification)).
- **Trust score (vetting score):** the score a brand earns from vetting, from which carriers derive its throughput caps ([Business Texting Registration](/books/hosting-and-cloud/texting-registration#vetting-and-trust-scores)).
- **UCS-2:** the SMS encoding used when any character falls outside the GSM alphabet, including one emoji, cutting a segment to 70 characters (67 when concatenated) ([Sending a Text](/books/hosting-and-cloud/sending-a-text#segments-and-encoding)).
- **Universal Service Fund (USF):** the federal fund for rural, low-income, schools and libraries, and rural health care programs, paid for by provider contributions on interstate and international end-user revenue ([911, CPNI & Telecom Duties](/books/hosting-and-cloud/telecom-duties#universal-service-and-regulatory-fees)).
- **Verified caller ID:** the record of a customer proving control of an outside number, by a verification call or text, before it may present that number as caller ID ([Voice Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/voice-platforms#how-to-build-it)).
- **VMC (Verified Mark Certificate):** the BIMI certificate type that requires a registered trademark and earns Gmail's verified checkmark ([Inbox Rules](/books/hosting-and-cloud/inbox-rules#bimi)).
- **VoIP direct access:** numbering resources obtained by an interconnected VoIP provider directly from NANPA and the Pooling Administrator, subject to certifications ([Phone Numbers & Porting](/books/hosting-and-cloud/phone-numbers-and-porting#voip-direct-access-and-its-certifications)).
- **Warmup:** gradually raising a new IP's volume so mailbox providers learn to trust it, with overflow to a reputable pool rather than delaying the customer's mail; state, not configuration ([Email Sending Platforms](/books/hosting-and-cloud/email-sending-platforms#warmup)).

## Metering and Billing

- **Affected ratio:** an SLA measure that weights an outage's minutes by the share of unique visitor IPs affected, so a partial outage earns a partial credit ([SLAs & Service Credits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/slas-and-service-credits#how-uptime-is-measured)).
- **Alert-first provider:** a provider that gives cost data and budget warnings but leaves stopping spend to the customer's own automation ([Cost Data, FinOps & Surprise Bills](/books/hosting-and-cloud/finops-and-surprise-bills#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Allowance:** a quantity a subscription includes (requests, CPU-hours, rows read), held on the plan version and consumed during rating before anything is priced; not a credit ([Metering & Rating](/books/hosting-and-cloud/metering-and-rating#allowances-tiers-and-monthly-caps)).
- **Always free:** a monthly allowance per meter that resets each period, in effect a negative price on the first units of a meter, every month, forever ([Free Tiers, Credits & Startup Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/free-tiers-and-credits#always-free-trial-and-credit)).
- **Anomaly detection (cost):** flagging spend that departs from an account's pattern; actionable only if it names the project, SKU or region that changed, and worth most when it runs on provisional hourly rating ([Cost Data, FinOps & Surprise Bills](/books/hosting-and-cloud/finops-and-surprise-bills#anomaly-detection)).
- **Attributes (SKU):** the descriptive properties of a SKU for humans and tools (vCPUs, memory, storage class, operating system, tenancy), as distinct from the SKU code that billing points at ([Pricing Catalogs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pricing-catalogs#skus-and-attributes)).
- **Automatic non-billing:** an SLA remedy that simply doesn't charge for unavailable hours, with no claim needed, applied as a correction during rating rather than as a credit ([SLAs & Service Credits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/slas-and-service-credits#the-alternatives)).
- **Automatic renewal law (state):** state statutes on auto-renewing consumer contracts, such as California's, with their own disclosure, reminder and cancellation rules, versioned as rule rows by jurisdiction and consent date ([Subscriptions, Renewals & Cancellation Law](/books/hosting-and-cloud/renewals-and-cancellation-law#state-automatic-renewal-laws)).
- **Bill pipeline:** the ordered stages from usage events to invoice (rating, allowances, commitments, credits, tax, rounding), in which discounts never edit rated usage and nothing is rounded before the invoice line ([Metering & Rating](/books/hosting-and-cloud/metering-and-rating#the-bill-pipeline)).
- **Billing mode:** a contract fact on the account deciding how it pays: `self_serve` charged automatically, `invoiced` with net terms, or `billed_by_parent` through a reseller or organization ([Invoices, Dunning & Suspension](/books/hosting-and-cloud/invoices-dunning-and-suspension#payment-terms-and-late-interest)).
- **Breakage:** the value of credits that expire unused, computed and reported to finance by cohort rather than posted by the product; the order credits expire in changes it ([Free Tiers, Credits & Startup Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/free-tiers-and-credits#credits-on-the-books)).
- **Budget:** an amount, a scope, a period, a cost basis, thresholds that alert and optionally an action; providers word budgets as alerts and customers often read them as limits ([Cost Data, FinOps & Surprise Bills](/books/hosting-and-cloud/finops-and-surprise-bills#budgets-and-alerts)).
- **Budget actions:** AWS's mechanism that applies a policy or stops resources when a budget threshold is crossed, opt-in and customer-configured ([Cost Data, FinOps & Surprise Bills](/books/hosting-and-cloud/finops-and-surprise-bills#how-it-got-here)).
- **Capacity minutes:** an SLA measure for capacity reservations counting minutes a reserved unit can't be allocated after a supported deployment ([SLAs & Service Credits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/slas-and-service-credits#how-uptime-is-measured)).
- **Capped price:** a price shape that is flat until a period ceiling per resource, such as a monthly cap on an hourly server price ([Pricing Catalogs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pricing-catalogs#price-shapes)).
- **Cascade (reseller):** applying a non-paying reseller's hold to its sub-accounts' resources, at once for `no_new_resources` and only after notice for stronger effects ([Invoices, Dunning & Suspension](/books/hosting-and-cloud/invoices-dunning-and-suspension#resellers-and-the-cascade)).
- **Charge date as cancellation deadline:** the rule that a renewal reminder must arrive before the card is charged, not before expiry, because the charge is the last moment the customer can stop it ([Subscriptions, Renewals & Cancellation Law](/books/hosting-and-cloud/renewals-and-cancellation-law#domains-auto-renew-and-the-errp-clock)).
- **`charge_lead_days`:** the plan version column that makes an early renewal charge explicit and anchors reminders and invoice runs on the charge rather than the expiry ([Subscriptions, Renewals & Cancellation Law](/books/hosting-and-cloud/renewals-and-cancellation-law#how-to-build-it)).
- **Click-to-cancel:** the FTC's 2024 amendments to the Negative Option Rule that would have made a federal cancellation standard, vacated by the Eighth Circuit in July 2025; the FTC restored the 1973 text in 2026 ([Subscriptions, Renewals & Cancellation Law](/books/hosting-and-cloud/renewals-and-cancellation-law#the-vacated-rule-and-the-2026-advance-notice)).
- **Commitment drawdown (marketplace):** the selling point of cloud marketplaces: a customer with an unmet spend commitment buys a vendor's software and retires commitment at the same time ([Commitments, Discounts & Marketplaces](/books/hosting-and-cloud/commitments-and-marketplaces#marketplaces)).
- **Committed use discount (CUD):** Google's commitment discount, spend-based (an hourly dollar amount across projects the billing account pays for) or resource-based (bought for a project) ([Commitments, Discounts & Marketplaces](/books/hosting-and-cloud/commitments-and-marketplaces#resource-and-spend-commitments)).
- **Connectivity minutes:** an SLA measure counting minutes a resource has no external connectivity (for multi-zone commitments, every instance in two or more zones at once) ([SLAs & Service Credits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/slas-and-service-credits#how-uptime-is-measured)).
- **Consumed Quantity and Pricing Quantity:** the FOCUS split between what a meter measured and the unit the price applies to, which is where minimums, increments and package sizes show ([Cost Data, FinOps & Surprise Bills](/books/hosting-and-cloud/finops-and-surprise-bills#cost-exports-cur-and-focus)).
- **Contracting entity versus billing entity:** the split in which one legal entity is party to the terms and disputes while another numbers invoices and charges tax ([Tax on Hosting & Cloud](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tax#seller-entities)).
- **Correction (usage):** a new event that restates a wrong one through `corrects_id`, carrying the full corrected quantity (zero to void), never an update or a subtraction ([Metering & Rating](/books/hosting-and-cloud/metering-and-rating#late-duplicate-and-corrected-usage)).
- **Cost export:** the provider's rated usage, discounts, credits, taxes and fees as one row per charge, delivered on a schedule to the customer's storage and restated as rating changes ([Cost Data, FinOps & Surprise Bills](/books/hosting-and-cloud/finops-and-surprise-bills#cost-exports-cur-and-focus)).
- **Counter (meter kind):** a meter of how many times something happened (requests, rows read, segments, tokens), aggregated by sum ([Metering & Rating](/books/hosting-and-cloud/metering-and-rating#meters-units-and-granularity)).
- **Credit balance with expiry:** a free offer bounded by an amount and a clock, easy to void and booked as a liability with breakage ([Free Tiers, Credits & Startup Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/free-tiers-and-credits#the-alternatives)).
- **Credit exhaustion cap:** a spend cap where nothing else can be billed by construction, because the account has no payment method or can't be charged once credit runs out ([Cost Data, FinOps & Surprise Bills](/books/hosting-and-cloud/finops-and-surprise-bills#spend-caps-and-pauses)).
- **Credit tier (SLA):** the percentage of the affected service's fee credited for a period, chosen by the lowest tier the measured uptime falls below ([SLAs & Service Credits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/slas-and-service-credits#credit-tiers)).
- **`credit_grants`:** the table of credits issued to an account (promotional, startup, SLA, goodwill, commitment shortfall), each with its amount, scope, exclusions and expiry, drawn down by invoice lines ([Free Tiers, Credits & Startup Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/free-tiers-and-credits#how-to-build-it)).
- **CUR (Cost and Usage Report):** AWS's native line-item cost export, carrying every provider-specific column, as opposed to the cross-provider FOCUS format ([Cost Data, FinOps & Surprise Bills](/books/hosting-and-cloud/finops-and-surprise-bills#cost-exports-cur-and-focus)).
- **Cycle invoice:** the regular invoice for usage in arrears for the period plus recurring charges in advance where the plan bills that way, issued on the cycle date ([Invoices, Dunning & Suspension](/books/hosting-and-cloud/invoices-dunning-and-suspension#invoice-runs-and-cadence)).
- **Data after termination:** the window after an account ends during which content is retained and retrievable, published by each provider and sometimes conditioned on payment ([Invoices, Dunning & Suspension](/books/hosting-and-cloud/invoices-dunning-and-suspension#data-after-termination)).
- **Digital services tax:** a tax aimed at platforms, advertising and marketplaces rather than infrastructure rent, different from withholding tax on cloud payments ([Tax on Hosting & Cloud](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tax#withholding-tax-and-gross-up)).
- **Dimensions (meter):** the attributes on a usage event (region, operating system, carrier, direction, storage class, model, outcome) that split one meter into many prices and decide the SKU ([Metering & Rating](/books/hosting-and-cloud/metering-and-rating#meters-units-and-granularity)).
- **Dispute (invoice):** a record against invoice lines that pauses interest and dunning for those lines only, setting a dunning case to paused rather than closing it ([Invoices, Dunning & Suspension](/books/hosting-and-cloud/invoices-dunning-and-suspension#payment-terms-and-late-interest)).
- **Dunning:** the schedule of payment retries, notices and grace after a failed payment, stored as rows with offsets per billing mode and escalating by adding stronger holds ([Invoices, Dunning & Suspension](/books/hosting-and-cloud/invoices-dunning-and-suspension#failed-payments-and-dunning)).
- **Duration (meter kind):** a meter of how long something existed or ran (instance-seconds, GPU-hours, address-hours), aggregated as the overlap of each lifetime with the window ([Metering & Rating](/books/hosting-and-cloud/metering-and-rating#meters-units-and-granularity)).
- **Effective events:** the usage events rated for a window: those in it, received before the run's cutoff and not superseded by a later correction ([Metering & Rating](/books/hosting-and-cloud/metering-and-rating#rating-from-events-to-money)).
- **Electronically supplied service (EU VAT):** a service on the EU's Implementing Regulation 282/2011 Annex I list, which includes website and webpage hosting, taxed where a consumer is ([Tax on Hosting & Cloud](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tax#vat-and-gst-on-electronic-services)).
- **Error-rate intervals:** an SLA measure averaging server errors over requests in each five-minute interval of the month, with empty intervals at 0% ([SLAs & Service Credits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/slas-and-service-credits#how-uptime-is-measured)).
- **Error-rate threshold:** an SLA measure that counts minutes only when more than a set share of valid requests fail for consecutive minutes with the client backing off ([SLAs & Service Credits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/slas-and-service-credits#how-uptime-is-measured)).
- **ERRP clock (domains):** the pre-expiry and post-expiry notices ICANN's Expired Registration Recovery Policy requires, generated as a second rule set beside consumer renewal rules on the same subscription ([Subscriptions, Renewals & Cancellation Law](/books/hosting-and-cloud/renewals-and-cancellation-law#domains-auto-renew-and-the-errp-clock)).
- **Exemption certificate:** a customer's tax exemption document, held with its expiry and review status and checked at invoicing ([Tax on Hosting & Cloud](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tax#exemptions-and-tax-codes-per-sku)).
- **Flat price:** the simplest price shape, quantity times unit price, used for instance-seconds, segments and most usage ([Pricing Catalogs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pricing-catalogs#price-shapes)).
- **FOCUS (FinOps Open Cost and Usage Specification):** the FinOps Foundation's common cost export format with List Cost, Effective Cost and Billed Cost columns, readable across providers ([Cost Data, FinOps & Surprise Bills](/books/hosting-and-cloud/finops-and-surprise-bills#cost-exports-cur-and-focus)).
- **Forgiveness (surprise bill):** choosing not to collect for usage that was real, recorded as a goodwill credit grant, as distinct from fixing a faulty meter by rerating ([Cost Data, FinOps & Surprise Bills](/books/hosting-and-cloud/finops-and-surprise-bills#surprise-bills-and-forgiveness)).
- **Gauge (meter kind):** a meter of a level held over time (stored GB, provisioned memory), aggregated as a time-weighted average and converted to GB-months or GB-hours ([Metering & Rating](/books/hosting-and-cloud/metering-and-rating#meters-units-and-granularity)).
- **Graduated tiers:** a price shape in which each volume band is priced at its own rate and the bands are summed ([Metering & Rating](/books/hosting-and-cloud/metering-and-rating#allowances-tiers-and-monthly-caps)).
- **Gross-up:** a contract clause under which a customer whose country withholds tax still owes the provider the full invoice amount, so a short payment equal to the withholding rate stays owed ([Tax on Hosting & Cloud](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tax#withholding-tax-and-gross-up)).
- **Hard cap (spending limit):** a limit that stops spend rather than alerting, implemented as an account hold when provisional spend reaches the limit; sold as hard, any overshoot is credited ([Free Tiers, Credits & Startup Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/free-tiers-and-credits#spending-limits-the-hard-cap)).
- **Hourly spend commitment:** a dollar-per-hour promise for a term (AWS Savings Plans, Azure savings plans, Google spend-based CUDs), in which each hour's eligible usage is priced at commitment rates and unused commitment is lost ([Commitments, Discounts & Marketplaces](/books/hosting-and-cloud/commitments-and-marketplaces#resource-and-spend-commitments)).
- **Idempotency key (usage event):** the producer's key on every event, unique per source, so a replayed batch inserts nothing and duplicates are a no-op ([Metering & Rating](/books/hosting-and-cloud/metering-and-rating#late-duplicate-and-corrected-usage)).
- **Invoice run:** a batch job with a period, a cutoff and a seller that turns rated usage, commitments and credits into numbered, taxed invoices for every account, idempotent and resumable ([Invoices, Dunning & Suspension](/books/hosting-and-cloud/invoices-dunning-and-suspension#invoice-runs-and-cadence)).
- **Invoiced net terms:** billing large accounts by invoice payable within a contractual period, with receivables, late payers and collections, instead of charging a card ([Invoices, Dunning & Suspension](/books/hosting-and-cloud/invoices-dunning-and-suspension#payment-terms-and-late-interest)).
- **Involuntary churn:** customers lost to failed payment rather than cancellation, reported as its own metric because its fix is billing, not product ([Invoices, Dunning & Suspension](/books/hosting-and-cloud/invoices-dunning-and-suspension#failed-payments-and-dunning)).
- **Late interest:** a contractual rate and trigger for overdue invoices, charged as an adjustment line on a later invoice and never as a change to the late one ([Invoices, Dunning & Suspension](/books/hosting-and-cloud/invoices-dunning-and-suspension#payment-terms-and-late-interest)).
- **Lateness window:** how late a usage event may arrive and still rate into its own period, stated per meter (`meters.lateness_window`); events after the cutoff go to the next run as adjustments ([Metering & Rating](/books/hosting-and-cloud/metering-and-rating#late-duplicate-and-corrected-usage)).
- **Lease tax (Chicago):** Chicago's Lease Transaction Tax, which the City's Ruling #12 applied to charges under a "non-possessory computer lease", reaching cloud compute; an effective-dated rate the City changes with its budgets ([Tax on Hosting & Cloud](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tax#local-cloud-taxes-chicago)).
- **List, Effective and Billed Cost:** the three costs per cost-export row: what usage would cost at list, what it cost with commitment purchases amortized, and what the customer is billed ([Cost Data, FinOps & Surprise Bills](/books/hosting-and-cloud/finops-and-surprise-bills#cost-exports-cur-and-focus)).
- **Listing fee:** a cloud marketplace's share of a vendor's sale, set by offer type and contract value and frozen at acceptance; the provider's revenue is the fee, not the line ([Commitments, Discounts & Marketplaces](/books/hosting-and-cloud/commitments-and-marketplaces#marketplaces)).
- **MACC (Microsoft Azure Consumption Commitment):** a contractual commitment to a level of Azure spending over a period, tracked by remaining balance and eligible spend, with statuses Active, Complete, Expired and Canceled ([Commitments, Discounts & Marketplaces](/books/hosting-and-cloud/commitments-and-marketplaces#enterprise-commitments-and-shortfalls)).
- **Marketplace (cloud):** a provider's catalog of third-party software billed on the provider's invoice, where the vendor's charge is a pass-through line and eligible purchases count toward commitments ([Commitments, Discounts & Marketplaces](/books/hosting-and-cloud/commitments-and-marketplaces#marketplaces)).
- **Merchant of record:** a third party that sells on a provider's behalf and takes on the registrations and filings, for a fee per transaction ([Tax on Hosting & Cloud](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tax#the-alternatives)).
- **Meter:** a named measurement with a unit, a way of aggregating it and the dimensions that can split it; a SKU rates a meter, and several SKUs can rate the same one ([Metering & Rating](/books/hosting-and-cloud/metering-and-rating#meters-units-and-granularity)).
- **Meter registry:** the contract between service teams and billing: a new meter is a row with an owner, kind, allowed dimensions and lateness window before any event may name it ([Metering & Rating](/books/hosting-and-cloud/metering-and-rating#how-to-build-it)).
- **Milestone (commitment):** an optional intermediate spend target with a due date and amount inside an enterprise commitment, whose miss can trigger a shortfall ([Commitments, Discounts & Marketplaces](/books/hosting-and-cloud/commitments-and-marketplaces#enterprise-commitments-and-shortfalls)).
- **Minimum and increment:** the per-session billing rules (a one-minute minimum, per-second or per-minute rounding) a SKU applies, charged once per launch or job rather than per hour ([Metering & Rating](/books/hosting-and-cloud/metering-and-rating#rating-from-events-to-money)).
- **Monthly cap:** a ceiling at which an hourly charge stops for the month, such as a server never billed above its monthly price ([Metering & Rating](/books/hosting-and-cloud/metering-and-rating#allowances-tiers-and-monthly-caps)).
- **Negative Option Rule:** the FTC's 1973 rule on prenotification plans, the book-club model, which the FTC's later amendments sought to extend to all negative option offers ([Subscriptions, Renewals & Cancellation Law](/books/hosting-and-cloud/renewals-and-cancellation-law#how-it-got-here)).
- **No setoff:** the contract rule that a customer pays the invoice in full even if it believes it is owed an SLA credit, which arrives separately through the SLA process ([Invoices, Dunning & Suspension](/books/hosting-and-cloud/invoices-dunning-and-suspension#payment-terms-and-late-interest)).
- **Notice (price change):** the record of when a price change was announced, through which channels and to which affected accounts, enforced so an effective date before the required notice can't be inserted ([Pricing Catalogs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pricing-catalogs#price-changes-and-notice)).
- **One Stop Shop (OSS):** the EU scheme, since July 2021, letting a seller file one quarterly VAT return for consumer sales across all member states ([Tax on Hosting & Cloud](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tax#vat-and-gst-on-electronic-services)).
- **Package price:** a price shape charged per block with partial blocks rounded up, such as traffic overage in fixed blocks or credit packs ([Pricing Catalogs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pricing-catalogs#price-shapes)).
- **Partial invoice (threshold):** an invoice issued mid-cycle when accrued usage crosses the account's threshold; partial and end-of-cycle invoices must add up to the period without double billing ([Invoices, Dunning & Suspension](/books/hosting-and-cloud/invoices-dunning-and-suspension#invoice-runs-and-cadence)).
- **Pass-through fee:** a supplier's charge (carrier, registry, regulatory, marketplace vendor) billed on its own SKU and rated as a separate row from the same event, so it never blends into the provider's margin ([Metering & Rating](/books/hosting-and-cloud/metering-and-rating#pass-through-fees)).
- **Period-close run:** the authoritative rating run for a period that rerates all effective events with tiers, caps and allowances applied over the whole period, superseding the provisional hourly runs ([Metering & Rating](/books/hosting-and-cloud/metering-and-rating#how-to-build-it)).
- **Price book:** a set of price versions (list, a contract, a commitment's rates); rating looks in the account's contract book first, then list ([Pricing Catalogs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pricing-catalogs#price-shapes)).
- **Price version:** an effective-dated price for a SKU in a price book and currency, with its tier shape; a price change is a new version with an announcement, boundary, audience and notice obligation ([Pricing Catalogs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pricing-catalogs#price-changes-and-notice)).
- **Pricing page as truth:** the rule that the page, API, calculator, checkout and rater all render the same price rows, because the published page wins every dispute ([Pricing Catalogs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pricing-catalogs#the-pricing-page-as-truth)).
- **Private offer:** a negotiated marketplace price and contract terms for one buyer, sometimes through a channel partner who records its own margin ([Commitments, Discounts & Marketplaces](/books/hosting-and-cloud/commitments-and-marketplaces#private-offers-and-channel-partners)).
- **Proportional credit:** an SLA remedy that matches the credit to the measured damage, allowing a headline 100% promise that pays little ([SLAs & Service Credits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/slas-and-service-credits#the-alternatives)).
- **`rated_usage`:** the rating output per run: billable quantity after minimums, the part an allowance covered, and the unrounded amount, which commitments and credits point at rather than edit ([Metering & Rating](/books/hosting-and-cloud/metering-and-rating#rating-from-events-to-money)).
- **Rating:** the deterministic function that turns the effective events for a window, the SKUs that match them and the price versions in force when usage happened into money, written to `rated_usage` unrounded ([Metering & Rating](/books/hosting-and-cloud/metering-and-rating#rating-from-events-to-money)).
- **Renewal price:** the list price a plan renews at after its intro term, a material term to disclose before billing, often several times the intro price ([Subscriptions, Renewals & Cancellation Law](/books/hosting-and-cloud/renewals-and-cancellation-law#terms-intro-prices-and-the-renewal)).
- **Rerating:** fixing a price, a rule or a batch of events by a new rating run that supersedes the old one for its scope, with the diff as the evidence for a disputed bill ([Metering & Rating](/books/hosting-and-cloud/metering-and-rating#late-duplicate-and-corrected-usage)).
- **Reserved Instance:** a commitment to a specific resource (instance type, region) for a term in exchange for a discount, applied before savings plans ([Commitments, Discounts & Marketplaces](/books/hosting-and-cloud/commitments-and-marketplaces#resource-and-spend-commitments)).
- **Reverse charge:** the B2B VAT rule under which the business customer, not the seller, accounts for VAT on a cross-border service (Article 196 of the VAT Directive) ([Tax on Hosting & Cloud](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tax#vat-and-gst-on-electronic-services)).
- **ROSCA (Restore Online Shoppers' Confidence Act):** the US federal law (15 U.S.C. 8403) requiring clear disclosure of material terms before billing information is collected, express informed consent and simple mechanisms to stop recurring charges ([Subscriptions, Renewals & Cancellation Law](/books/hosting-and-cloud/renewals-and-cancellation-law#rosca-and-the-ftc)).
- **Savings Plans:** AWS's dollar-per-hour commitment for one or three years, as Compute Savings Plans across families, Regions and services or EC2 Instance Savings Plans for one family in one Region ([Commitments, Discounts & Marketplaces](/books/hosting-and-cloud/commitments-and-marketplaces#how-it-got-here)).
- **Seller entity (seller of record):** the legal entity that sells to an account and whose registrations, invoice series and tax rules apply, assigned from address data and changeable only for future invoices ([Tax on Hosting & Cloud](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tax#seller-entities)).
- **Service-level cap:** a spend cap that stops new usage of one service in one project while in-flight requests and persistent resources continue ([Cost Data, FinOps & Surprise Bills](/books/hosting-and-cloud/finops-and-surprise-bills#spend-caps-and-pauses)).
- **Shortfall charge:** the amount owed when an enterprise spend commitment or milestone isn't met, recorded as an invoice line and a credit grant of kind `commitment_shortfall` ([Commitments, Discounts & Marketplaces](/books/hosting-and-cloud/commitments-and-marketplaces#enterprise-commitments-and-shortfalls)).
- **SKU:** one billable unit with one meter, such as one instance type in one region on one operating system per second; its code is permanent and a discontinued SKU is retired, never deleted ([Pricing Catalogs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/pricing-catalogs#skus-and-attributes)).
- **SLA (service level agreement):** a price adjustment, not a promise of uptime: a published definition of uptime with credit tiers, exclusions and a claim process, usually the customer's sole remedy ([SLAs & Service Credits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/slas-and-service-credits)).
- **SLA versus SLO:** the difference between the contractual measure a provider credits against and its internal objective; the error budget must use the published definition to predict credit exposure ([SLAs & Service Credits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/slas-and-service-credits#sla-versus-slo)).
- **Sole remedy:** the SLA clause making service credits the customer's only remedy for downtime, alongside exclusions for causes outside the provider's control, the customer's doing, previews, throttling, suspension and maintenance ([SLAs & Service Credits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/slas-and-service-credits#exclusions-and-sole-remedy)).
- **Spend commitment (total):** a dollar total over a term, sometimes with milestones, that eligible invoiced spend decrements, in exchange for a discount on everything ([Commitments, Discounts & Marketplaces](/books/hosting-and-cloud/commitments-and-marketplaces#resource-and-spend-commitments)).
- **Spot capacity:** the provider's idle buffer sold at a discount with a recall clause, interrupted with short or no notice when capacity is needed, recorded as the end of the resource's lifetime ([Commitments, Discounts & Marketplaces](/books/hosting-and-cloud/commitments-and-marketplaces#spot-and-interruptible-capacity)).
- **Startup program:** a credit with a sponsor, often an investor whose identifier unlocks it, sized up to six figures, with exclusions enforced at application time ([Free Tiers, Credits & Startup Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/free-tiers-and-credits#startup-programs)).
- **Stop-first provider:** a provider or plan that pauses or stops usage at a limit (free plans, credit subscriptions, optional budget pauses) rather than only alerting ([Cost Data, FinOps & Surprise Bills](/books/hosting-and-cloud/finops-and-surprise-bills#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Suspension clock:** the published days from non-payment to suspension, termination and data deletion, stored on the account hold so notices quote the same dates the system enforces ([Invoices, Dunning & Suspension](/books/hosting-and-cloud/invoices-dunning-and-suspension#suspension-and-deletion-clocks)).
- **Sustained use discount:** Google's automatic discount for running a resource much of the month, delivered as monthly credits and not applied to usage a commitment already covers ([Commitments, Discounts & Marketplaces](/books/hosting-and-cloud/commitments-and-marketplaces#resource-and-spend-commitments)).
- **Tax location:** the basis recorded on each invoice for where the customer is (tax ID country, matching card, billing or contact address), chosen by a published precedence ([Tax on Hosting & Cloud](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tax#where-is-the-customer)).
- **Threshold billing:** charging a new or risky account mid-cycle whenever accrued usage crosses a per-account threshold that rises with payment history; a threshold collects money and is not a spend cap ([Invoices, Dunning & Suspension](/books/hosting-and-cloud/invoices-dunning-and-suspension#thresholds-and-mid-cycle-charges)).
- **Trial:** full or restricted access for a period, a clock that ends in a decision; a trial that converts to paid without a new act is an automatic renewal under state law ([Free Tiers, Credits & Startup Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/free-tiers-and-credits#always-free-trial-and-credit)).
- **Turn off auto-renew:** the toggle many providers use to end a plan at term end, which is legally and in the data a cancellation ([Subscriptions, Renewals & Cancellation Law](/books/hosting-and-cloud/renewals-and-cancellation-law#cancellation-online)).
- **Unavailable minutes with a floor:** an SLA measure that counts API unavailability only in outages longer than a minimum continuous duration ([SLAs & Service Credits](/books/hosting-and-cloud/slas-and-service-credits#how-uptime-is-measured)).
- **Usage time:** the time usage happened, which picks the price version, as opposed to processing time, so a rerun next quarter prices the same way ([Metering & Rating](/books/hosting-and-cloud/metering-and-rating#rating-from-events-to-money)).
- **USF recovery line:** the provider's recovery of its Universal Service contribution, billed as a regulatory pass-through SKU on the interstate share of revenue rather than as a tax line ([Tax on Hosting & Cloud](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tax#telecom-taxes-and-regulatory-fees)).
- **VAT ID validation:** checking a customer's VAT number, the trigger for reverse charge, with a pending or unverifiable state when the validation service doesn't answer ([Tax on Hosting & Cloud](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tax#vat-and-gst-on-electronic-services)).
- **Volume tiers:** a price shape in which the whole quantity takes the rate of the band it ends in ([Metering & Rating](/books/hosting-and-cloud/metering-and-rating#allowances-tiers-and-monthly-caps)).
- **Withdrawal right:** the consumer right to withdraw from a distance contract within a set period, which a provider starting service at once must handle inside a prepaid term ([Subscriptions, Renewals & Cancellation Law](/books/hosting-and-cloud/renewals-and-cancellation-law#eu-germany-and-the-uk)).
- **Withholding tax:** tax a customer's country deducts from a cross-border payment to the provider, which a provider can avoid with a local entity or offset by grossing up ([Tax on Hosting & Cloud](/books/hosting-and-cloud/tax#withholding-tax-and-gross-up)).

## Abuse, Trust and the Law

- **50 Percent Rule:** OFAC's rule that an entity owned 50 percent or more by blocked persons is itself blocked; control without that ownership doesn't block an entity automatically ([Sanctions, Exports & Bulletproof Hosts](/books/hosting-and-cloud/sanctions-and-bulletproof-hosts#what-the-rules-require)).
- **512(a) transitory communications:** the DMCA safe harbor for a conduit that carries transmissions initiated by others through an automatic technical process, with no notice-and-takedown duty because it has nothing to take down ([Conduits, Caches & Blocking Orders](/books/hosting-and-cloud/conduits-caches-and-blocking-orders#the-conduit-and-caching-safe-harbors)).
- **512(b) system caching:** the DMCA safe harbor for a cache that doesn't modify the material and follows the originating site's rules, removing cached copies once the origin has removed them or a court has ordered it ([Conduits, Caches & Blocking Orders](/books/hosting-and-cloud/conduits-caches-and-blocking-orders#the-conduit-and-caching-safe-harbors)).
- **512(j) injunctions:** the DMCA provision that lets a court enjoin an intermediary even inside a safe harbor, the route to blocking a conduit ([Conduits, Caches & Blocking Orders](/books/hosting-and-cloud/conduits-caches-and-blocking-orders#the-conduit-and-caching-safe-harbors)).
- **Acceptable use policy (AUP):** the contract term every abuse action rests on, listing illegal content, infringement, security violations, network abuse and abuse of the provider, with a remedy clause; kept as versioned data ([Abuse on Infrastructure](/books/hosting-and-cloud/abuse-on-infrastructure#the-acceptable-use-policy)).
- **Account linking:** connecting accounts through shared signals (payment fingerprint, device, phone, IP range, SSH key) so a fraud ring is acted on at once; each link is evidence with a strength, not a verdict ([Free-Tier Fraud & Stolen Keys](/books/hosting-and-cloud/free-tier-fraud-and-stolen-keys#free-tier-farming)).
- **AI Diffusion Rule:** the BIS export rule on AI chips and model weights that was rescinded before it took effect in May 2025 ([Sanctions, Exports & Bulletproof Hosts](/books/hosting-and-cloud/sanctions-and-bulletproof-hosts#ai-chips-and-hosted-capacity)).
- **Article 5n:** the provision of EU Council Regulation 833/2014 barring listed services (IT consultancy, then enterprise-management and design software, including cloud delivery and updates) to the Russian government and entities established in Russia ([Sanctions, Exports & Bulletproof Hosts](/books/hosting-and-cloud/sanctions-and-bulletproof-hosts#what-the-rules-require)).
- **Block BEARD Act:** a US Senate discussion draft of July 2025 that would let courts order blocking of foreign piracy sites by intermediaries ([Conduits, Caches & Blocking Orders](/books/hosting-and-cloud/conduits-caches-and-blocking-orders#blocking-bills-in-the-united-states)).
- **Blocking order:** a court or regulator order requiring an intermediary to stop resolving, proxying or serving named domains or IPs in a territory, modeled as a legal notice with its clock and status ([Conduits, Caches & Blocking Orders](/books/hosting-and-cloud/conduits-caches-and-blocking-orders#blocking-orders-in-europe)).
- **Bulletproof host:** a provider whose product is non-enforcement; when designated, such hosts tend to rebrand and move infrastructure to new companies in other countries ([Sanctions, Exports & Bulletproof Hosts](/books/hosting-and-cloud/sanctions-and-bulletproof-hosts#bulletproof-hosts)).
- **Containment (fraud):** limiting what a new or compromised account can do, through risk quotas on GPUs, mail and egress, the third of four responses to free-tier abuse ([Free-Tier Fraud & Stolen Keys](/books/hosting-and-cloud/free-tier-fraud-and-stolen-keys#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Continuous screening:** rescreening every account against each sanctions list update and on changes to account data, catching new designations within a day ([Sanctions, Exports & Bulletproof Hosts](/books/hosting-and-cloud/sanctions-and-bulletproof-hosts#the-alternatives)).
- **Core services (abuse tier):** authoritative DNS, public resolvers, registries, registrars and transit, where the possible actions (remove records, hold, refuse resolution, null-route) reach far beyond one customer and must be narrow ([Abuse on Infrastructure](/books/hosting-and-cloud/abuse-on-infrastructure#three-tiers-of-service)).
- **Cox v. Sony:** the 2026 US Supreme Court decision that a conduit receiving infringement notices and continuing to serve a customer isn't contributorily liable for that alone ([Conduits, Caches & Blocking Orders](/books/hosting-and-cloud/conduits-caches-and-blocking-orders#cox-v-sony-and-infrastructure)).
- **Cryptomining (abuse):** the dominant use of stolen and free compute, because it needs only cycles and pays immediately; containment has to be automatic because a human response is too slow ([Free-Tier Fraud & Stolen Keys](/books/hosting-and-cloud/free-tier-fraud-and-stolen-keys#cryptomining)).
- **Dynamic blocking order:** a blocking order whose target list grows as rights holders report new domains and IPs, each new target carrying its own compliance clock ([Conduits, Caches & Blocking Orders](/books/hosting-and-cloud/conduits-caches-and-blocking-orders#blocking-orders-in-europe)).
- **FADPA (Foreign Anti-Digital Piracy Act):** H.R. 791, introduced in January 2025 to allow site-blocking orders against intermediaries, including large DNS resolvers, with no action after referral ([Conduits, Caches & Blocking Orders](/books/hosting-and-cloud/conduits-caches-and-blocking-orders#blocking-bills-in-the-united-states)).
- **Free-tier farming:** opening many accounts to collect a free tier's allowances or credits; the quotas a new account gets are the real limit, so they belong to the risk system ([Free-Tier Fraud & Stolen Keys](/books/hosting-and-cloud/free-tier-fraud-and-stolen-keys#free-tier-farming)).
- **General License D-2:** OFAC's license authorizing services, software and hardware incident to communications for people in Iran, which lets consumer communications and their cloud services reach them ([Sanctions, Exports & Bulletproof Hosts](/books/hosting-and-cloud/sanctions-and-bulletproof-hosts#what-the-rules-require)).
- **Geoblock on the proxy:** refusing listed hostnames to requests from the ordering country, with a 451, the narrowest compliance with a blocking order ([Conduits, Caches & Blocking Orders](/books/hosting-and-cloud/conduits-caches-and-blocking-orders#the-alternatives)).
- **Hosted services (abuse tier):** shared hosting, VMs, object storage, static sites and managed databases, where the provider holds the content and can remove or quarantine it, suspend or terminate ([Abuse on Infrastructure](/books/hosting-and-cloud/abuse-on-infrastructure#three-tiers-of-service)).
- **IaaS customer identification rule:** the BIS rule proposed in January 2024 under Executive Order 13984 to make US IaaS providers verify foreign customers; listed as withdrawn in December 2025, with no final rule as of October 2026 ([Sanctions, Exports & Bulletproof Hosts](/books/hosting-and-cloud/sanctions-and-bulletproof-hosts#the-iaas-customer-identification-rule)).
- **LLMjacking:** using stolen cloud credentials to run hosted AI models at the victim's expense, named in Sysdig's 2024 report ([Free-Tier Fraud & Stolen Keys](/books/hosting-and-cloud/free-tier-fraud-and-stolen-keys#stolen-keys)).
- **Mere conduit:** the liability category for pure transmission (exchange points, access points, VPNs, DNS services and resolvers among the DSA's Recital 29 examples), which carries no duty to remove content ([Abuse on Infrastructure](/books/hosting-and-cloud/abuse-on-infrastructure#three-tiers-of-service)).
- **Nondisclosure order:** a court order under 18 U.S.C. 2705(b) forbidding a provider to tell its customer about a request, which the provider must decide whether to challenge ([Law Enforcement at Infrastructure Providers](/books/hosting-and-cloud/law-enforcement-at-providers#customers-who-are-companies)).
- **OFAC (Office of Foreign Assets Control):** the US Treasury office whose programs prohibit US persons from serving blocked persons on the SDN list and, under comprehensive embargoes, anyone in the embargoed region, unless licensed ([Sanctions, Exports & Bulletproof Hosts](/books/hosting-and-cloud/sanctions-and-bulletproof-hosts#what-the-rules-require)).
- **Own-initiative detection:** scanning hosted content for known-bad hashes and phishing kits and watching compute for mining and scanning patterns, finding abuse before anyone reports it ([Abuse on Infrastructure](/books/hosting-and-cloud/abuse-on-infrastructure#automation-and-scale)).
- **Pass-through services (abuse tier):** CDN, reverse proxy, WAF, DDoS protection and load balancing in front of a customer origin, which can show an interstitial, stop caching or stop proxying but can't remove the content ([Abuse on Infrastructure](/books/hosting-and-cloud/abuse-on-infrastructure#three-tiers-of-service)).
- **Piracy Shield:** Italy's AGCOM platform under its 2023 anti-piracy law, through which rights holders report domains and IPs that intermediaries must block within 30 minutes ([Conduits, Caches & Blocking Orders](/books/hosting-and-cloud/conduits-caches-and-blocking-orders#blocking-orders-in-europe)).
- **Preservation (infrastructure):** a snapshot of the named volumes and buckets into a restricted store under a legal hold, kept for the statutory period and released if no process follows, without changing the customer's resources ([Law Enforcement at Infrastructure Providers](/books/hosting-and-cloud/law-enforcement-at-providers#what-the-provider-can-see)).
- **Quarantine policy (stolen key):** a managed deny policy attached to an exposed credential that blocks launching compute, creating persistence and similar actions while legitimate reads keep working; modeled as a permission ceiling ([Free-Tier Fraud & Stolen Keys](/books/hosting-and-cloud/free-tier-fraud-and-stolen-keys#stolen-keys)).
- **Redirection to the customer:** asking the government to serve an enterprise customer directly rather than producing its data, a disposition only an infrastructure provider has, counted separately in transparency reports ([Law Enforcement at Infrastructure Providers](/books/hosting-and-cloud/law-enforcement-at-providers#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Related-account fan-out:** acting on every account linked to a confirmed fraud ring at once, with the link strength recorded ([Abuse on Infrastructure](/books/hosting-and-cloud/abuse-on-infrastructure#automation-and-scale)).
- **Resolving the identifier:** turning an IP, hostname or timestamp in a request into the account that held it at that instant, minding time zones, shared addresses, pass-through services, resellers and deleted resources ([Law Enforcement at Infrastructure Providers](/books/hosting-and-cloud/law-enforcement-at-providers#resolving-the-identifier)).
- **Risk quotas:** small limits for new and unverified accounts, especially on GPUs and mail, that cap the damage any one account can do ([Free-Tier Fraud & Stolen Keys](/books/hosting-and-cloud/free-tier-fraud-and-stolen-keys#the-alternatives)).
- **SDN list (Specially Designated Nationals):** OFAC's list of blocked persons that a provider screens owners and end customers against, naming the list version each screening used ([Sanctions, Exports & Bulletproof Hosts](/books/hosting-and-cloud/sanctions-and-bulletproof-hosts#what-the-rules-require)).
- **Secret scanning partnership:** a code host's program in which token issuers register their token formats and an endpoint, so leaked credentials found in public code are reported to the issuer automatically ([Free-Tier Fraud & Stolen Keys](/books/hosting-and-cloud/free-tier-fraud-and-stolen-keys#stolen-keys)).
- **Stored Communications Act ladder:** the US framework of subpoena, court order and warrant for successively more sensitive records, with preservation, emergency disclosure and nondisclosure orders around it ([Law Enforcement at Infrastructure Providers](/books/hosting-and-cloud/law-enforcement-at-providers)).
- **Structured intake:** reports from trusted reporters, blocklists and scanners arriving as structured feeds, so a match to a known phishing kit or malware hash can be actioned without a reviewer ([Abuse on Infrastructure](/books/hosting-and-cloud/abuse-on-infrastructure#automation-and-scale)).
- **Three tiers of service:** the split of infrastructure into hosted, pass-through and core services, each with different possible actions, recorded on the resource because one account can hold all three ([Abuse on Infrastructure](/books/hosting-and-cloud/abuse-on-infrastructure#three-tiers-of-service)).
- **Tiered policy:** acting on content only where the provider hosts it, acting on pass-through for law, safety and abuse of the provider, and resisting global blocks on core services ([Abuse on Infrastructure](/books/hosting-and-cloud/abuse-on-infrastructure#the-alternatives)).
- **Voluntary termination:** a provider ending service to a customer on its own judgment rather than under law or a report, rare and recorded with its reason as an exception to the ladder ([Abuse on Infrastructure](/books/hosting-and-cloud/abuse-on-infrastructure#voluntary-terminations)).

## Market Rules and Compliance

- **Attestation of Compliance (PCI):** the PCI DSS document a Qualified Security Assessor issues for a service provider's part in protecting cardholder data ([Shared Responsibility & Compliance Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/compliance-programs#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **`attestation_scopes`:** the rows recording which services in which regions each compliance program covers over which period, so a trust portal, a sales answer and a responsibility matrix all agree and gaps are a query ([Shared Responsibility & Compliance Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/compliance-programs#how-to-build-it)).
- **Bridge letter:** a provider's letter covering the gap between the end of an audit report's period and the next report, because customers' audit periods don't line up with the provider's ([Shared Responsibility & Compliance Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/compliance-programs#soc-2-and-iso-27001-for-a-provider)).
- **Bring your own license (BYOL):** the customer supplying its own software licenses with mobility rights to run on a provider, subject to licensor restrictions such as Listed Provider terms ([Licensing Disputes](/books/hosting-and-cloud/licensing-disputes#the-alternatives)).
- **CISPE settlement:** the July 2024 agreement in which the European cloud trade group withdrew its competition complaint over Microsoft's licensing in exchange for a product for European hosters, monitored by ECCO ([Licensing Disputes](/books/hosting-and-cloud/licensing-disputes#microsoft-and-the-listed-providers)).
- **CLOUD Act:** the US law letting authorities compel a US provider to produce data in its possession, custody or control wherever it is stored, the concern sovereign cloud structures answer ([Shared Responsibility & Compliance Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/compliance-programs#sovereign-cloud)).
- **CMA cloud market investigation:** the UK Competition and Markets Authority's investigation, referred by Ofcom, whose 2025 final decision found an adverse effect on competition from egress fees, software licensing and committed spend ([Competition Cases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/competition-cases#the-uk-market-investigation-to-sms)).
