- **Complementary user entity controls:** the controls a provider's audit report says its customers must operate for the provider's controls to achieve their objectives, the "in the cloud" half of shared responsibility written into the audit ([Shared Responsibility & Compliance Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/compliance-programs#soc-2-and-iso-27001-for-a-provider)).
- **Conduct regime designation:** a regulator designating a firm for standing, tailored obligations, such as UK strategic market status or an EU DMA gatekeeper ([Competition Cases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/competition-cases#the-alternatives)).
- **DMA gatekeeper designation:** the European Commission's Digital Markets Act investigations, opened in November 2025, into designating AWS and Azure as gatekeepers for cloud below the quantitative thresholds ([Competition Cases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/competition-cases#the-eu-dma-gatekeeper-designation)).
- **ECCO (European Cloud Competition Observatory):** the monitor that tracked Microsoft's delivery under the CISPE settlement with traffic-light ratings ([Licensing Disputes](/books/hosting-and-cloud/licensing-disputes#microsoft-and-the-listed-providers)).
- **Egress fee:** the charge for data leaving a provider's network, the main switching cost that regulators targeted and that providers price as metered, bundled per server or zero ([Egress & Switching](/books/hosting-and-cloud/egress-and-switching)).
- **EU Data Act switching rules:** Chapter VI of Regulation (EU) 2023/2854, in force from September 2025, setting a two-month maximum notice to switch, transitional and retrieval periods, the phase-out of switching charges and interoperability duties ([Egress & Switching](/books/hosting-and-cloud/egress-and-switching#the-eu-data-act)).
- **Exit waiver:** free data transfer out for a customer leaving a provider, granted on request and usually as credits after approval, offered by the three hyperscalers from 2024 ([Egress & Switching](/books/hosting-and-cloud/egress-and-switching#exit-waivers-2024)).
- **FedRAMP:** the US authorization program for federal agencies' use of cloud services, run under Rev5 with agency sponsors or the newer 20x process ([Shared Responsibility & Compliance Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/compliance-programs#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **FedRAMP 20x:** FedRAMP's newer authorization process, piloted in phases and offered in classes by impact level, which a provider starting today should plan on instead of Rev5 ([Shared Responsibility & Compliance Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/compliance-programs#fedramp-20x-and-govramp)).
- **GovRAMP:** the non-profit verification program for state and local governments, formerly StateRAMP, the state and local counterpart to FedRAMP ([Shared Responsibility & Compliance Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/compliance-programs#fedramp-20x-and-govramp)).
- **ISO/IEC 27001:** the certification of an information security management system against the standard, with 27017 and 27018 adding cloud and personal-data controls ([Shared Responsibility & Compliance Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/compliance-programs#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **License Mobility:** Microsoft's Software Assurance right to bring licenses to any provider, as distinct from licenses without mobility rights, which can't be deployed on Listed Providers ([Licensing Disputes](/books/hosting-and-cloud/licensing-disputes#microsoft-and-the-listed-providers)).
- **License-included SKU:** a provider-supplied software license sold as a pass-through SKU with its own price versions, so a licensor's price change is a new version with notice ([Licensing Disputes](/books/hosting-and-cloud/licensing-disputes#how-to-build-it)).
- **Listed Providers:** the clouds (Microsoft, Alibaba, Amazon and Google) on which Microsoft's outsourcing terms restrict deploying licenses that lack mobility rights; any other provider is an Authorized Outsourcer ([Licensing Disputes](/books/hosting-and-cloud/licensing-disputes#microsoft-and-the-listed-providers)).
- **Multicloud transfer at cost:** discounted or zero-charge transfer for traffic a customer designates between a provider and another cloud while using both, offered by Google and Azure in 2025 to meet the Data Act ([Egress & Switching](/books/hosting-and-cloud/egress-and-switching#multicloud-transfer-2025)).
- **PCI DSS Appendix A1:** the additional PCI DSS requirements for multi-tenant service providers, part of what a host's responsibility matrix covers ([Shared Responsibility & Compliance Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/compliance-programs#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Responsibility matrix:** for each requirement of a framework such as PCI DSS, whether the provider, the customer or both are responsible, per service; the deliverable customers actually use ([Shared Responsibility & Compliance Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/compliance-programs#pci-dss-for-hosts)).
- **Scope (attestation):** what a compliance program certifies: named services in named regions over a named period, not a company; a service outside scope inherits nothing ([Shared Responsibility & Compliance Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/compliance-programs#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Shared responsibility model:** the split in which the provider secures the cloud itself (hardware, software, networking, facilities) and the customer secures what it puts in the cloud; a contract boundary more than a security design ([Shared Responsibility & Compliance Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/compliance-programs#the-shared-responsibility-model)).
- **SOC 2:** an audit report on controls against the AICPA Trust Services Criteria (Security, Availability, Processing Integrity, Confidentiality, Privacy), over a period (Type 2) or at a point in time (Type 1) ([Shared Responsibility & Compliance Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/compliance-programs#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Sovereign cloud:** not an audit but a structure of legal entities, staff, locations and keys arranged so the provider's home government can't reach the data; a spectrum from data location to operational, key and legal control ([Shared Responsibility & Compliance Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/compliance-programs#sovereign-cloud)).
- **Strategic market status (SMS):** the UK digital markets regime's designation that lets the CMA impose conduct requirements on a firm, the route it chose for Microsoft's cloud licensing ([Competition Cases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/competition-cases#the-uk-market-investigation-to-sms)).
- **Subservice organization:** a provider a service relies on (a PaaS's hyperscaler) named in the audit report, whose controls are carved out or included ([Shared Responsibility & Compliance Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/compliance-programs#soc-2-and-iso-27001-for-a-provider)).
- **Switching charges:** charges for moving to another provider, which the EU Data Act allows only as reduced charges not exceeding the provider's costs until January 2027 ([Egress & Switching](/books/hosting-and-cloud/egress-and-switching#the-eu-data-act)).
- **Trust Services Criteria:** the AICPA criteria a SOC 2 report tests controls against, currently the 2017 criteria with revised points of focus ([Shared Responsibility & Compliance Programs](/books/hosting-and-cloud/compliance-programs#soc-2-and-iso-27001-for-a-provider)).
- **VCSP (VMware Cloud Service Provider program):** VMware's program for providers reselling VMware-based private cloud, which Broadcom phased out with its white-label model in the EEA from late 2025, replacing it with an invitation-only program ([Licensing Disputes](/books/hosting-and-cloud/licensing-disputes#broadcom-and-vmware)).
- **VMware perpetual license end:** Broadcom's December 2023 move ending perpetual VMware licenses and their support renewals in favor of subscriptions, which customers challenged in court ([Licensing Disputes](/books/hosting-and-cloud/licensing-disputes#broadcom-and-vmware)).
- **Voluntary commitments (competition):** steps firms design and offer instead of binding remedies, fast but not binding and dependent on review, as the CMA accepted in March 2026 ([Competition Cases](/books/hosting-and-cloud/competition-cases#the-alternatives)).

## Running the Provider

- **`account_id`, `resource_id`, `sku_id` and time ranges:** the four keys every provider join runs through: the paying tenant and its standing, the thing that ran, its price, and the periods (`exists_during`, `held_during`, `valid_during`) that tie records to instants ([Provider Architecture](/books/hosting-and-cloud/provider-architecture#the-parts-and-how-they-fit)).
- **Annual revenue retention:** the share of last year's revenue not lost to customers who left, the retention variant that isolates churn from expansion ([Operating Metrics](/books/hosting-and-cloud/operating-metrics#net-dollar-retention-and-its-variants)).
- **ARR (annual recurring revenue):** in this industry almost never a sum of contracts but a recent period's revenue annualized, so its definition is part of the number ([Operating Metrics](/books/hosting-and-cloud/operating-metrics#recurring-revenue-arr-and-run-rate)).
- **Back-end (scale-out) fabric:** the network that carries GPU-to-GPU traffic between servers, InfiniBand or RoCE, bundled into a cluster or placement construct rather than priced separately ([GPU & AI Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/gpu-and-ai-clouds#how-a-gpu-instance-works)).
- **Batch pricing (AI APIs):** discounted asynchronous processing on frontier model APIs, about half the standard price, which stacks with prompt caching ([Where AI Runs: Home, Colo, Cloud or Frontier](/books/hosting-and-cloud/where-ai-runs#frontier-apis)).
- **Blast radius:** the share of customers one failure can reach, bounded by design through regions, zones, cells and shuffle sharding rather than discovered in an outage ([Provider Architecture](/books/hosting-and-cloud/provider-architecture#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Break-even month:** the month in a year-over-year model when cumulative savings from owning recover the up-front purchase ([Total Cost of Ownership](/books/hosting-and-cloud/total-cost-of-ownership#year-over-year)).
- **Cached input:** prompt tokens served from a model provider's cache at a fraction of the input price, one of the main levers on frontier API cost ([Where AI Runs: Home, Colo, Cloud or Frontier](/books/hosting-and-cloud/where-ai-runs#frontier-apis)).
- **Capacity block (GPU):** a fixed number of GPU instances for fixed dates, bought ahead at a price set at purchase, the way hyperscalers ration scarce large GPUs ([GPU & AI Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/gpu-and-ai-clouds#buying-capacity)).
- **Capex versus cash view:** owned hardware seen as straight-line depreciation over a useful life for the comparison, and as cash leaving at purchase (or financing payments) for whether the company can afford it ([Total Cost of Ownership](/books/hosting-and-cloud/total-cost-of-ownership#buying-the-servers)).
- **Commissioned TCO study:** a vendor-funded analysis, such as a Forrester Total Economic Impact study, that picks the composite customer, baseline and period; marketing, not a model of a workload ([Total Cost of Ownership](/books/hosting-and-cloud/total-cost-of-ownership#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Component status:** the state of one product and region on a status page (Operational, Under Maintenance, Degraded Performance, Partial Outage, Major Outage), from which the top-level status is computed ([Outages, Post-Mortems & Status Pages](/books/hosting-and-cloud/outages-and-status-pages#status-pages)).
- **Constant work:** a reliability pattern in which a system does the same amount of work whatever happens, such as health checkers pushing every result every time, so load never spikes during failure ([Provider Architecture](/books/hosting-and-cloud/provider-architecture#provisioning-from-request-to-running-thing)).
- **Control-plane dependency:** a hidden dependency of a control plane on one building, another cloud, a database or the monitoring itself, the cause of several large outages ([Outages, Post-Mortems & Status Pages](/books/hosting-and-cloud/outages-and-status-pages#control-plane-dependencies)).
- **Cost per unit:** cost of revenue divided by what was delivered (per VM-hour, GB-month, GB delivered, message, GPU-hour, million tokens), the number pricing should be set from ([Operating Metrics](/books/hosting-and-cloud/operating-metrics#cost-per-unit)).
- **Downgrade to free:** an account moving from paid to a free plan, counted separately from churn because the account still exists ([Operating Metrics](/books/hosting-and-cloud/operating-metrics#customers-churn-and-renewal)).
- **Earning hours (GPU):** the hours a GPU spends under paid use, its real utilization measure and what decides whether owning it beats renting ([Operating Metrics](/books/hosting-and-cloud/operating-metrics#utilization)).
- **Front door:** the control-plane layer that authenticates, authorizes, rate-limits and checks quota on every API call before anything is accepted ([Provider Architecture](/books/hosting-and-cloud/provider-architecture#the-parts-and-how-they-fit)).
- **Front-end network (GPU):** the network that carries everything except GPU-to-GPU traffic in an AI cluster: storage, checkpoints, the customer's other services and the internet ([GPU & AI Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/gpu-and-ai-clouds#how-a-gpu-instance-works)).
- **Frontier API:** a model lab's hosted API, where the customer owns nothing and pays per token (input, cached input, cache writes, output) under the lab's terms ([Where AI Runs: Home, Colo, Cloud or Frontier](/books/hosting-and-cloud/where-ai-runs#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **FTE fraction:** a placement's recurring platform work in hours a month divided by productive hours, a stated and dated estimate rather than a fact ([Total Cost of Ownership](/books/hosting-and-cloud/total-cost-of-ownership#counting-people)).
- **Fully loaded cost:** what an employee costs beyond salary; the chapter's public placeholder divides the BLS median systems administrator wage by the 70% wage share of compensation, about $141,600 a year ([Total Cost of Ownership](/books/hosting-and-cloud/total-cost-of-ownership#counting-people)).
- **Global-config failure pattern:** the recurring outage shape: something that isn't code changes, propagates everywhere quickly by design, crashes a consumer that trusted it, and takes longer to recover from than to cause ([Outages, Post-Mortems & Status Pages](/books/hosting-and-cloud/outages-and-status-pages#the-global-config-failure-pattern)).
- **Gross margin with pass-through:** revenue less the cost of serving it over revenue, which pass-through fees lower without changing gross profit, so it is reported with and without them ([Operating Metrics](/books/hosting-and-cloud/operating-metrics#gross-margin-and-pass-through)).
- **Hosted open model:** an inference provider serving an open-weight model the customer chooses, billed per token, with precision and speed varying by host ([Where AI Runs: Home, Colo, Cloud or Frontier](/books/hosting-and-cloud/where-ai-runs#hosted-open-models)).
- **Hosted status page:** a SaaS status page product that models components, incidents and subscribers and runs on infrastructure independent of the provider's own ([Outages, Post-Mortems & Status Pages](/books/hosting-and-cloud/outages-and-status-pages#how-the-providers-do-it)).
- **Incident lifecycle (status page):** an incident's movement through Investigating, Identified, Monitoring and Resolved, with scheduled maintenance on its own lifecycle ([Outages, Post-Mortems & Status Pages](/books/hosting-and-cloud/outages-and-status-pages#status-pages)).
- **Kinds of ending:** the ways a product ends (free tier withdrawn, product shut down, version end of life, business sold, maintenance mode, location or endpoint moved), each owing customers something different ([Migrations, Shutdowns & End of Life](/books/hosting-and-cloud/migrations-and-end-of-life#the-kinds-of-ending)).
- **Logo churn:** customers active at the start of a period and not at its end, over customers at the start, meaningful only with the threshold that defines "active" written down ([Operating Metrics](/books/hosting-and-cloud/operating-metrics#customers-churn-and-renewal)).
- **Magic Pocket:** the storage system Dropbox built to move file content off Amazon S3 into its own infrastructure in colocation, the best-documented storage repatriation ([Repatriation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/repatriation#dropbox-builds-magic-pocket-to-2016)).
- **Maintenance mode:** a product kept running with fixes but no new features, which leaves customers to decide whether to stay ([Migrations, Shutdowns & End of Life](/books/hosting-and-cloud/migrations-and-end-of-life#the-kinds-of-ending)).
- **Metering pipeline:** the path from data-plane usage emission to rated usage, in which the data plane emits and never waits, every hop is idempotent and counts are reconciled against what is running ([Provider Architecture](/books/hosting-and-cloud/provider-architecture#the-metering-pipeline)).
- **Multi-year take-or-pay:** dedicated GPU capacity contracted for years, partly prepaid and paid whether used or not, the contract neoclouds finance their GPUs against ([GPU & AI Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/gpu-and-ai-clouds#buying-capacity)).
- **Neocloud:** a GPU-focused cloud provider that sells clusters on demand and under multi-year contracts, a utility financed like an infrastructure project with contracts as the collateral ([GPU & AI Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/gpu-and-ai-clouds#the-neocloud-business)).
- **Net dollar retention:** what last year's customers spend now, as a share of what they spent then; each provider computes it differently enough that the definition is part of the number ([Operating Metrics](/books/hosting-and-cloud/operating-metrics#net-dollar-retention-and-its-variants)).
- **NVLink domain:** the set of GPUs joined by NVLink and NVSwitch, the fastest network a model can use, inside one server or, in rack-scale systems such as GB200 NVL72, across a rack ([GPU & AI Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/gpu-and-ai-clouds#how-a-gpu-instance-works)).
- **Off-bill costs:** what no provider invoices: people and on-call, upgrade treadmills, observability and security tools, migration and exit, commitment risk, opportunity cost and depreciation ([Total Cost of Ownership](/books/hosting-and-cloud/total-cost-of-ownership#the-costs-not-on-the-bill)).
- **On-call floor:** the minimum rotation for 24-hour response, about eight engineers at one site or six per site across two, a headcount whatever the workload's size ([Total Cost of Ownership](/books/hosting-and-cloud/total-cost-of-ownership#staffing-by-platform)).
- **Operation (long-running):** the record an API returns for anything slower than a few seconds, which the caller polls while the provisioning workflow runs ([Provider Architecture](/books/hosting-and-cloud/provider-architecture#provisioning-from-request-to-running-thing)).
- **Opportunity cost (infrastructure):** the product work an engineer doesn't do while running infrastructure, the reason small teams pay a managed service's premium ([Total Cost of Ownership](/books/hosting-and-cloud/total-cost-of-ownership#the-costs-not-on-the-bill)).
- **Own the baseline, rent the burst:** running steady load on owned hardware busy and keeping peaks and unknowns elastic in the cloud, at the cost of two platforms and the transfer between them ([Repatriation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/repatriation#the-alternatives)).
- **Owned annual cost:** hardware over useful life plus colocation, network, support and spares, licenses, the change in staff and the cost of capital, compared with the rented side's effective rate and transfer costs ([Repatriation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/repatriation#a-cost-model-for-owning)).
- **`ownership_costs`:** effective-dated rows of off-bill cost per workload and scenario, on a monthly, FTE, one-time or capex basis, compared with the bill in one query ([Total Cost of Ownership](/books/hosting-and-cloud/total-cost-of-ownership#how-to-build-it)).
- **Paid extended support:** keeping old versions running for a fee before a forced upgrade, which turns a forced migration into a choice with a price ([Migrations, Shutdowns & End of Life](/books/hosting-and-cloud/migrations-and-end-of-life#the-alternatives)).
- **People delta:** the people a placement needs minus the people the company has regardless; zero when the existing team stays the same size, as 37signals reported ([Total Cost of Ownership](/books/hosting-and-cloud/total-cost-of-ownership#counting-people)).
- **Per-account status:** status a provider computes for one customer's own resources and regions, telling each customer what is theirs where a public page can't ([Outages, Post-Mortems & Status Pages](/books/hosting-and-cloud/outages-and-status-pages#status-pages)).
- **Per-output pricing:** serverless model APIs charging per image, second of video or megapixel rather than per GPU-second ([GPU & AI Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/gpu-and-ai-clouds#serverless-gpu-and-per-output-pricing)).
- **Physalia:** AWS's transactional key-value store for control-plane state, begun as the configuration master for EBS, highly available and strongly consistent by being available for only the keys each client needs ([Provider Architecture](/books/hosting-and-cloud/provider-architecture#cells-shards-and-blast-radius)).
- **Post-mortem:** a provider's public account of an incident, owed under published criteria, revised as understanding improves with both versions kept, and listing remediations as promises ([Outages, Post-Mortems & Status Pages](/books/hosting-and-cloud/outages-and-status-pages#post-mortems-as-a-product)).
- **Productive engineer-month:** the 140 hours a month the chapter assumes an engineer spends on work after meetings and leave, the divisor for FTE fractions ([Total Cost of Ownership](/books/hosting-and-cloud/total-cost-of-ownership#staffing-by-platform)).
- **Provisioning:** turning an accepted request into a running thing: authenticate, check standing and quota, accept idempotently, return an operation, place, run the workflow, converge and report ([Provider Architecture](/books/hosting-and-cloud/provider-architecture#provisioning-from-request-to-running-thing)).
- **P/S/C (shared responsibility by task):** marking each recurring task as absorbed by the provider, shared, or the customer's, per platform, the basis for counting people ([Total Cost of Ownership](/books/hosting-and-cloud/total-cost-of-ownership#staffing-by-platform)).
- **Reconciliation model:** a provisioning style, made familiar by Kubernetes, that declares desired state and lets controllers converge current state toward it, instead of running an explicit workflow ([Provider Architecture](/books/hosting-and-cloud/provider-architecture#provisioning-from-request-to-running-thing)).
- **Refresh (hardware):** buying the fleet again at the end of its useful life, year six of a five-year model, which is why a five-year saving isn't permanent ([Total Cost of Ownership](/books/hosting-and-cloud/total-cost-of-ownership#buying-the-servers)).
- **Renewal rate:** the share of customers or names renewing at term end, reported by hosting and domain businesses and best broken out by term number for prepaid products ([Operating Metrics](/books/hosting-and-cloud/operating-metrics#customers-churn-and-renewal)).
- **Repatriation:** moving a workload from rented cloud capacity back to hardware the company owns or leases, usually in colocation, worth it when load is steady, the team exists and the cloud's discounts are already taken ([Repatriation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/repatriation)).
- **Revenue churn:** revenue lost from customers who left or shrank, over starting revenue, the complement of revenue retention ([Operating Metrics](/books/hosting-and-cloud/operating-metrics#customers-churn-and-renewal)).
- **Router-level fallback:** a model router trying models or providers in order and falling back on any error, billing at the model that served ([Where AI Runs: Home, Colo, Cloud or Frontier](/books/hosting-and-cloud/where-ai-runs#routing-and-fallbacks)).
- **Serverless GPU:** GPU compute billed per second or per output that scales to zero, with cold starts and a premium per hour at high utilization ([GPU & AI Clouds](/books/hosting-and-cloud/gpu-and-ai-clouds#the-alternatives)).
- **Sold versus installed:** capacity allocated to customers over capacity installed, by zone and instance type, the utilization measure that drives capacity orders and spot pricing ([Operating Metrics](/books/hosting-and-cloud/operating-metrics#utilization)).
- **Steadiness:** typical load over peak load; the closer the median is to the peak, the busier an owned fleet runs and the better owning compares with renting ([Repatriation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/repatriation#a-cost-model-for-owning)).
- **Sticker price:** the provider's invoice alone, often the smallest term in a small workload's total cost ([Total Cost of Ownership](/books/hosting-and-cloud/total-cost-of-ownership#a-worked-tco)).
- **Sunset lifecycle:** the ordered end of a product: announce every date at once, close to new customers, stop changes, go read-only, shut down, then delete after a stated window ([Migrations, Shutdowns & End of Life](/books/hosting-and-cloud/migrations-and-end-of-life#the-lifecycle-of-a-sunset)).
- **Third-party components:** status page components that mirror a vendor's own status page, so customers see when the failure is in the underlay ([Outages, Post-Mortems & Status Pages](/books/hosting-and-cloud/outages-and-status-pages#status-pages)).
- **Total cost of ownership (TCO):** everything a workload costs to run for a period: the bill plus people, upgrades, off-bill tools, migration and exit, commitments and, for owned hardware, depreciated capital ([Total Cost of Ownership](/books/hosting-and-cloud/total-cost-of-ownership)).
- **Used versus sold:** what allocated capacity actually does; the gap between it and sold capacity is the provider's overcommit margin and the noisy neighbor's risk ([Operating Metrics](/books/hosting-and-cloud/operating-metrics#utilization)).
- **Useful life:** the period owned hardware is depreciated over; five years is the default for general compute, and Amazon moved its servers from five to six years in 2024 and a subset back to five in 2025 ([Total Cost of Ownership](/books/hosting-and-cloud/total-cost-of-ownership#buying-the-servers)).
- **Utilization of the owned fleet:** average used capacity over capacity owned, the ratio that decides whether owning beats renting ([Repatriation](/books/hosting-and-cloud/repatriation#a-cost-model-for-owning)).
- **Vendor risk:** outages caused by a privileged agent's content push, a provider's own tooling, a building or a dependency of a dependency, rather than by the provider's own code ([Outages, Post-Mortems & Status Pages](/books/hosting-and-cloud/outages-and-status-pages#vendor-risk)).
- **Workflow model:** a provisioning style that runs an explicit sequence of steps with compensation on failure, recording each step's result so a crashed workflow resumes rather than restarts ([Provider Architecture](/books/hosting-and-cloud/provider-architecture#provisioning-from-request-to-running-thing)).

## References

**Reference**

- [RFC 5730: Extensible Provisioning Protocol (EPP)](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc5730), the registry protocol behind the domain object, status and transfer terms
- [RFC 8555: Automatic Certificate Management Environment (ACME)](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8555), the issuance protocol behind the account, order, authorization and challenge terms
- [ICANN: 2013 Registrar Accreditation Agreement (as amended, 21 January 2024)](https://itp.cdn.icann.org/en/files/accredited-registrars/registrar-accreditation-agreement-21jan24-en.htm), the registrar duties behind the domain lifecycle and registration data terms
- [17 U.S.C. 512](https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/17/512), the DMCA safe harbors behind the conduit, caching and hosting terms
- [Digital Services Act, Recital 29 (springlex mirror)](https://www.springlex.eu/en/packages/dsa/dsa-regulation/recital-29/), the examples of mere conduit, caching and hosting infrastructure
- [FOCUS specification](https://focus.finops.org/focus-specification/), the cost export columns behind the FinOps terms
